Function field analogue of Shimura’s conjecture on period symbols Thanks: The second author is partially supported by NSTC grant 107-2628-M-007-002-MY4 and 111-2628-M-007-002. The fourth author is partially supported by NSTC grant 109-2115-M-007-017-MY5 and the National Center for Theoretical Sciences.
W. Dale Brownawell, Chieh-Yu Chang, Matthew A. Papanikolas, Fu-Tsun Wei

Abstract
. In this paper we introduce the notion of Shimura’s period symbols over function fields in positive characteristic and establish their fundamental properties. We further formulate and prove a function field analogue of Shimura’s conjecture on the algebraic independence of period symbols. Our results enable us to verify the algebraic independence of the coordinates of any nonzero period vector of an abelian t t -module with complex multiplication whose CM type is non-degenerate and defined over an algebraic function field. This is an extension of Yu’s work on Hilbert-Blumenthal t t -modules.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11J93; Secondary 11G09, 11G15

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Motivation

The aim of this paper is to formulate and prove a function field analogue of Shimura’s conjecture on the algebraic independence of period symbols. To state Shimura’s conjecture, we first review the theory of Shimura’s period symbols developed in
[
Sh98
]
.

Let
K/\mathbb{Q}
be a CM field of degree
2n
, and denote by
J_{K}
the set of all embeddings of
K
into
\mathbb{C}
and by
I_{K}
the free abelian group generated by
J_{K}
.
Let
\Phi\subset J_{K}
be a CM type of
K
, i.e.,
\Phi\lor\Phi\rho=J_{K}
, where
\rho
is complex conjugation. For each
\sigma\in\Phi
, Shimura constructed a constant
p_{K}(\sigma,\Phi)\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
via integration of a holomorphic differential form on an abelian variety defined over
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
with CM type
(K,\Phi)
. He showed that it is unique up to algebraic multiples, and so we view the special value
p_{K}(\sigma,\Phi)
as an element of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}/\bar{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
. For
\tau\in\Phi\rho
, Shimura defined

p_{K}(\tau,\Phi)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=p_{K}(\tau\rho,\Phi)^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}/\bar{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times},

and further showed that
p_{K}
can be extended to a bilinear map

p_{K}:I_{K}\times I_{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}/\bar{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}

that has the following properties:

(i)

p_{K}(\xi\rho,\eta)=p_{K}(\xi,\eta\rho)=p_{K}(\xi,\eta)^{-1}
for every
\xi,\eta\in I_{K}
;

(ii)

p_{K}(\Res_{L/K}(\xi),\eta)=p_{L}(\xi,\Inf_{L/K}(\eta))
for
\xi\in I_{L}
and
\eta\in I_{K}
, where
L
is a CM field containing
K
;

(iii)

p_{L}(\Inf_{L/K}(\xi),\eta)=p_{K}(\xi,\Res_{L/K}(\eta))
for
\xi\in I_{K}
and
\eta\in I_{L}
, where
L
is a CM field containing
K
;

(iv)

p_{K^{\prime}}(\gamma\xi,\gamma\eta)=p_{K}(\xi,\eta)
if
\gamma:K^{\prime}\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}K
is an isomorphism.

We note that property (i) shows that if
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is a CM type for
K
, then

E_{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\left(p_{K}(\iota,\operatorname{id})\mid\iota\in J_{K}\right)=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\left(p_{K}(\sigma_{1},\operatorname{id})),\ldots,p_{K}(\sigma_{n},\operatorname{id})\right),

and so

\trdeg_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\left(p_{K}(\iota,\operatorname{id})\mid\iota\in J_{K}\right)=\trdeg_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\left(p_{K}(\sigma_{1},\operatorname{id})),\ldots,p_{K}(\sigma_{n},\operatorname{id})\right)\leq n.

In
[
Sh80
, p. 319]
, Shimura proposed the following conjecture/question
asking whether we actually have equality here.

Conjecture 1.1.1
.

Let
K
be a CM field of degree
2n
. If
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is a CM type of
K
, then

p_{K}(\sigma_{1},\operatorname{id}),\ldots,p_{K}(\sigma_{n},\operatorname{id})

are algebraically independent over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
.

In other words, Shimura’s conjecture asserts that, no matter the CM type
\Phi
of
K
, the set

\left\{p_{K}(\sigma_{1},\operatorname{id}),\ldots,p_{K}(\sigma_{n},\operatorname{id})\mid\sigma_{i}\in\Phi\right\}

is a generating transcendence basis of
E_{K}
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. Thus property (i) above generates all
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebraic relations on the period symbols
\{p_{K}(\iota,\operatorname{id})\mid{\iota\in J_{K}}\}
. In
[
Yo03
, p. 270]
, Yoshida described the following conjecture, which also includes
\pi
, and derived it from Deligne’s conjecture
[
De82
]
, which is a special case of Grothendieck’s period conjecture (see
[
Ay14
]
and prologue of
[
HW22
]
).

Conjecture 1.1.2
.

Let
K
be a CM field which is Galois over
\mathbb{Q}
, and let
\Phi
be a CM type of
K
. Then we have

\trdeg_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\pi,\,p_{K}(\operatorname{id},\sigma)\mid\sigma\in\Phi\right)=1+[K:\mathbb{Q}]/2.

In what follows, we mention that Conjecture
1.1.2
implies the Lang-Rohrlich conjecture on special
\Gamma
-values. By
special
\Gamma
-values
we mean the values of Euler’s
\Gamma
-function at proper fractions. The Lang-Rohrlich conjecture
[
Lang90
]
asserts that all
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebraic relations among special
\Gamma
-values and
2\pi\sqrt{-1}
are explained by the standard functional equations of
\Gamma
-function. An equivalent formulation of the Lang-Rohrlich conjecture is that for any integer
n>2
, we have

\trdeg_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\pi,\Gamma(\frac{1}{n}),\Gamma(\frac{2}{n}),\ldots,\Gamma(\frac{n-1}{n})\right)=1+\frac{\phi(n)}{2}.

Note that if we let
K_{n}
be the
n
th cyclotomic extension over
\mathbb{Q}
, then
K_{n}/\mathbb{Q}
is an abelian CM field with
[K_{n}:\mathbb{Q}]=\phi(n)
. Using Deligne’s theorem
[
And82
, Thm. 4.7]
and Anderson’s work
[
And82
]
, one can show that

\trdeg_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\pi,p_{K_{n}}(\operatorname{id},\sigma)\mid\sigma\in\Phi\right)=\trdeg_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\pi,\Gamma(\frac{1}{n}),\Gamma(\frac{2}{n}),\ldots,\Gamma(\frac{n-1}{n})\right)

for any CM type
\Phi
of
K_{n}
. So Conjecture
1.1.2
implies the Lang-Rohrlich conjecture. The goal of this paper is to prove function field analogues of Conjecture
1.1.1
and Conjecture
1.1.2
.

1.2.
CM types and CM dual
t
-motives

In the theory of global function fields, Anderson
[
And86
]
introduced abelian
t
-modules (and their
t
-motives) to serve as the analogue of commutative algebraic groups in the classical theory. As with classical motives,
t
-motives possess linear algebra data and they capture Betti, de Rham, and
v
-adic Tate module realizations. The primary algebro-geometric objects here will be
uniformizable
abelian
t
-modules with complex multiplication that are analogous to CM abelian varieties, and our period symbols arise from the periods of these CM
t
-modules as in the case of Shimura’s period symbols. Uniformizability is automatic in the classical case for abelian varieties over
\mathbb{C}
.

Let
\mathbb{F}_{q}
be a finite field with
q
elements, and fix a variable
t
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
By a
CM field
(over the rational function field
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
), we mean a finite separable extension
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
containing a “totally real” subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
(i.e., the infinite place
\infty
of
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
splits completely in
\matheur{K}^{+}
) so that every place of
\matheur{K}^{+}
lying above
\infty
is non-split in
\matheur{K}
(cf. Definition
3.1.1
), namely it has a unique extension to
\matheur{K}
.
We call
\matheur{K}^{+}
the
maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
Unlike the classical case, the field extension
\matheur{K}/\matheur{K}^{+}
may not be Galois, and the degree of
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{K}^{+}
can be arbitrarily large.

As
\matheur{K}
(resp.
\matheur{K}^{+}
) corresponds to a smooth projective curve
X
(resp.
X^{+}
) over the constant field
\mathbb{F}{K}
of
\matheur{K}
(resp.
\mathbb{F}_{q}
),
we shall introduce our CM types of
\matheur{K}
in a geometric way as follows.
Fix another variable
\theta
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. Let
k\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(\theta)
,
k_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(\!(1/\theta)\!)
, and
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\widehat{\bar{k}}_{\infty}
, the completion of a chosen algebraic closure of
k_{\infty}
.
Let
\bar{k}
be the algebraic closure of
k
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Viewing
X
as an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-scheme,
the embedding
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}
corresponds to a finite
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism
\pi_{X/\mathbb{P}^{1}}:X\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Let
\mathbf{X}
(resp.
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
) be the base change of
X
(resp.
\pi_{X/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
) from
\mathbb{F}_{q}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
We set
J{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\theta)
(i.e. the preimage of the point
t=\theta
on the projective
t
-line
\mathbb{P}^{1}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
).
Let
\pi_{X/X^{+}}:X\rightarrow X^{+}
be the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism corresponding to the embedding
\matheur{K}^{+}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}
, and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}
be the base change of
\pi_{X/X^{+}}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.

Definition 1.2.1
.

(cf. Definition
3.3.2
)
Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
A
generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
is an effective divisor
\Xi
of
\mathbf{X}
of the following form

\Xi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi,

such that there exists a positive integer
{\wt}(\Xi)
satisfying

(1.2.2)

{\wt}(\Xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}m_{\xi},\quad\forall\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

We call
\Xi
a
CM type of
\matheur{K}
if
{\wt}(\Xi)=1
.

Remark 1.2.3
.

Let
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
be the set of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-linear embeddings from
\matheur{K}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
sending
t
to
\theta
. “Evaluating at points in
J{K}
” gives a one-to-one correspondence
(\xi\leftrightarrow\nu_{\xi})
between
J{K}
and
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
(see Section
3.3
). In particular, for each CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
, we may write
\Xi=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}
where
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
, and obtain

\Emb(\matheur{K}^{+},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})=\bigl\{\nu_{\xi_{1}}\big|_{\matheur{K}^{+}},\ldots,\nu_{\xi_{d}}\big|_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\bigr\}.

Under this identification between
J{K}
and
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
, our CM types are analogous to classical ones.

Just as in the classical case, our period symbols are closely connected with periods of abelian
t
-modules with CM, but we make this precise through the theory of
dual
t
-motives
, which were introduced in
[
ABP04
]
and are dual to the
t
-motives in
[
And86
]
. The category of uniformizable abelian
t
-modules is equivalent to the category of uniformizable dual
t
-motives (see
[
Mau21
]
). Thus the investigation of CM uniformizable abelian
t
-modules is transformed to the study of CM uniformizable dual
t
-motives (see Proposition
8.3.4
).
Recall that in the classical case, for any CM type
(K,\Phi)
, Shimura was able to contruct a CM abelian variety with CM type
(K,\Phi)
. Our initial step is to establish the analogue of Shimura’s construction following
[
Sinha97
,
BP02
,
ABP04
]
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field and
\Xi
be a (generalized) CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
We use indifferently the terminology that “
\Xi
is a (generalized) CM type of
\matheur{K}
” and that “
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
is a (generalized) CM type.”
In Section
4
, we construct a CM dual
t
-motive with given generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
. However, when the CM field in question is not geometric, there are complexities in proving that the constructed underlying space is a dual
t
-motive, and we work out all of the details in the appendix. For simplicity,
here we omit the precise definitions and refer readers to Definition
4.2.6
and (
4.2.1
) when
\mathbb{F}{K}=\mathbb{F}_{q}
(resp. Definition
B.2.4
and (
B.2.1
) when
\mathbb{F}{K}\supsetneq\mathbb{F}_{q}
).

Remark 1.2.4
.

Hartl and Singh
[
HS20
,
HS21
]
, introduced the notion of “CM” Anderson
t
-motives for semi-simple algebras
K
and define “CM-type” by using their Hodge-Pink weights.
Through the comparison in Proposition
7.4.1
,
our CM types seem analogous to Hartl and Singh’s,
although Theorem
4.3.7
and Proposition
C.1.3
show that ours involve the following three properties instead:

(1)

K
is a CM field;

(2)

\Xi
satisfies (
1.2.2
);

(3)

\matheur{M}
is pure.

These properties guarantee in our setting that the following essential result holds (see Proposition
4.2.8
and Theorem
4.3.7
):

Theorem 1.2.5
.

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field with
\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.

(1)

Any two CM dual
t
-motives over
\mathbb{K}
with the same (generalized) CM type must be isogeneous over
\mathbb{K}
.

(2)

Every CM dual
t
-motive over
\mathbb{K}
is uniformizable (and pure).

Remark 1.2.6
.

The existence of a CM dual
t
-motive defined over
\bar{k}
with any given generalized CM type follows
directly from the geometric construction in (
4.2.1
) and (
B.2.1
).
Theorem
1.2.5
(1) then ensures in particular that every CM dual
t
-motive is isomorphic to one defined over
\bar{k}
.

We note that uniformization of
t
-modules,
t
-motives or dual
t
-motives is a non-trivial problem, and the situation in this paper is entirely different from classical approaches. When the CM field is a Carlitz cyclotomic extension arising from the study of special geometric
\Gamma
-values, Sinha
[
Sinha97
]
first constructed a CM
t
-module associated with the CM field in question, and successfully proved the uniformization using shtuka functions. Sinha’s approaches were later on applied in
[
BP02
]
. In
[
ABP04
]
, the framework above was transferred to the study of dual
t
-motives, and the uniformization issue was proved by giving explicit rigid analytic trivializations using generalized Coleman’s functions playing the role of shtuka functions.

However, there is an assumption on the convergence of the infinite product of the twists of shtuka functions in Sinha’s approach for arbitrary CM fields, and hence difficulty and complexity arise once the study of uniformization is approached through shtuka functions. As the CM fields we consider are arbitrary, constructing a rigid analytic trivialization explicitly as in
[
ABP04
]
for all the dual
t
-motives in question seems impractical.

To circumvent the aforementioned difficulty, we first consider the case of CM fields with CM types and adopt the correspondence between certain lattices and Hilbert-Blumenthal
t
-modules established by Anderson
[
And86
]
. This enables us to show that the constructed CM dual
t
-motive is uniformizable, and the case of generalized CM types can be achieved by the fundamental property of CM dual
t
-motives in Proposition
4.2.7
and the isogeny property in Theorem
1.2.5
(1). We refer the reader to Section
C
for the details.

1.3.
Analogue of Shimura’s period symbol

To motivate the definition of our period symbols, we first recall that in Shimura’s theory his period symbols are defined via period integrals on the CM abelian variety in question. As mentioned in the previous subsection, the Betti and de Rham realizations of CM uniformizable abelian
t
-modules factor through their uniformizable dual
t
-motives.
So we can use the comparison isomorphism of the Betti and de Rham modules of the CM uniformizable dual
t
-motive in question together with Theorem
1.2.5
to define our period symbols.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a uniformizable dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
.
Let
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
be the Betti module of
\matheur{M}
(given in Remark
2.4.4
), and

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
be the de Rham module of
\matheur{M}
over
\bar{k}
(following
[
HaJu20
, Section 4.5]
, see Section
5.1
).
We introduce the
de Rham pairing
in (
5.1.1
):

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})

\times

H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})

\longrightarrow

\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
,

(\omega

,

\gamma)

\longmapsto

\displaystyle\int_{\gamma}\omega
,

which is non-degenerate (see Lemma
5.1.2
).
The result of a nonzero “cycle integration”
\int_{\gamma}\omega
is called a
period of
\matheur{M}
, which matches with the terminology in
[
P08
]
by Proposition
6.2.7
.

Suppose
\matheur{M}
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
For each
\xi\in J{K}
, one may associate a nonzero differential
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
, unique up to
\bar{k}^{\times}
-multiples, so that (see Proposition
5.2.1
)

\alpha^{*}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}=\nu_{\xi}(\alpha)\cdot\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}

for every
\alpha
in the integral closure
O{K}
of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
.
Here we view
\alpha
as an endomorphism of
\matheur{M}
, and
\alpha^{*}\omega
is the
pull-back of
\omega
by
\alpha
for
\omega\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
(see Section
5.1
).
From Theorem
1.2.5
(2), we derive that up to
\bar{k}^{\times}
-multiples, the quantity

\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}

depends only on the chosen
\xi
and the CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
.
Our
period symbol
is the unique element
\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi)
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
represented by
\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
for any nonzero
\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
(see Definition
5.2.2
), which is analogous to the “motivic version” in
[
De80
, p. 28]
of Shimura’s classical one.

Throughout this paper, we fix a fundamental period
\tilde{\pi}
of the Carlitz
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module (see
[
Goss96
,
T04
]
). Let
I{K}
be the free abelian group generated by
J{K}
, and
I{K}^{0}
be the subgroup generated by (generalized) CM types of
\matheur{K}
. We prove the following (see Theorem
5.3.2
).

Theorem 1.3.1
.

For each CM field
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, there exists a unique bi-additive pairing

\mathcal{P}{K}:I{K}\times I{K}^{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}

satisfying:

(1)

For each point
\xi\in J{K}
and each generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
,
\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi)
is the period symbol given above (see Definition
5.2.2
).

(2)

Let
\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field containing
\matheur{K}
. For
\Phi^{\prime}\in I_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}
and
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
,

\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}}\bigl({\Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{\prime}),\Phi^{0}\bigr)=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\bigl(\Phi^{\prime},{\Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{0})\bigr),

where
{\Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}
and
{\Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}
are the restriction and inflation, respectively (see Section
3.4
).

(3)

Let
\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field containing
\matheur{K}
and let
\matheur{K}^{\prime+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}^{\prime}
.
For
\Phi\in I{K}
and
\Phi^{\prime,0}\in I_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}^{0}
, we have

\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\bigl({\Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi),\Phi^{\prime,0}\bigr)=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}}\bigl(\Phi,{\Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{\prime,0})\bigr).

(4)

Let
\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field together with an isomorphism
\varrho:\matheur{K}^{\prime}\rightarrow\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, and let
\pi_{\varrho}:X\rightarrow X^{\prime}
be the isomorphism corresponding to
\varrho
.
Then

\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\bigl((\pi_{\varrho})_{*}(\Phi),(\pi_{\varrho})_{*}(\Phi^{0})\bigr)=\mathcal{P}{K}(\Phi,\Phi^{0}),\quad\forall(\Phi,\Phi^{0})\in I{K}\times I{K}^{0}.

(5)

Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
Given
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
and
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, one has that

\tilde{\pi}^{\text{\rm wt}(\Phi^{0})}\cdot\bar{k}^{\times}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Phi^{0})\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/{\bar{k}}^{\times}.

Remark 1.3.2
.

Following Shimura, we write
x\sim y
for any
x
,
y\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
with
x/y\in\bar{k}^{\times}
.
Given
\Phi\in I{K}
and
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, we use the notation
p{K}(\Phi,\Phi^{0})
for an arbitrary representative of the coset
\mathcal{P}{K}(\Phi,\Phi^{0})\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
.
We also call
p{K}(\Phi,\Phi^{0})
a period symbol if no confusion will arise.
The property in Theorem
1.3.1
(5) is then equivalent to:

\tilde{\pi}^{\text{\rm wt}(\Phi^{0})}\sim\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}p{K}(\xi,\Phi^{0}),\quad\forall\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

In Section
5.3
, we extend
\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}}
bi-additively to a pairing (still denoted by)

\mathcal{P}{K}:I{K}\times I{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times},

and keep using the notation
p{K}(\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2})
for an arbitrary representative of
\mathcal{P}{K}(\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2})
for every
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}\in I{K}
.
We now present our main theorem.

Theorem 1.3.3

(See Theorem
6.1.3
)
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\matheur{K}^{+}
its maximal totally real subfield.
For fixed
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
, all algebraic relations among the period symbols
p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})
varying over
\xi\in J{K}
are generated by the “Legendre relation” (see Lemma
6.1.1
):

\tilde{\pi}^{1/[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]}\sim\prod_{\xi\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi^{+})}p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0}),\quad\text{$\forall\,\xi_{0}\in J{K}$ and $\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}$.}

Equivalently, we have

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K})=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

Remark 1.3.4
.

As one purpose of this paper is to study the transcendence theory of “CM periods,” our extension of
\mathcal{P}{K}
in Section
5.3
is slightly different from the one in Shimura’s setting.
In particular,
the Legendre relation given in Theorem
1.3.3
shows that the Carlitz period
\tilde{\pi}
is always contained in
\bar{k}(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi_{1}\in J{K})
.

Remark 1.3.5
.

In the classical case, nonzero values of arithmetic (quasi) modular forms and their Serre derivatives at CM points can be interpreted using Shimura’s period symbols (see
[
Sh80
]
, and
[
C12b
]
for the case of arithmetic quasi-modular forms). In the function field setting, we have the same result for any arithmetic Drinfeld modular form
f
of rank two at a (separable) CM point
\alpha
. More precisely, if
f(\alpha)\neq 0
, it is shown in
[
C12a
, Thm.2.2.1]
that
f(\alpha)\sim\left(\omega_{\alpha}/\tilde{\pi}\right)^{\ell}
, where
\omega_{\alpha}
is a nonzero period of a CM Drinfeld
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module
E_{\rho}
of rank two defined over
\bar{k}
with CM field
\matheur{K}
, which is imaginary quadratic over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, and
\ell
is the weight of
f
. Note that
E_{\rho}
is a Hilbert-Blumenthal
t
-module of dimension one. Therefore, from the proof of Theorem
8.5.2
one sees that
\omega_{\alpha}\sim p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi,\Xi)
for the CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
, where
\xi
is any element of
J_{\matheur{K}}
and
\Xi=\xi
. It follows that

f(\alpha)\sim\frac{p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi,\Xi)^{\ell}}{{\tilde{\pi}}^{\ell}}.

In particular, when
f
has full-level, its
Serre derivative

\partial_{\ell}(f)
(introduced by
[
Ge88
, (8.5)]
)
becomes an arithmetic Drinfeld modular form of weight
\ell+2
.
Hence

\partial_{\ell}(f)(\alpha)\sim\frac{p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi,\Xi)^{\ell+2}}{{\tilde{\pi}}^{\ell+2}}\quad\textup{if $\partial_{\ell}(f)(\alpha)\neq 0$.}

Furthermore, for any
arithmetic quasi-modular form

\tilde{f}
of weight
\ell
(see
[
C12b
, p. 542–543]
), by
[
C12b
, Theorem 3.3 (c)]
we may express
\tilde{f}(\alpha)
in the following form:

\tilde{f}(\alpha)=\sum_{0\leq a\leq\ell/2}\beta_{a}\cdot\frac{p{K}(\xi,\Xi)^{\ell-2a}}{\tilde{\pi}^{\ell-a}},\quad\text{ where }\beta_{a}\in\bar{k}\text{ for $0\leq a\leq\ell/2$}.

Theorem
1.3.3
shows that if
f(\alpha)
(resp.
\partial_{\ell}(f)(\alpha)
,
\tilde{f}(\alpha)
) is nonzero, then it must be transcendental over
\bar{k}
, which agrees with the results in
[
C12a
, Theorem 1.2.1]
and
[
C12b
, Theorem 1.2]
.
Moreover, we would naturally expect to determine the algebraic independence of the CM values in question as well from Theorem
1.3.3
.

As an application, Theorem
1.3.3
leads to the following result that matches the spirit of Conjectures
1.1.1
and
1.1.2
.

Corollary 1.3.6
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\matheur{K}^{+}
its maximal totally real subfield.

(1)

Suppose that
\matheur{K}\neq\matheur{K}^{+}
, and let

\Xi=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{r}
be a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Given
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
, the period symbols

p{K}(\xi_{1},\xi_{0}),\ldots,p{K}(\xi_{r},\xi_{0})

are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
.

(2)

Suppose that
\matheur{K}
is Galois over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
Then for each
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
we have

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}(\tilde{\pi},p{K}(\xi_{0},\xi)\mid\xi\in J{K})=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

1.4.
Strategy of proof of the main theorem

Before describing our strategy and ideas about proofs of the main results above, we first say a few words about the classical case and illustrate the essential differences between it and our situation. As mentioned in Sec.
1.1
, the period conjecture of Deligne/Grothendieck implies Conjecture
1.1.2
, and in this case it suffices to compute the dimension of the Mumford-Tate group in question. As pointed out by Yoshida, from the description of the Mumford-Tate group
[
Yo03
, p. 270, (4)]
in that case there is no difficulty to see that the dimension in question is given by the rank of the subgroup generated by the reflex of the given CM type.

In the function field setting, it is shown in
[
P08
]
that there is an affine algebraic group
\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
associated to a uniformizable dual
t
-motive from Tannakian duality, and one views
\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
as playing a role analogous to that of the Mumford-Tate group in the classical theory. It is further established in
[
P08
]
that the dimension of
\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
is equal to the transcendence degree of the field generated by the periods of
\matheur{M}
over
\bar{k}
. However, it can be difficult to compute the dimension of
\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
in general, as the defining equations of
\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
arising from the rigid analytic trivialization of
\matheur{M}
can be complicated to determine. Unlike the classical case, in our case of CM dual
t
-motives it is not obvious and direct to compute
\dim\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
in terms of the given generalized CM type. In fact, we appeal to Hodge-Pink’s theory to generate a subtorus of the
\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
in question. A detailed outline is given as follows.

The overall strategy of proving Theorem
1.3.3
is to give upper and lower bounds for the transcendence degree in question. We first observe that
the Legendre relations among period symbols provides the inequality

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K})\leq 1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

We derive the opposite inequality through the following steps:

(1)

Take a generalized CM type
\Xi_{0}
of
\matheur{K}
so that
\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)
is algebraic over
\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)
(see Lemma
6.2.4
).

(2)

Let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{0})
over
\bar{k}
.
Apply the function field analogue of Grothendieck’s period conjecture (from
[
P08
]
, see Theorem
2.5.3
) to obtain

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)=\dim\Gamma{M},

where
\Gamma{M}
is the
t
-motivic Galois group of
\matheur{M}
(see Proposition
6.2.7
and Theorem
2.5.3
).

(3)

Use the
Hodge-Pink filtration
of
\matheur{M}
(see Definition
7.2.2
) to construct a cocharacter
\chi{M}
of
\Gamma{M}
over
k^{\rm sep}
(see (
7.3.2
)).
As
\Gamma{M}
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
,
the image of all Galois conjugates of
\chi{M}
produces a subtorus
T{M}
inside
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
, the base change of
\Gamma{M}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.

(4)

From the connection between the “Hodge-Pink weights” of
\matheur{M}
and the given CM type
\Xi_{0}
in (
7.4.2
), we show that
\dim T{M}
is equal to the rank of the “Galois submodule”
I_{\Xi_{0}}^{0}
generated by
\Xi_{0}
in
I{K}^{0}
(defined in Remark
7.4.4
(2)).

(5)

Finally, from the
non-degeneracy
of the chosen
\Xi_{0}
proved in Lemma
6.2.4
, i.e.,

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi_{0}}^{0}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I{K}^{0},

we obtain that

\displaystyle\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} \dim\Gamma{M}&\geq\dim T{M}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi_{0}}^{0}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I{K}^{0}\\
&=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)],\end{aligned}

as desired.

Remark 1.4.1
.

(1) For a CM dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
, the field generated by the periods of
\matheur{M}
is (see (
6.2.8
))

\bar{k}\Bigl(\int_{\gamma}\omega\Bigm|\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),\ \omega\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\Bigr)=\bar{k}(p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr).

When
\Xi
is non-degenerate, we then have that

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}\Bigl(\int_{\gamma}\omega\Bigm|\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),\ \omega\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\Bigr)=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

(2) As
\matheur{M}
is a CM dual
t
-motive, the
t
-motivic Galois group
\Gamma{M}
is commutative (see the discussion after Proposition
7.4.1
).
If
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
is generated by the images of all “Hodge-Pink cocharacters” of
\matheur{M}
(cf.
[
Pink97
, Theorem 7.11]
), i.e.
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}=T{M}
,
then we actually obtain

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)=\dim\Gamma{M}=\dim T{M}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi}^{0},

which says that the transcendence degree of the field generated by all period symbols
p{K}(\xi,\Xi)
for
\xi\in J{K}
is determined by finding the rank of the corresponding Galois submodule
I_{\Xi}^{0}
in
I{K}^{0}
.

1.5.
Applications

Let
E_{\rho}
be an abelian
t
-module over
\bar{k}
(recalled in Definition
2.1.1
).
Put
\mathscr{M}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(E_{\rho},\mathbb{G}_{a})
(introduced by Anderson
[
And86
]
, see Definition
2.1.1
(2)).
Let
\matheur{M}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho),\bar{k}[t]dt)
(constructed by Hartl and Juschka
[
HaJu20
]
, see Section
2.3
),
where
\tau
is identified with the
q
-power Frobenius endomorphism on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
.
We know that
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is a dual
t
-motive (and
E_{\rho}
is always “
A
-finite,” see Theorem
2.3.2
), called the
Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motive associated with
E_{\rho}
.
It is worth clarifying that the traditional dual
t
-motive associated with a given
t
-module
E_{\rho}
is usually regarded as
\matheur{N}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}_{a},E_{\rho})
(also introduced by Anderson, see Definition
2.1.1
(2)), and there is an isomorphism between
\matheur{M}(\rho)
and
\matheur{N}(\rho)
by
[
HaJu20
, Theorem 2.5.13]
(see Theorem
2.3.2
).
For our purposes, we shall utilize
\matheur{M}(\rho)
instead of
\matheur{N}(\rho)
throughout this paper.
Suppose
E_{\rho}
is uniformizable (for which so is
\matheur{M}(\rho)
, see Theorem
2.4.3
).
Let
\Lambda_{\rho}
be the period lattice of
E_{\rho}
and let
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})
be the de Rham module of
E_{\rho}
(see (
8.3.1
), following
[
BP02
, §3.1]
and
[
NP21
, Proposition 4.1.3]
).
Let
[\cdot,\cdot]:H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})\times\Lambda_{\rho}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
be the de Rham pairing introduced in
[
NP21
, (4.3.3) and (4.3.4)]
(see (
8.3.3
)).
In order to apply our results to abelian
t
-modules, we first naturally identify
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})
(resp.
\Lambda_{\rho}
) with
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}(\rho),\bar{k})
(resp.
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))
), and derive the following comparison between the associated de Rham pairings (cf. Proposition
8.3.4
):

\textstyle{[\ ,\ ]:}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{\Lambda_{\rho}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\int:}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}(\rho),\bar{k})}
\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
\textstyle{-z}

We say that
E_{\rho}
is a
CM abelian
t
-module with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
if the corresponding dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}(\rho)
has generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
In this case, the above comparison of de Rham pairings leads us to the following (cf. Theorem
8.4.1
):

Theorem 1.5.1
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
Then

\displaystyle 1+\frac{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]

\displaystyle\geq\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\bigl([\delta,\lambda]\bigm|\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k}),\ \lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi}^{0}.

In particular, if the generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
is non-degenerate, then

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\bigl([\delta,\lambda]\bigm|\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k}),\ \lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\bigr)=1+\frac{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

Furthermore, suppose
\Xi
is a CM type (i.e.
{\rm wt}(\Xi)=1
) for
\matheur{K}
.
This is equivalent to saying that
E_{\rho}
is a “Hilbert-Blumenthal
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
-module” with
\operatorname{rank}_{A}(\Lambda_{\rho})=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
, and the structure homomorphism
\rho
can be extended to a homomorphism (still denoted by
\rho
)
O{K}\rightarrow\End_{\text{\rm ab.\ $t$-mod}}(E_{\rho})
(cf. Section
8.5
).
Recall in
[
Yu89
]
that the coordinates of any nonzero period vector of a Hilbert-Blumenthal
t
-module
E_{\rho}
over
\bar{k}
must be transcendental over
k
.
Here we extend Yu’s result to the case when
E_{\rho}
is CM with a non-degenerate CM type (see Theorem
8.5.2
):

Theorem 1.5.2
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module of dimension
d
with non-degenerate CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
Fix an identification
\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\bar{k}^{d}
where
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
and extend to
\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
.
Then every nonzero period vector
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subseteq\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
has coordinate vector
(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
with
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d}
which are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
.

1.5.3.
Lang-Rohrlich conjecture over function fields.

As special values of various transcendental functions appear as CM periods, and hence period symbols, in certain circumstances we may apply our theorems to study transcendence problems and algebraic relations among special values.
For instance, let
A_{+}
be the set of monic polynomials in
A
. Given
f\in A_{+}
with
\deg f>0
,
suppose the CM field
\matheur{K}=\matheur{K}_{f}
, the
f
-th Carlitz cyclotomic function field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
Using the “generalized soliton dual
t
-motives” constructed in
[
ABP04
]
, we are able to show that
\bar{k}\bigl(p_{\matheur{K}_{f}}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}_{f}}\bigr)

is a finite extension over

\bar{k}\bigl(\tilde{\pi},\Gamma(x)\mid x\in\frac{1}{f}A\setminus(\{0\}\cup-A_{+})\bigr),

where
\Gamma(x)
is the
geometric gamma function
defined by Thakur
[
T91
]
:

\Gamma(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=x^{-1}\prod_{a\in A_{+}}(1+\frac{x}{a})^{-1}.

Therefore Theorem
1.3.3
implies

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\bigl(\tilde{\pi},\Gamma(x)\mid x\in\frac{1}{f}A\setminus(\{0\}\cup-A_{+})\bigr)\\
=1+\frac{[\matheur{K}_{f}:\matheur{K}_{f}^{+}]-1}{[\matheur{K}_{f}:\matheur{K}_{f}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}_{f}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]\ =\ 1+\frac{q-2}{q-1}\cdot\#(A/f)^{\times},

which coincides with
[
ABP04
, Corollary 1.2.]
. In other words, our main theorem provides an alternative approach to the Lang-Rohrlich conjecture for the geometric Gamma values.

1.5.4.
Remark.

We note that classical CM abelian varieties correspond in our setting to those Hilbert-Blumenthal
t
-modules arising from CM dual
t
-motives with CM type (
\matheur{K},\Xi
), namely a generalized CM type with
\wt(\Xi)=1
. It seems that there are no appropriate objects in the classical theory that correspond to the CM
t
-modules associated to CM dual
t
-motives of generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
with
\wt(\Xi)>1
, and so we could expect that applying our results above to CM
t
-modules of generalized CM types gives rise to new kinds of results.

For instance, when the given CM field
\matheur{K}
is contained in a constant field extension of a Carlitz cyclotomic function field, it is shown in current work
[
Wei22
]
that any quasi-period of the
t
-module associated to the CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
is related to
special two-variable gamma values
(introduced by Goss in
[
Go88
]
, see also
[
T91
]
), which previously have not been investigated from a transcendence point of view in this generality, and whose algebraic relations are determined based on the results of this paper. Moreover, in
[
Wei22
]
the recipe/conjecture on the
Chowla-Selberg phenomenon
proposed by Thakur
[
T91
]
is completely proved, and the intrinsic relations between period symbols and two-variable gamma values are found by developing an analogue of Anderson’s
period distribution
.

1.6.
Contents

This paper is organized as follows.
We first set up basic notation in the beginning of Section
2
, and review the equivalence between abelian
t
-modules and dual
t
-motives in Section
2.1
–
2.3
.
After introducing the Betti module of a dual
t
-motive in Section
2.4
, we review the theory of the function field analogue of Grothendieck’s period conjecture established by the third author in
[
P08
]
.

In Section
3
, we introduce the CM function fields and the CM types (when the CM field is geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
), including the inflation and restriction maps in Section
3.4
and the “reduction of CM types at infinity” in Section
3.5
.

In Section
4
, we utilize the “shtuka” functions associated to CM types (given in Section
4.1
) to construct CM dual
t
-motives in a geometric way (when the CM field is geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
) in Section
4.2
(cf.
[
Sinha97
,
BP02
,
ABP04
]
).
We also conclude at the end of Section
4.2
that two CM dual
t
-motives with the same (generalized) CM type must be isogeneous.
The uniformizablility of CM dual
t
-motives is proved
in Section
4.3
.

After introducing the de Rham pairing of a dual
t
-motive and showing the needed properties in Section
5.1
, we introduce an analogue of Shimura’s period symbol in Section
5.2
.
The fundamental properties of the period symbols in Theorem
1.3.1
are proved in Section
5.3
.

We state our main theorem and prove Corollary
1.3.6
in Section
6.1
.
Some preparation for proving the main theorem is given in Section
6.2
.

In Section
7
, we recall the needed results from the Hodge-Pink theory of the category introduced by Hartl and Juschka
[
HaJu20
]
.
After indicating the isomorphism between the
t
-motivic Galois groups coming from the category of pre-
t
-motives and the Hartl-Juschka category in Section
7.1
, we recall the Hodge-Pink filtration and the Hodge-Pink cocharacter in Section
7.2
and Section
7.3
, respectively.
In Section
7.4
, we connect the weight of the Hodge-Pink cocharacter of a given CM dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
with its associated CM type, which leads to a lower bound of the dimension of the
t
-motivic Galois group in question.
Finally, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.3.3
at the end of Section
7.4
.

Section
8
contains applications of this theorem to the periods of CM abelian
t
-modules.
We first make natural comparisons on the Lie spaces, the Betti modules, the de Rham modules, and the de Rham pairings between the given abelian
t
-modules
and the associated dual
t
-motives in Section
8.1
–
8.3
. As further applications, we derive Theorem
1.5.1
and Theorem
1.5.2
in Section
8.4
and Section
8.5
, respectively.

Finally, we work out the generalization of the definition of CM types and CM dual
t
-motives for those CM fields which are not geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
in Appendix
A
and
B
. Using the characterization in Proposition
C.1.3
of those dual
t
-motives which are CM, we complete the proof of the uniformizability of CM dual
t
-motives at the end of Section
C
.

2.
Preliminaries

Let
A\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}[\theta]
be the polynomial ring in the variable
\theta
over a finite field of
q
elements denoted by
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and let
k\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(\theta)
be the fraction field of
A
. We fix a normalized absolute value
|\cdot|_{\infty}
on
k
associated to the place at infinity of
k
, and we let
k_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}={\mathbb{F}_{q}(\!(1/\theta)\!)}
be the completion of
k
with respect to
|\cdot|_{\infty}
. Let
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
be the completion of a fixed algebraic closure of
k_{\infty}
, completed with respect to the absolute value that canonically extends the one on
k_{\infty}
. Let
\bar{k}
be a fixed algebraic closure of
k
embedded inside
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.

2.1.
Abelian
t
-modules

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field with
k\subset\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, and let
t
be a new variable independent from
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
. We recall the notion of an abelian
t
-module introduced by Anderson
[
And86
]
, which plays the analogous role of a commutative algebraic group in the classical setting.

Definition 2.1.1
.

Let
{\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{/\mathbb{K}}
be the additive group over
\mathbb{K}
.

(1)

A
t
-module of dimension

d>0
over
\mathbb{K}
is a pair
(E,\rho)
with the following properties.

•

E
is a
d
-dimensional additive group
{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{K}}
defined over
\mathbb{K}
,

•

\rho:\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}({\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{K}})
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-linear ring homomorphism so that
\partial\rho_{t}-\theta I_{d}
acts nilpotently on
\Lie({\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{K}})
, where
\partial\rho_{t}
denotes the differential of the homomorphism
\rho_{t}:{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{K}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{K}}
.

We denote by
E_{\rho}
the underlying space of
{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{K}}
equipped with the
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module structure via
\rho
.

(2)

E_{\rho}
is called an
abelian
(resp.
A
-finite
)
t
-module
if there exists a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{K}
-vector subspace
V
of
\mathscr{M}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(E_{\rho},\mathbb{G}_{a})
(resp.
\matheur{N}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}_{a},E_{\rho})
) of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-linear morphisms of algebraic groups over
\mathbb{K}
so that

\mathscr{M}(\rho)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}V\circ\rho_{t^{j}}\quad(\text{resp.}\quad\matheur{N}(\rho)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\rho_{t^{j}}\circ V).

Let
\mathbb{K}[t,\tau]
(resp.
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
) be the non-commutative polynomial ring over
\mathbb{K}
subject to the relations,

tc=ct,\ t\tau=\tau t,\ \tau c=c^{q}\tau\quad(\textup{resp.}\ t\sigma=\sigma t,\ \sigma c=c^{1/q}\sigma),\quad\forall\,c\in\mathbb{K}.

Identifying
\tau
with the Frobenius
q
-th power endomorphism
(x\mapsto x^{q})\in\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}_{a/\mathbb{K}})
, we note that
\mathscr{M}(\rho)
(resp.
\matheur{N}(\rho)
)
has a left
\mathbb{K}[t,\tau]
-module (resp.
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module) structure given as follows: for
c\in\mathbb{K}
,
a\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
,
m\in\mathscr{M}(\rho)
(resp.
\matheur{n}\in\matheur{N}(\rho)
),

(1)

c\cdot m\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=cm
(resp.
c\cdot\matheur{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{n}c
).

(2)

\tau\cdot m\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\tau\circ m
(resp.
\sigma\cdot\matheur{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{n}\circ\tau
).

(3)

a\cdot m\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=m\circ\rho_{a}
(resp.
a\cdot\matheur{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\rho_{a}\circ\matheur{n}
).

We note that
E_{\rho}
being an abelian (resp.
A
-finite)
t
-module is equivalent to
\mathscr{M}(\rho)
(resp.
\matheur{N}(\rho)
) being finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}[t]
.

Morphisms of abelian (resp.
A
-finite)
t
-modules are defined as morphisms of the underlying algebraic groups that are also left
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module homomorphisms. A surjective morphism of abelian (resp.
A
-finite)
t
-modules is called an
isogeny
if it has finite kernel.

The basic examples of abelian and
A
-finite
t
-modules are Drinfeld
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-modules over
\mathbb{K}
. They are defined as
E_{\rho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=({\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{/\mathbb{K}},\rho)
, where
\rho_{t}
is a morphism of
{\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{/\mathbb{K}}
of the form

\rho_{t}:X\mapsto\theta X+a_{1}X^{q}+\cdots+a_{r}X^{q^{r}},

where
r
is a positive integer and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{r}\in\mathbb{K}
with
a_{r}\neq 0
. Here
r
is called the
rank
of the Drinfeld module
E_{\rho}
.

Given an abelian
t
-module
E_{\rho}
over
\mathbb{K}
, Anderson
[
And86
]
showed that there is an associated exponential function

\exp_{\rho}:\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\rightarrow E_{\rho}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty}),

given by the unique
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-linear power series satisfying the relations,

•

\exp_{\rho}(\partial\rho_{t}(\mathbf{z}))=\rho_{t}(\exp_{\rho}(\mathbf{z}))
,

•

\exp_{\rho}(\mathbf{z})\equiv\mathbf{z}\ \textup{(mod deg $q$)}
.

Definition 2.1.2
.

An abelian
t
-module
E_{\rho}
is called
uniformizable
if its exponential map
\exp_{\rho}
is surjective. In this case,
\Lambda_{\rho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Ker\exp_{\rho}
is called the
period lattice
of
E_{\rho}
, and
E_{\rho}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\dim E_{\rho}}/\Lambda_{\rho}
as an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module.

2.2.
Anderson dual
t
-motives

For an integer
n
, we define
n
-fold twisting
on the Laurent series field
{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t)\!)}
as

\biggl(f\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum a_{i}t^{i}\ \mapsto\ f^{(n)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum a_{i}^{q^{n}}t^{i}\biggr):{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t)\!)}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t)\!)}.

We note that
n
-fold twisting is an
{\mathbb{F}_{q^{n}}(\!(t)\!)}
-linear automorphism of the field
{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t)\!)}
. We may regard
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
as the non-commutative polynomial ring obtained by adjoining the variable
\sigma
to
\mathbb{K}[t]
and multiplying by the rule

\sigma f=f^{(-1)}\sigma\textnormal{ for }f\in\mathbb{K}[t].

Definition 2.2.1
.

An (Anderson)
dual
t
-motive
over
\mathbb{K}
is a left
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module
\matheur{M}
so that

•

\matheur{M}
is a free left
\mathbb{K}[t]
-module of finite rank,

•

\matheur{M}
is a free left
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
-module of finite rank,

•

(t-\theta)^{N}\matheur{M}/\sigma\matheur{M}=(0)
for a sufficiently large integer
N
.

Morphisms
of dual
t
-motives over
\mathbb{K}
are defined to be left
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module homomorphisms.

Example 2.2.2
.

The basic example of a dual
t
-motive is the Carlitz motive
\matheur{C}
over
\mathbb{K}
. The underlying space of
\matheur{C}
is
\mathbb{K}[t]
, on which the
\sigma
-action is given by

\sigma f\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(t-\theta)f^{(-1)},\quad\textup{for $f\in\matheur{C}$.}

Let
E_{\rho}=(\mathbb{G}_{a/\mathbb{K}}^{d},\rho)
be an
A
-finite
t
-module over
\mathbb{K}
. Then
\matheur{N}(\rho)
must be free of finite rank over
\mathbb{K}[t]
. In other words,
\matheur{N}(\rho)
is a dual
t
-motive. Moreover, given a morphism
f:E_{\rho_{1}}\rightarrow E_{\rho_{2}}
between
A
-finite
t
-modules, there is an induced
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module homomorphism

\Theta_{f}:\matheur{N}(\rho_{1})\rightarrow\matheur{N}(\rho_{2}),\quad\Theta_{f}(\matheur{n}_{1})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=f\circ\matheur{n}_{1}.

Theorem 2.2.3

(Anderson, see
[
HaJu20
, Prop. 2.5.8 and Thm. 2.5.11]
)
.

The functor

(E_{\rho}\mapsto\matheur{N}(\rho),\quad f\mapsto\Theta_{f})

from the category of
A
-finite
t
-modules to the category of dual
t
-motives is an equivalence. Moreover,
{\matheur{N}}(\rho)/(\sigma-1){\matheur{N}}(\rho)
and
{\matheur{N}(\rho)}/\sigma{\matheur{N}(\rho)}
are naturally identified with
E_{\rho}(\mathbb{K})
and
\Lie({E_{\rho}})(\mathbb{K})
respectively.

Remark 2.2.4
.

(1) An inverse functor of this equivalence from the category of dual
t
-motives to the category of
A
-finite
t
-modules is given as follows.
Let
\matheur{M}
be a dual
t
-motive of rank
d
over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
. We note that the action of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
on
\matheur{M}
commutes with the operator
\sigma
and, so the quotient

\matheur{M}/(\sigma-1)\matheur{M}

is a left
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module.
As
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\sigma]}\matheur{M}=d
, we can view
\matheur{M}/(\sigma-1)\matheur{M}
as the
\mathbb{K}
-valued points of
{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{K}}
, and its
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module structure induces an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra homomorphism
\rho{M}:\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}_{a/\mathbb{K}}^{d})
. It is known that
E{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\mathbb{G}_{a/\mathbb{K}}^{d},\rho{M})
is an
A
-finite
t
-module over
\mathbb{K}
. Moreover, let
\Theta:\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}
be a morphism of dual
t
-motives. the corresponding
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module homomorphism

\overline{\Theta}:\matheur{M}_{1}/(\sigma-1)\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}/(\sigma-1)\matheur{M}_{2},

induces a morphism of
A
-finite
t
-modules
\overline{\Theta}:E_{\matheur{M}_{1}}\rightarrow E_{\matheur{M}_{2}}
.

(2) We refer the readers to
[
CP11
]
,
[
CP12
]
for explicit examples of how to associate dual
t
-motives to Drinfeld modules. For general dual
t
-motives and
A
-finite
t
-modules, see also
[
BP20
, §1.5.4]
,
[
NP21
, §3.1]
.

Definition 2.2.5
.

An injective morphism
\Theta:\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}
of dual
t
-motives with cokernel finite-dimensional over
\mathbb{K}
is called an
isogeny
. We say that the dual
t
-motives
\matheur{M}_{1}
and
\matheur{M}_{2}
are
isogenous
if there exists an isogeny
\Theta:\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}
.

Let
\Theta:\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}
be an isogeny of dual
t
-motives. We note that the induced morphism
\overline{\Theta}
of abelian
t
-modules is surjective with finite kernel (cf.
[
ABP04
, Lem. 4.1.4]
), whence an isogeny of abelian
t
-modules. We further note that the isogeny relation of dual
t
-motives is an equivalence relation (see
[
ABP04
, p. 266]
).

2.3.
Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motives associated with abelian
t
-modules

Let
\Omega_{\mathbb{K}[t]/\mathbb{K}}

\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}[t]\,dt
be the module of Kähler differentials of
\mathbb{K}[t]
over
\mathbb{K}
. Given an abelian
t
-module
E_{\rho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\mathbb{G}_{a/\mathbb{K}}^{d},\rho)
over
\mathbb{K}
, following Hartl and Juschka
[
HaJu20
, Proposition 2.4.3]
we define

(2.3.1)

\matheur{M}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{K}[t]}(\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho),\Omega_{\mathbb{K}[t]/\mathbb{K}}),

together with the following
\sigma
-action: for
\matheur{m}\in\matheur{M}(\rho)
,

(\sigma\cdot\matheur{m})(\tau\cdot m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{m}(\tau^{2}\cdot m)^{(-1)},\quad\forall\tau m\in\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho).

This gives a
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module structure on
\matheur{M}(\rho)
, and
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is free of finite rank over
\mathbb{K}[t]
. See also
[
NP21
, § 4.4]
. Moreover, the following holds.

Theorem 2.3.2
.

Let
E_{\rho}=(\mathbb{G}_{a/\mathbb{K}}^{d},\rho)
be an abelian
t
-module over
\mathbb{K}
. Then
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is a dual
t
-motive over
\mathbb{K}
isomorphic to
\matheur{N}(\rho)
as
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-modules. Consequently,
E_{\rho}
is
A
-finite, and
E_{\rho}\cong E_{\matheur{M}(\rho)}
.

Proof.

From
[
HaJu20
, Theorem 2.5.13]
, there exists an injective
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module homomorphism
\Theta:\matheur{M}(\rho)\hookrightarrow\matheur{N}(\rho)
.
As
\matheur{N}(\rho)
is free of finite rank over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
, so is
\matheur{M}(\rho)
.
Moreover, we have that

\displaystyle\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\sigma]}\matheur{M}(\rho)=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\left(\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)}\right)

\displaystyle=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\left(\frac{\mathscr{M}(\rho)}{\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\tau]}\mathscr{M}(\rho)=\dim E_{\rho}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\sigma]}\matheur{N}(\rho),

where the second equality follows from
[
HaJu20
, Remark 2.4.4 (c)]
.
This implies that the cokernel of
\Theta
must be of finite dimension over
\mathbb{K}
.
Since
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is free of finite rank over
\mathbb{K}[t]
, we obtain that
\matheur{N}(\rho)
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{K}[t]
-module.
Therefore
E_{\rho}
is
A
-finite, and by
[
HaJu20
, Theorem 2.5.13]
again
\Theta:\matheur{M}(\rho)\rightarrow\matheur{N}(\rho)
is an isomorphism.
Finally, the induced isomorphism
\overline{\Theta}:E_{\matheur{M}(\rho)}\cong E_{\matheur{N}(\rho)}

and the identification
E_{\matheur{N}(\rho)}=E_{\rho}
completes the proof.
∎

Remark 2.3.3
.

For each abelian
t
-module
E_{\rho}
, we call
\matheur{M}(\rho)
the
Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motive associated with
E_{\rho}
.
For our purpose, we utilize
\matheur{M}(\rho)
here (instead of
\matheur{N}(\rho)
) in order to derive the natural compatibility between the de Rham pairings of
E_{\rho}
and
\matheur{M}(\rho)
, see Section
8.3
.

2.4.
Uniformizability of dual
t
-motives

Given a dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
of rank
r
over
\mathbb{K}[t]
, we fix
\mathbf{m}\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(\matheur{M})
so that the entries of
\mathbf{m}
comprise a
\mathbb{K}[t]
-basis of
\matheur{M}
. Then the action of
\sigma
on
\mathbf{m}
is presented as

\sigma\mathbf{m}=\Upphi\mathbf{m}

for some
\Upphi\in\Mat_{r}(\mathbb{K}[t])
. Since
(t-\theta)^{N}\matheur{M}/\sigma\matheur{M}=(0)
,
\det\Phi
is of the form
c(t-\theta)^{n}
for some
c\in\mathbb{K}^{\times}
and an integer
n
. Let
\mathbb{T}
be the Tate algebra in the variable
t
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, which consists of all formal power series in
{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t]\!]}
that are convergent on the closed unit disk.

Let
\sigma
act on
\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}
via
f\otimes m\mapsto f^{(-1)}\otimes\sigma m
. Then it is not hard to see that the natural map

(2.4.1)

\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}\left(\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}\right)^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}

is injective, where
(\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M})^{\sigma}
denotes the part fixed by
\sigma
.

Definition 2.4.2
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a dual
t
-motive.
\matheur{M}
is said to be
uniformizable
(or rigid analytically trivial) if the injective map (
2.4.1
) is also surjective.

In fact, the equivalence in Theorem
2.2.3
preserves uniformizability.

Theorem 2.4.3

(See
[
HaJu20
, Prop. 2.4.17]
)
.

The category of unifomizable
A
-finite
t
-modules is equivalent to the category of uniformizable dual
t
-motives.

Remark 2.4.4
.

For a uniformizable dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
over
\bar{k}
, we define the
Betti
module of
\matheur{M}
by

H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigl(\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}\bigr)^{\sigma},

which is free of rank
r\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{rank}_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
. If we define
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\subset\mathbb{T}
to be the ring of rigid analytic functions on
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus\{\theta^{q^{i}}\mid i\in\mathbb{N}\}
, then the result of Hartl and Juschka
[
HaJu20
, Prop. 2.3.30]
shows that

H_{\textup{Betti}}(\matheur{M})=\left\{x\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}\mid\sigma x=x\right\}.

Remark 2.4.5
.

We note that the map (
2.4.1
) is surjective if and only if there exists a matrix
\Uppsi\in\GL_{r}(\mathbb{T})
satisfying the difference equation

\Uppsi^{(-1)}=\Upphi\Uppsi.

2.5.
Tannakian formalism for
t
-motives

For later use in transcendence problems, in this subsection we fix
\mathbb{K}
to be
\bar{k}
and review the theory developed by the third author of the present paper
[
P08
]
.

We let
\bar{k}(t)[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]
be the Laurent polynomial ring in
\sigma
over the field
\bar{k}(t)
subject to the relations

\sigma^{n}f=f^{(-n)}\sigma^{n},\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}.

A left
\bar{k}(t)[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]
-module is called a
pre-
t
-motive
if it is finite-dimensional over
\bar{k}(t)
. Morphisms of pre-
t
-motives are left
\bar{k}(t)[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]
-module homomorphisms.

It is natural to pass from a dual
t
-motive to a pre-
t
-motive via tensor product. More explicitly, let
\matheur{M}
be a dual
t
-motive and consider the tensor product
M\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
. We extend the action of
\sigma
on
\matheur{M}
to this tensor product via
f\otimes m\mapsto f^{(-1)}\otimes\sigma m
. So
M
is a finite-dimensional
\bar{k}(t)
-vector space on which the action of
\sigma
is invertible, and thereby one can regard it as a left
\bar{k}(t)[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]
-module. It follows that
M
is a pre-
t
-motive.

Given a pre-
t
-motive of dimension
r
over
\bar{k}(t)
, we take
\mathbf{m}\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(M)
so that the entries of
\mathbf{m}
comprise a
\bar{k}(t)
-basis of
M
. Then the action of
\sigma
on
M
is
represented as

\sigma\mathbf{m}=\Upphi\mathbf{m}

for some
\Upphi\in\GL_{r}(\bar{k}(t))
. Let
\mathbb{L}
be the fraction field of
\mathbb{T}
. Then
M
is called
rigid analytically trivial
if there exists
\Psi\in\GL_{r}(\mathbb{L})
satisfying

\Uppsi^{(-1)}=\Upphi\Uppsi.

Extending the action of
\sigma
on
\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\bar{k}(t)}M
via
f\otimes m\mapsto f^{(-1)}\otimes\sigma m
and denoting by
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(M)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\bar{k}(t)}M)^{\sigma}
the elements fixed by
\sigma
, one of the main results in
[
P08
]
is as follows.

Theorem 2.5.1

(Papanikolas
[
P08
, Thm. 3.3.15]
)
.

The category
\mathcal{R}
of rigid analytically trivial pre-
t
-motives forms a neutral Tannakian category over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with fiber functor

M\mapsto H_{\textup{Betti}}(M).

Definition 2.5.2
.

(1)

We define the category
\mathcal{AR}^{I}
of uniformizable dual
t
-motives up to isogeny as follows:

•

Objects of
\mathcal{AR}^{I}
: uniformizable dual
t
-motives.

•

Morphisms of
\mathcal{AR}^{I}
: For uniformizable dual
t
-motives
\matheur{M},\matheur{N}
,

\Hom_{\mathcal{AR}^{I}}(\matheur{M},\matheur{N})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}\Hom_{\bar{k}[t,\sigma]}\left(\matheur{M},\matheur{N}\right).

(2)

We define the
category
\mathcal{T}
of
t
-motives
to be the strictly full Tannakian subcategory of
\mathcal{R}
generated by the essential image of the functor

\Bigl(\matheur{M}\mapsto\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}\Bigr):\mathcal{AR}^{I}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}.

Given a
t
-motive
M
, we let
\mathcal{T}_{M}
be the strictly full Tannakian subcategory of
\mathcal{T}
generated by
M
. Then by Tannakian duality there exists an affine algebraic group scheme
\Gamma_{M}
defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
so that
\mathcal{T}_{M}
is equivalent to the
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
-finite dimensional representations of the algebraic group
\Gamma_{M}
. We call
\Gamma_{M}
the
t
-motivic Galois group
of
M
.

In the case that
M=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
for a uniformizable Anderson
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
, we let
\Upphi\in\Mat_{r}(\bar{k}[t])\cap\GL_{r}(\bar{k}(t))
be the matrix representing the action of
\sigma
on a
\bar{k}[t]
-basis of
\matheur{M}
, and let
\Uppsi\in\GL_{r}(\mathbb{T})
satisfy
\Uppsi^{(-1)}=\Upphi\Uppsi
. We note that by
[
ABP04
, Prop. 3.1.3]
the entries of
\Uppsi
are convergent on all of
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
. Let
\bar{k}\left(\Uppsi(\theta)\right)
be the field over
\bar{k}
generated by all the entries
\Uppsi_{ij}(\theta)
of
\Uppsi(\theta)
. The following equality is a function field analogue of Grothendieck’s periods conjecture for abelian varieties.

Theorem 2.5.3
.

(Papanikolas
[
P08
, Thm. 1.1.7]
)

Given a uniformizable dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
, we define
M\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
and let
(\matheur{M},\Upphi,\Uppsi)
be given as above. Then

\dim\Gamma_{M}=\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}(\Uppsi(\theta)).

3.
CM fields and CM types

3.1.
CM fields

A finite extension
\matheur{F}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is called
totally real
if the infinite place
\infty
of
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
splits completely in
\matheur{F}
. We note that
\matheur{F}
is then necessarily separable over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and that every separable finite extension
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
contains a maximal totally real intermediate field. We define the following notion of CM fields, which differs slightly from the classical case.

Definition 3.1.1
.

A finite separable extension
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is called a
CM field
if every place
\infty^{+}
of its maximal real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
lying over
\infty
in
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is non-split in
\matheur{K}
, i.e.,
\infty^{+}
has a unique extension to
\matheur{K}
.

Remark 3.1.2
.

(1) Based on this definition, the field
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is itself a CM field. This is partly for convenience so that we can incorporate the period of the Carlitz module in this context. Similarly, every totally real field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is also a CM field in our setting.

(2) Let
\matheur{K}
be an
imaginary
field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, i.e. the place
\infty
of
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is non-split in
\matheur{K}
, and suppose
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is separable. Then
\matheur{K}
is a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with
\matheur{K}^{+}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
. In particular, the constant field extension
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}(t)
is a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
for every positive integer
\ell
.

(3) Other typical examples of CM fields are the
Carlitz cyclotomic extensions
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
Given a non-constant polynomial
f\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
, the
f
-th Carlitz cyclotomic extension is the field
\matheur{K}_{f}
generated by the
f
-torsion points of the Carlitz
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
. It is known that
\matheur{K}_{f}
is finite abelian over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with Galois group isomorphic to
\left(\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]/(f)\right)^{\times}
under the Artin map (see
[
Hayes74
, Thm. 2.3]
or
[
Rosen02
, Thm. 12.8]
). The maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}_{f}^{+}
is the fixed field of
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
, and each place of
\matheur{K}_{f}^{+}
lying above
\infty
is totally ramified in
\matheur{K}_{f}
with ramification index
q-1
(see
[
Hayes74
, Thm. 3.2]
or
[
Rosen02
, Thm. 12.14]
).

(4) Unlike the classical case, the compositum of two CM fields over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
may not be a CM field.
For instance, let
\matheur{K}_{1}=\mathbb{F}_{2}(\sqrt[3]{t})
and
\matheur{K}_{2}=\mathbb{F}_{2}(\sqrt[3]{t+1})
. The infinite place
\infty
of
\mathbb{F}_{2}(t)
is totally ramified in both
\matheur{K}_{1}
and
\matheur{K}_{2}
, which says in particular that
\matheur{K}_{1}
and
\matheur{K}_{2}
are imaginary fields over
\mathbb{F}_{2}(t)
. The compositum
\matheur{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{K}_{1}\cdot\matheur{K}_{2}
has degree
9
over
\mathbb{F}_{2}(t)
and contains
\matheur{K}_{3}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{2}(\sqrt[3]{(t+1)/t})
. As

{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\![1/t]\!]}[x]\ \ni\ x^{3}-\frac{t+1}{t}\ \equiv\ x^{3}-1\quad\bmod(1/t),

it follows from Hensel’s lemma that there exists exactly one root of
x^{3}-(t+1)/t
in
{\mathbb{F}_{2}(\!(1/t)\!)}
. Thus
\infty
splits into two places in
\matheur{K}_{3}
(with residue degree equal to
1
and
2
, respectively), and both of these are totally ramified in
\matheur{K}
with ramification index
3
. Hence there are exactly two places of
\matheur{K}
lying above
\infty
, which implies that
\matheur{K}
cannot be a CM field.

(5) Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field and
\matheur{F}
be a totally real field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
(contained in an algebraic closure of
\matheur{K}
). We claim that the compositum
\matheur{K}\cdot\matheur{F}
of
\matheur{K}
and
\matheur{F}
is also a CM field with the maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}\cdot\matheur{F}
.
To prove this, let
\tilde{\infty}^{+}
be a place of
\matheur{K}^{+}\cdot\matheur{F}
lying above
\infty
and take
a place
\tilde{\infty}
of
\matheur{K}\cdot\matheur{F}
lying above
\tilde{\infty}^{+}
.
We also denote by

\infty_{K}
and
\infty^{+}
the places of
\matheur{K}
and
\matheur{K}^{+}
lying below
\tilde{\infty}
, respectively.
Since
\matheur{K}^{+}\cdot\matheur{F}
is totally real over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, one has that
\infty^{+}
splits completely in
\matheur{K}^{+}\cdot\matheur{F}
.
Thus we have the following (in-)equalities of the ramification indices and residue degrees:

(3.1.3)

\begin{cases}e(\tilde{\infty}^{+}/\infty^{+})=f(\tilde{\infty}^{+}/\infty^{+})=1;\\
e(\infty_{K}/\infty^{+})\leq e(\tilde{\infty}/\infty^{+})=e(\tilde{\infty}/\tilde{\infty}^{+});\\
f(\infty_{K}/\infty^{+})\leq f(\tilde{\infty}/\infty^{+})=f(\tilde{\infty}/\tilde{\infty}^{+}).\end{cases}

On the other hand, as
\matheur{K}
is a CM field, one has that

(3.1.4)

\displaystyle e(\infty_{K}/\infty^{+})f(\infty_{K}/\infty^{+})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]

\displaystyle\geq[\matheur{K}\cdot\matheur{F}:\matheur{K}^{+}\cdot\matheur{F}]

\displaystyle\geq e(\tilde{\infty}/\tilde{\infty}^{+})f(\tilde{\infty}/\tilde{\infty}^{+}).

By (
3.1.3
) and (
3.1.4
) we get

[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]=e(\infty_{K}/\infty^{+})f(\infty_{K}/\infty^{+})=e(\tilde{\infty}/\tilde{\infty}^{+})f(\tilde{\infty}/\tilde{\infty}^{+})=[\matheur{K}\cdot\matheur{F}:\matheur{K}^{+}\cdot\matheur{F}].

This says that
\tilde{\infty}
is the only place of
\matheur{K}\cdot\matheur{F}
lying above
\tilde{\infty}^{+}
, i.e.
\tilde{\infty}^{+}
is non-split in
\matheur{K}\cdot\matheur{F}
.
Since
\tilde{\infty}^{+}
is taken arbitrarily, the claim holds.

3.2.
Notation on curves

Given a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, we will assume henceforth for simplicity that
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is a geometric extension. As the case of non-geometric CM fields requires some additional care, we leave the details of the general case to
Appendix
A
.

Recall that
\matheur{K}^{+}
denotes the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
. Let
X
(resp.
X^{+}
) be a smooth, projective, geometrically connected algebraic curve defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
whose function field is
\matheur{K}
(resp.
\matheur{K}^{+}
). The field embeddings

\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}^{+}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K},

induce corresponding morphisms of algebraic curves over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
,

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{X/X^{+}}}
\textstyle{X^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{X^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}.}

For a chosen algebraically closed field
\mathbb{K}
with
k\subset\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, we denote by
\mathbf{X}
(resp.
\mathbf{X}^{+}
) the base change of
X
(resp.
X^{+}
) to
\mathbb{K}
, and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}
and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
the base change of the respective morphisms above to
\mathbb{K}
. Define
\mathbf{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X})
(resp.
\mathbf{K}^{+}
) to be the function field of
\mathbf{X}
(resp.
\mathbf{X}^{+}
) over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
O_{\matheur{K}}
(resp.
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
) be the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
(resp.
\matheur{K}^{+}
), and put
U\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Spec O_{\matheur{K}}
(resp.
U^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Spec O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
).
The base change of
U
and
U^{+}
to
\mathbb{K}
are denoted

\mathbf{U}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Spec(O_{\mathbf{K}})\quad\textup{and}\quad\mathbf{U}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Spec(O_{\mathbf{K}^{+}}),

where

O_{\mathbf{K}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O_{\matheur{K}}\cong\mathbb{K}[t]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}O{K}\quad\textup{and}\quad O_{\mathbf{K}^{+}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\cong\mathbb{K}[t]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}O{K}.

Using this framework one checks that

\mathbf{K}=\mathbb{K}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\matheur{K}\quad\textup{and}\quad\mathbf{K}^{+}=\mathbb{K}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\matheur{K}^{+}.

Note that
O_{\mathbf{K}}
(resp.
O_{\mathbf{K}^{+}}
) is the integral closure of
\mathbb{K}[t]
in
\mathbf{K}
(resp.
\mathbf{K}^{+}
), and that

\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{X}\setminus\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)\quad(\text{resp.}\quad\mathbf{U}^{+}=\mathbf{X}^{+}\setminus\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)).

3.3.
CM types

We continue in the setting above starting with a given geometric CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, and for now we set
\mathbb{K}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
in §
3.2
. Let

(3.3.1)

J{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\theta)=\{\xi\in\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\mid\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi)=\theta\}

be the set of points of
\mathbf{X}
lying above
\theta
. Denote by
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
the set of all
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra embeddings from
\matheur{K}
into
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
which send
t
to
\theta
. We may identify
J{K}
with
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
as follows. Each
\xi\in J{K}
corresponds to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism

\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\rightarrow X.

As
\xi
is not
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-valued (where
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
is the algebraic closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
), this morphism must factor through the generic point
\Spec(\matheur{K})\hookrightarrow X
.
Thus we obtain an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism
\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\rightarrow\Spec(\matheur{K})
, which corresponds to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra embedding

\nu_{\xi}:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty},\quad\nu_{\xi}(t)=\theta,

where the latter part follows from
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi)=\theta
. Conversely, for
\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
, the induced
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism

\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\Spec(\matheur{K})\hookrightarrow X

gives a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-valued point
\xi_{\nu}
of
\mathbf{X}
so that
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi_{\nu})=\theta
(as
\nu(t)=\theta
). It is clear that

\xi_{\nu_{\xi}}=\xi,\quad\forall\xi\in J{K},\quad\textup{and}\quad\nu_{\xi_{\nu}}=\nu,\quad\forall\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

Let
I{K}
be the free abelian group generated by all elements of
J{K}
. We may view
I{K}
as a subgroup of
\Div(\mathbf{X})
consisting of all the divisors supported on
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\theta)
.

Definition 3.3.2
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a geometric CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
. Let
I{K}^{0}
be the subgroup of
I{K}
consisting of the divisors

\Phi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi,\quad m_{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z},

satisfying

\sum_{\xi\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi_{1}^{+})}m_{\xi}=\sum_{\xi^{\prime}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi_{2}^{+})}m_{\xi^{\prime}},\quad\forall\xi_{1}^{+},\,\xi_{2}^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

For
\Phi\in I_{\matheur{K}}^{0}
, the
weight
of
\Phi
is then

\wt(\Phi)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi^{+})}m_{\xi}\quad\text{ for any }\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

We say that a
generalized CM type
of
\matheur{K}
is a nonzero effective divisor
\Xi
in
I{K}^{0}
, and that a
CM type
of
\matheur{K}
is a generalized CM type
\Xi
for which
\text{wt}(\Xi)=1
.

Remark 3.3.3
.

Let
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
. The correspondence between
J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
and
\Emb(\matheur{K}^{+},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
implies that there are exactly
d
points in
J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
, say
\xi_{1}^{+},\ldots,\xi_{d}^{+}
. Then a CM type of
\matheur{K}
must be of the form
\sum_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}\in I{K}
, where
\xi_{i}\in\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
is a point lying above
\xi_{i}^{+}
for
1\leq i\leq d
.

The following basic properties are straightforward from the definitions.

Proposition 3.3.4
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a geometric CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.

(1)

Define the generalized CM type

N{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}\xi\in I{K}^{0}.

Then
\wt(N{K})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]
, and for any
\Phi_{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, there exists
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
so that

\mu N{K}+\Phi_{0}

becomes a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.

(2)

Every generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
of
\wt(\Xi)=n
can be written as

\Xi=\Xi_{1}+\cdots+\Xi_{n},

where
\Xi_{1},\ldots,\Xi_{n}
are CM types of
\matheur{K}
.

Consequently,
I{K}^{0}
is generated by all the CM types of
\matheur{K}
.

Suppose that
\matheur{K}
and
\matheur{K}^{\prime}
are geometric CM fields over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and that
\varrho:\matheur{K}^{\prime}\rightarrow\matheur{K}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
-embedding. Let
\pi_{\varrho}:X\rightarrow X^{\prime}
be the corresponding surjective
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism of algebraic curves over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. When it is clear from the context, we will still denote the corresponding base changes to
\mathbb{K}
by
\varrho:\mathbf{K}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbf{K}
and
\pi_{\varrho}:\mathbf{X}\rightarrow\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
. Then
\pi_{\varrho}
induces a map
J_{\matheur{K}}\rightarrow J_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}
.
From the one-to-one correspondence

\begin{array}[]{ccc}J_{K}&\longleftrightarrow&\Emb\left(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\right),\\
\xi&\longleftrightarrow&\nu_{\xi}\end{array}

we obtain that

\nu_{\pi_{\varrho}(\xi)}=\nu_{\xi}\circ\varrho.

In particular, for any
\varrho\in\Aut_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\matheur{K})
, the automorphism group of
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, the corresponding map
\pi_{\varrho}
gives a permutation on
J{K}
. Defining

\xi^{\varrho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\varrho}(\xi)

for
\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}
, one has that

(\xi)^{\varrho_{1}\varrho_{2}}=(\xi^{\varrho_{1}})^{\varrho_{2}},\quad\forall\xi\in J{K}\ \textup{and}\ \varrho_{1},\,\varrho_{2}\in\Aut_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\matheur{K}).

Extending additively, we have a right action of
\Aut_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\matheur{K})
on
I{K}
, and the following lemma holds.

Lemma 3.3.5
.

For a generalized CM type
\Xi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi
of
\matheur{K}
and
\varrho\in\Aut_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\matheur{K})
,

\Xi^{\varrho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\cdot\xi^{\varrho}

is also a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
. In other words,
I{K}^{0}
is stable under the right action of
\Aut_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\matheur{K})
.

Remark 3.3.6
.

Since
\matheur{K}
is separable over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, every point
\xi\in J{K}
is actually defined over
k^{\mathrm{sep}}
. This also implies that
\nu_{\xi}
embeds
\matheur{K}
into
k^{\mathrm{sep}}
. We then have a natural (left) action of
\Gal(k^{\mathrm{sep}}/k)
on
J{K}
so that

\nu_{\varsigma(\xi)}=\varsigma\circ\nu_{\xi},\quad\forall\xi\in J{K}\text{ and }\varsigma\in\Gal(k^{\mathrm{sep}}/k).

This gives a
\Gal(k^{\rm sep}/k)
-module structure on
I{K}
, and
I{K}^{0}
is clearly a submodule of
I{K}
.

3.4.
Restriction and inflation of CM types

Let
\matheur{K}_{1}
and
\matheur{K}_{2}
be two geometric CM fields over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with
\matheur{K}_{1}\subset\matheur{K}_{2}
. Let
X_{1}
(resp.
X_{2}
) be the projective, smooth, geometrically connected curve over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
associated to
\matheur{K}_{1}
(resp.
\matheur{K}_{2}
), and let
\pi:X_{2}\rightarrow X_{1}
be the morphism over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
corresponding to the embedding
\matheur{K}_{1}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}_{2}
. Define

\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}:I_{\matheur{K}_{1}}\longrightarrow I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}\quad\textup{and}\quad\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}:I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}\longrightarrow I_{\matheur{K}_{1}}

by

\displaystyle\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Phi_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi^{*}(\Phi_{1}),\quad\forall\Phi_{1}\in I_{\matheur{K}_{1}},

\displaystyle\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Phi_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{*}(\Phi_{2}),\quad\forall\Phi_{2}\in I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}.

Then we have

\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}\Bigl(\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Phi_{1})\Bigr)=\pi_{*}\bigl(\pi^{*}(\Phi_{1})\bigr)=\deg\pi\cdot\Phi_{1}=[\matheur{K}_{2}:\matheur{K}_{1}]\cdot\Phi_{1},\ \forall\Phi_{1}\in I_{\matheur{K}_{1}},

and one checks that

\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}\bigl(I_{\matheur{K}_{1}}^{0}\bigr)\subset I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}^{0}\quad\textup{and}\quad\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}\bigl(I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}^{0}\bigr)\subset I_{\matheur{K}_{1}}^{0},

which in particular implies the following basic result.

Proposition 3.4.1
.

Let
\matheur{K}_{1}
and
\matheur{K}_{2}
be two geometric CM fields over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with
\matheur{K}_{1}\subset\matheur{K}_{2}
. For each generalized CM type
\Xi_{1}
of
\matheur{K}_{1}

(resp.
\Xi_{2}
of
\matheur{K}_{2}
)
