, the divisor
\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Xi_{1})

(resp.
\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Xi_{2})
)
is a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}_{2}

(resp.
\matheur{K}_{1}
)
.

3.5.
Reduction of points at infinity

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field which is geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, and recall the one-to-one correspondence between
J{K}
and
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
. Put

O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{\alpha\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\mid|\alpha|_{\infty}\leq 1\}.

For each
\xi\in J{K}
, the associated embedding
\nu_{\xi}\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
provides a valuation ring
\nu_{\xi}^{-1}(O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})
of
\matheur{K}
, whose maximal ideal gives rise to a closed point of
X
, and hence a morphism

\Spec\bigl(\nu_{\xi}^{-1}(O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})\bigr)\rightarrow X.

The ring homomorphisms

\nu_{\xi}^{-1}O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\overset{\nu_{\xi}}{\hookrightarrow}O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q},

where the latter map is given by reduction, gives us an
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-valued point of
X
,

\Spec(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\longrightarrow\Spec(O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\Spec\bigl(\nu_{\xi}^{-1}(O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})\bigr)\longrightarrow X,

denoted by
\overline{\infty}_{\xi}
, so that
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\overline{\infty}_{\xi})=\infty
. To summarize we have the following maps

J{K}

\mathrel{{\mathop{\longleftrightarrow}\limits}}

\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})

{\mathrel{\to}}

\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)

\xi

\longleftrightarrow

\nu_{\xi}

\longmapsto

\overline{\infty}_{\xi}
,

where the surjectivity of
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
is implied by the following proposition.

Proposition 3.5.1
.

Let
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a geometric CM field with notation given as above. Then

\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)=\bigl\{\overline{\infty}_{\xi}\mid\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}\bigr\}.

Proof.

Given
\overline{\infty}\in\mathbf{X}
with
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\overline{\infty})=\infty
, we know that
\overline{\infty}
must be
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-valued, and the valuation of
O_{\matheur{K},\overline{\infty}}
on
\matheur{K}
associated to (the
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
-orbit of)
\overline{\infty}
is extended from the one on
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
associated to
\infty\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Take an (
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra) embedding
\nu:O_{\matheur{K},\overline{\infty}}\hookrightarrow O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
so that the induced morphism

\Spec(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\longrightarrow\Spec(O_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})\overset{\nu^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\Spec\bigl(O_{\matheur{K},\overline{\infty}}\bigr)\longrightarrow X

corresponds to
\overline{\infty}
. Extending
\nu
to an embedding
\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
(still denoted by
\nu
), we obtain that
\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
. Thus the bijection between
J{K}
and
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
implies that there exists
\xi\in J{K}
so that
\nu=\nu_{\xi}
. The construction of
\nu
gives us that
\overline{\infty}=\overline{\infty}_{\xi}
, and the result holds.
∎

Remark 3.5.2
.

(1) Background for the correspondence between
\xi
and
\overline{\infty}_{\xi}
above is that the point
\xi
lies in the residue disk around the infinite point
\overline{\infty}_{\xi}
using Sinha’s Green’s function
[
Sinha97
, Section 2.3]
.

(2) When
\matheur{K}=\matheur{K}^{+}
is totally real over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, the infinite place
\infty
of
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
splits completely in
\matheur{K}
. In this case, there are exactly
[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
points of
X
(
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-valued) lying above
\infty
, and the corresponding valuations give all the embeddings from
\matheur{K}
to
{\mathbb{F}_{q}(\!(1/t)\!)}
. Together with the isomorphism
{\mathbb{F}_{q}(\!(1/t)\!)}\cong k_{\infty}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
via the evaluation map sending
t
to
\theta
, we obtain a one-to-one correspondence between
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
, whence the above surjective map
J{K}\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)

is actually bijective when
\matheur{K}
is totally real over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
For an arbitrary geometric CM field
\matheur{K}
with maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
, we then have the following commutative diagram:

(3.5.3)

\textstyle{J{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}}
\textstyle{\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\cdot)|_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}
\textstyle{\pi^{-1}_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}}
\textstyle{J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\Emb(\matheur{K}^{+},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty).}

Definition 3.5.4
.

Given a generalized CM type
\Xi=\sum_{\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}}m_{\xi}\xi
of a geometric CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, we set

\matheur{I}_{\Xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}}m_{\xi}\overline{\infty}_{\xi}.

Note that
\matheur{I}_{\Xi}
is an
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-rational divisor of
\mathbf{X}
supported on
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
, and that
\Xi-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}
has degree zero.
In the next section, we shall apply Lang’s isogeny theorem to introduce the shtuka functions associated to
\Xi
and to define CM dual
t
-motives via geometric constructions.

Remark 3.5.5
.

The above constructions and definitions for non-geometric CM fields are contained in Appendix
A
.

4.
CM dual
t
-motives

Throughout this section, we fix
\matheur{K}
to be a CM field that is geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
X
to be the curve over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
associated with
\matheur{K}
. We further fix an algebraically closed subfield
\mathbb{K}
of
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
containing
k
. Recall that the base change of
X
to
\mathbb{K}
is denoted by
\mathbf{X}
, and
\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X})
is the function field of
\mathbf{X}
over
\mathbb{K}
. For each integer
i
, we define the
i
-th Frobenius twist automorphism
(f\mapsto f^{(i)}):\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X})\rightarrow\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X})
, where
f^{(i)}
is the function in
\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X})
obtained by raising each coefficient of
f
to the
q^{i}
-th power. For each closed point
P
on
\mathbf{X}
, we define
P^{(i)}
to be the point on
\mathbf{X}
obtained by raising each coordinate of
P
to the
q^{i}
-th power. This action extends linearly to the divisor group
\Div(\mathbf{X})
.

4.1.
Shtuka functions

In what follows, we will construct a dual
t
-motive from a generalized CM type along the lines of
[
Sinha97
,
BP02
,
ABP04
]
. We first need the following lemma that is a consequence of Lang’s “isogeny theorem.”

Lemma 4.1.1
.

Let
k\subset\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
be an algebraically closed intermediate field. Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field, geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, and let
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
. Then there exists a divisor
W\in\Div(\mathbf{X})
and a function
h\in\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X})
so that

(4.1.2)

\operatorname{div}(h)=W^{(1)}-W+\Xi-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}.

Proof.

As mentioned in Definition
3.5.4
, the divisor
\Xi-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}
is of degree zero and hence the class
[\Xi-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}]
can be regarded as a point in the
\mathbb{K}
-valued points of the Jacobian
J_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbb{K})
of the curve
\mathbf{X}
, which is identified with the group of degree zero divisors
\Div^{0}(\mathbf{X})
modulo principal divisors. Lang’s isogeny theorem
[
Lang56
, Cor., p. 557]
asserts that the map

\left([D]\rightarrow[D-D^{(1)}]\right):J_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbb{K})\rightarrow J_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbb{K})

is surjective. It follows that there is a divisor
W
satisfying

W-W^{(1)}\sim\Xi-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}.

In other words, there exists
h\in\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X})
with the desired divisor.
∎

Definition 4.1.3
.

The function
h
in (
4.1.2
) is called a
shtuka function
associated to the divisor
W
for the generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

Remark 4.1.4
.

For the definition of shtuka functions in the case of non-geometric CM fields over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, see Lemma
B.1.1
.

4.2.
Constructions of dual
t
-motives

For a geometric CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, we fix a generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
and let
(W,h)
be given as in Lemma
4.1.1
. Recall that
O{K}
is the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
and
O_{\mathbf{K}}=\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O{K}
. We also put

\mathbf{U}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Spec O_{\mathbf{K}}=\mathbf{X}\setminus\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty).

Define

(4.2.1)

\displaystyle\matheur{M}=\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma\left(\mathbf{U},O_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)})\right)

\displaystyle\bigl(=\bigl\{f\in\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X})\bigm|\bigl(\operatorname{div}(f)-W^{(1)}\bigr)|_{\mathbf{U}}\geq 0\bigr\}\bigr),

which is a projective
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module of rank one, together with the
\sigma
-action,

\sigma m\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=h\cdot m^{(-1)},\quad\forall m\in\matheur{M}.

As
\mathbb{K}[t]
is contained in
O_{\mathbf{K}}
, the above
\sigma
-action gives
\matheur{M}
the structure of a left
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module.
Moreover, the basic approach of Sinha
[
Sinha97
]
(cf.
[
ABP04
]
,
[
BP02
]
) shows the following:

Theorem 4.2.2
.

Let hypotheses and notation be as in Lemma
4.1.1
. The left
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module
\matheur{M}=\Gamma(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}))
is a dual
t
-motive.

Proof.

(cf.
[
Sinha97
, Thm. 3.2.3]
and
[
ABP04
, Lemma 6.4.1]
) As
\matheur{M}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}
, it is free of finite rank over
\mathbb{K}[t]
. Further, multiplication by
h
induces a
\mathbb{K}
-linear isomorphism of sheaves

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}-\Xi+\matheur{I}_{\Xi}),

whence

\sigma\matheur{M}=\Gamma\left(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}-\Xi+\matheur{I}_{\Xi})\right)=\Gamma\left(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}-\Xi)\right).

It follows that if
\Xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}}m_{\xi}\cdot\xi
, then

(t-\theta)^{N}\matheur{M}\subset\sigma\matheur{M}

for all
N\geq\max\{m_{\xi}\}
.

It remains to show that
\matheur{M}
is finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
. We let
j
be the smallest positive integer so that
\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{(j)}=\matheur{I}_{\Xi}
and denote by
\mathbf{I}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{I}_{\Xi}+\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{(-1)}+\cdots+\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{(-j+1)}
, whence
\mathbf{I}^{(1)}=\mathbf{I}
. We claim that the support of
\mathbf{I}
is equal to
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
.

To see this, notice first that the support of
\mathbf{I}
is contained in
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
. On the other hand, let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
. Then by the definition of generalized CM type,

\sum_{\xi\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi^{+})}m_{\xi}=\text{wt}(\Xi)>0,\quad\forall\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

Hence the commutative diagram (
3.5.3
) ensures that

\Supp(\mathbf{I})\cap\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\infty^{+})\supseteq\Supp(\matheur{I}_{\Xi})\cap\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\infty^{+})\neq\emptyset,\quad\forall\infty^{+}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty).

Since
\matheur{K}
is a CM field, one has that
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\infty^{+})
is actually an orbit under Frobenius twisting. As
\mathbf{I}^{(1)}=\mathbf{I}
, we obtain that

\Supp(\mathbf{I})\supset\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\infty^{+})
for each

\infty^{+}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
.
Therefore
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
is contained in the support of
\mathbf{I}
, and the claim follows.

We now write

\matheur{M}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\matheur{M}_{n},\quad\text{ where }\quad\matheur{M}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma\left(\mathbf{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}+n{\mathbf{I}})\right).

For each
n>1
, multiplication by
\sigma^{j}
induces an isomorphism

\sigma^{j}:\frac{\matheur{M}_{n}}{\matheur{M}_{n-1}}\rightarrow\frac{\Gamma\left(\mathbf{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}-\Xi-\Xi^{(-1)}-\dots-\Xi^{(-j+1)}+(n+1)\mathbf{I})\right)}{\Gamma\left(\mathbf{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}-\Xi-\Xi^{(-1)}-\dots-\Xi^{(-j+1)}+n\mathbf{I})\right)}.

By the Riemann-Roch theorem
[
Mum66
, p. 80, Cor. 2]
, for
n
sufficiently large the natural injective map

\pi_{n}:\frac{\Gamma\left(\mathbf{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}-\Xi-\Xi^{(-1)}-\dots-\Xi^{(-j+1)}+(n+1)\mathbf{I})\right)}{\Gamma\left(\mathbf{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)}-\Xi-\Xi^{(-1)}-\dots-\Xi^{(-j+1)}+n\mathbf{I})\right)}\rightarrow\frac{\matheur{M}_{n+1}}{\matheur{M}_{n}}

is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{K}
-vector spaces since the dimensions of both sides are equal to
\deg\mathbf{I}
. So for a sufficiently large integer
n_{0}
and for
n\geq n_{0}
, the composition gives an isomorphism

\pi_{n}\circ\sigma^{j}:\frac{\matheur{M}_{n}}{\matheur{M}_{n-1}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\frac{\matheur{M}_{n+1}}{\matheur{M}_{n}}.

Since
\matheur{M}_{n_{0}}
is of finite dimension over
\mathbb{K}
, any
\mathbb{K}
-basis of
\matheur{M}_{n_{0}}
generates
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma^{j}]
and therefore over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
. Therefore the proof is complete.
∎

The ranks of
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{K}[t]
and
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
are determined explicitly as follows.

Proposition 4.2.3
.

The rank of
\matheur{M}
as a dual
t
-motive is

r(\matheur{M})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

The dimension of
\matheur{M}
is

d(\matheur{M})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\sigma]}\matheur{M}=\deg\Xi.

Proof.

As
\matheur{M}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}
, it must be free of rank
[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
over
\mathbb{K}[t]
.
The rank of
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
follows immediately from the standard result in the next lemma by taking
F=-W^{(1)}
and
E=\Xi
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.4
.

Let
E
and
F
be fixed divisors on
\mathbf{X}
with the polar part of
E
equal to the part of
E
supported at infinity. Then

\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Gamma\left(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F)\right)/\Gamma\left(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F-E)\right)=\deg E|_{\mathbf{U}}.

Proof.

We note that the condition on
E
forces
E|_{\mathbf{U}}
to be effective. It suffices to prove the case when
E=P
is a
\mathbb{K}
-valued point on
\mathbf{U}
. Clearly, we have

\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Gamma(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F))/\Gamma(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F-P))\leq 1.

On the other hand, fix
\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
. The Riemann-Roch theorem implies that for
n
large enough, we can find

0\neq f\in\Gamma(\mathbf{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F+n\overline{\infty}))\setminus\Gamma(\mathbf{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F-P+n\overline{\infty})),

which means in particular that

\ord_{P}(f)=-\ord_{P}(F)
.
Hence

0\neq f\in\Gamma(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F))\setminus\Gamma(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F-P))

and

\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Gamma(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F))/\Gamma(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(F-P))\geq 1
. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.2.5
.

The construction of the dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
for a generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
when
\matheur{K}
is a non-geometric CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is given in (
B.2.1
).

Definition 4.2.6
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a geometric CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
A
CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
is a dual
t
-motive isomorphic to
\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
for some pair
(W,h)
, where
h
is a shtuka function associated to
W
for
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

Proposition 4.2.7
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
Given CM dual
t
-motives
\matheur{M}_{i}
with generalized CM types
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{i})
for
1=1,\ldots,n
,
the tensor product

\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\cdots\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{n}

with
\sigma
-action defined by

\sigma(m_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes m_{n})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sigma m_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\sigma m_{n},\quad\forall m_{i}\in\matheur{M}_{i},\ i=1,\ldots,n,

is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{1}+\cdots+\Xi_{n})
.

Proof.

Here we prove the case when
\matheur{K}
is geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, and refer the reader to Proposition
B.2.7
for the non-geometric case.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
n=2
and

\matheur{M}_{i}=\matheur{M}_{(W_{i},h_{i})}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W_{i}^{(1)})\bigr),

where

h_{i}
is a shtuka function associated to
W_{i}
for
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{i})
for
i=1,2
.
Put

W\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=W_{1}+W_{2},\quad h\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=h_{1}\cdot h_{2},\quad\text{ and }\quad\Xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Xi_{1}+\Xi_{2}.

One has that

\operatorname{div}(h)=W^{(1)}-W+\Xi-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}).

Let
\matheur{M}=\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)})\bigr)
.
The
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}
-module isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W_{1}^{(1)})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W_{2}^{(1)})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)})

induces the following
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module isomorphism

\Theta:

\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{2}

\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}

\matheur{M}

m_{1}\otimes m_{2}

\longmapsto

m_{1}\cdot m_{2}
.

Moreover, for
m_{i}\in\matheur{M}_{i}
,
i=1,2
, we have

\Theta\bigl(\sigma(m_{1}\otimes m_{2})\bigr)=\Theta(h_{1}m_{1}^{(-1)}\otimes h_{2}m_{2}^{(-1)})=h(m_{1}\cdot m_{2})^{(-1)}=\sigma\Theta\big(m_{1}\otimes m_{2}).

Therefore
\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{2}
is isomorphic to the CM dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
with the generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
as desired.
∎

To end this subsection, we derive the following.

Proposition 4.2.8
.

CM dual
t
-motives with the same generalized CM type are isogeneous.

Proof.

We prove the case of geometric CM fields over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
here, and refer readers to Proposition
B.2.8
for the non-geometric case. Let
\matheur{K}
be a geometric CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
X
be the curve over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
associated with
\matheur{K}
.
Recall that
\mathbf{X}
is the base change of
X
to
\mathbb{K}
.
It is a theorem of Chow that the Jacobian
J_{\mathbf{X}}
of
\mathbf{X}
and the map of
\mathbf{X}
to
J_{\mathbf{X}}

are actually defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
(see
[
Chow54
, Section 6]
).
By the
first half of that theorem, the
q
-power Frobenius acts on
J_{\mathbf{X}}
, and
J_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
consists of the points on the Jacobian fixed by it.
Since the Brauer group of a finite field is trivial,

[
Milne86
, Remark 1.6]
shows that
J_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\simeq\text{Pic}^{0}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.
By this and the second half of Chow’s
theorem, points of
J_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
are precisely those represented
by the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational divisors of degree zero.

Now, let
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Take
h_{1}
and

h_{2}
to be shtuka functions associated to divisors

W_{1}
and
W_{2}
, respectively, for
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
That is, for
i=1,2
,

W_{i}^{(1)}-W_{i}+\Xi-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}=\operatorname{div}(h_{i}).

Let
D_{0}
be an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational divisor of degree one
on
\mathbf{X}

[
Weil74
, Cor. VII. 5.5]
, and set

W\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=W_{2}-W_{1}-(\deg W_{2}-\deg W_{1})\cdot D_{0}.

Then
\deg W=0
and

W^{(1)}-W\thicksim 0
.
Hence the divisor class
[W]
corresponds to a point in
J_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.
The above discussion assures that
[W]
is represented by an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational divisor of degree zero, which we
call
D_{1}
, so that there is a rational function
f_{1}
on
\mathbf{X}
such that

W=D_{1}+\operatorname{div}(f_{1}).

Put
D_{2}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-D_{1}-(\deg W_{2}-\deg W_{1})\cdot D_{0}
and
Q\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\infty)
.
As
D_{2}
and
Q
are both
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational and
\deg Q>0
,
by the Riemann-Roch theorem there exists a sufficiently large integer
n
and
f_{2}\in\matheur{K}
so that

\operatorname{div}(f_{2})+D_{2}+nQ\quad\text{ is effective.}

Let
D_{3}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{div}(f_{2})+D_{2}

and
f\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=f_{1}\cdot f_{2}
.
We obtain that

D_{3}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational divisor of
\mathbf{X}
which is effective on
\mathbf{U}
, and

\displaystyle W_{2}-W_{1}+D_{3}

\displaystyle=W_{2}-W_{1}+\operatorname{div}(f_{2})-D_{1}-(\deg W_{2}-\deg W_{1})\cdot D_{0}

\displaystyle=W-D_{1}+\operatorname{div}(f_{2})\ =\ \operatorname{div}(f_{1}\cdot f_{2})\ =\ \operatorname{div}(f).

In particular, we have that

\operatorname{div}(f^{(1)}/f)=\operatorname{div}(h_{2}/h_{1})
.
Replacing
f

by a suitable
\mathbb{K}
-scalar multiple if necessary, we may further assume that
f^{(1)}/f=h_{2}/h_{1}
. Finally, define

\Theta:\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}\quad m_{1}\longmapsto f^{(1)}m_{1},\quad\forall m_{1}\in\matheur{M}_{1}.

As for each
m_{1}\in\matheur{M}_{1}
,

\operatorname{div}(f^{(1)}m_{1})-W_{2}^{(1)}=(\operatorname{div}(m_{1})-W_{1}^{(1)})+D_{3}

is always effective on
\mathbf{U}
,

\Theta
is a well-defined
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module homomorphism.
Moreover, the cokernel of
\Theta
is finite dimensional over
\mathbb{K}
by Lemma
4.2.4
, and

\Theta(\sigma m_{1})=\Theta(h_{1}m_{1}^{(-1)})=f^{(1)}h_{1}m_{1}^{(-1)}=h_{2}(fm_{1}^{(-1)})=\sigma\Theta(m_{1}),\ \forall\,m_{1}\in\matheur{M}_{1}.

Therefore
\Theta:\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}
is actually an isogeny, and the assertion holds.
∎

4.3.
Uniformization of CM dual
t
-motives

In this subsection, we shall outline the key ingredients of the argument of the uniformizability of CM dual
t
-motives, and refer the readers to Appendix
C
for the complete details.
Without loss of generality,
we may choose
\mathbb{K}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
throughout this subsection.

Let
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field with maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
.
We put
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
and let
\Xi=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{d}
be a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Notice that

\nu_{i}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu_{\xi_{i}}\big|_{\matheur{K}^{+}}:\matheur{K}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
,
i=1,\ldots,d
,
give all the embeddings from
\matheur{K}^{+}
into
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Recall that (see
[
And86
, Section 4.3]
) a
Hilbert-Blumenthal
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
-module
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
is a pure uniformizable abelian
t
-module
E
of dimension
d
equipped with a structure homomorphism
\rho_{E}:O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\rightarrow\End_{\text{ab.}\ t-{\rm mod.}}(E)
extending the tautological homomorphism
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]\rightarrow\End_{\textup{ab.\,$t$-mod.}}(E)
such that the induced
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-linear representation
\partial\rho_{E}:O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(\Lie(E))
is isomorphic to
\nu_{1}^{+}\oplus\cdots\oplus\nu_{d}^{+}
.
Set

\nu_{\Xi}:\matheur{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d},\quad\nu_{\Xi}(\alpha)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\nu_{\xi_{1}}(\alpha),\ldots,\nu_{\xi_{d}}(\alpha)),\quad\forall\alpha\in\matheur{K}.

Let
O{K}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
. Then
\nu_{\Xi}(O{K})
becomes a discrete
A
-lattice in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

There exists a Hilbert-Blumenthal
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
-module
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d},\rho^{\Xi})
whose period lattice is
\nu_{\Xi}(O{K})\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}=\Lie(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d})
.

Proof.

This follows directly from
[
And86
, Theorem 7 (II)]
.
∎

Moreover, by
[
And86
, Theorem 7 (I)]
we can actually extend
\rho^{\Xi}
to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra homomorphism from
O{K}
into
\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}({\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})
so that

\partial\rho^{\Xi}_{a}=\nu_{\xi_{1}}(a)\oplus\cdots\oplus\nu_{\xi_{d}}(a)
for every
a\in O{K}
. Recall that in (
2.3.1
),
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is the Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motive associated to
\rho^{\Xi}
.

Theorem 4.3.2
.

For each CM type
\Xi
of a given CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
,

\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is a CM dual
t
-motive with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

Remark 4.3.3
.

Note that in
[
BP02
, Sec. 2.3]
,
\matheur{K}
is called the CM field of the
t
-module
\rho^{\Xi}
and
\rho^{\Xi}
has CM type
\nu_{\Xi}
. Comparing with Theorem
4.3.2
, this matches with the present notion of the CM type of
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
.

Outline of the proof of Theorem
4.3.2

•

(Step 1.) As

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}\bigl(\nu_{\Xi}(O{K})\bigr)=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]

and the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra homomorphism
\rho^{\Xi}:O{K}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}_{a/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}^{d})
induces an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-algebra embedding
O{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t,\sigma]}(\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi}))
, we have that

\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O{K}
(see Lemma
C.1.1
).

•

(Step 2.) For each
\xi\in J{K}
, let
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
be the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
corresponding to
\xi
, and
put
\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi_{i}}
.
Then we show in Lemma
C.2.5
that

\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})=\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\cdot\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi}).

•

(Step 3.) Finally, since
\Xi
is a CM type of
\matheur{K}
and
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is pure (cf. Proposition
C.2.4
), the proof rests on the following characterization assuring that
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is a CM dual
t
-motive with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

We capture this situation in the following slightly more general result.

Proposition 4.3.4
.

(See Proposition
C.1.3
)

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
,
and
O{K}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
.
Given a dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
, suppose the following conditions hold:

(1)

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
;

(2)

there is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-algebra embedding
O{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t,\sigma]}\matheur{M}
, which induces an
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module structure on
\matheur{M}
;

(3)

there exists a (generalized) CM type
\Xi{M}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi
of
\matheur{K}
so that
\sigma\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi{M}}\matheur{M}
where
\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi{M}}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}}
;

(4)

\matheur{M}
is pure.

Then
\matheur{M}
is a CM dual
t
-motive with (generalized) CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi{M})
.

Remark 4.3.5
.

We briefly sketch the proof of the above characterization property when
\matheur{K}
is a geometric CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
. Conditions (1) and (2) imply that

\matheur{M}
is a projective
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module of rank one (see Lemma
C.1.1
).
We may write
\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{J}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}
for some fractional ideal
\mathfrak{J}^{(1)}
of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
and
\mathbf{m}\in\matheur{M}
.
From condition (3), we derive that

O_{\mathbf{K}}\cdot\sigma\mathbf{m}=\mathfrak{J}^{(1)}\cdot\mathfrak{J}^{-1}\cdot\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi{M}}\cdot\mathbf{m},

and hence there exists a nonzero function
h\in\mathfrak{J}^{(1)}\cdot\mathfrak{J}^{-1}\cdot\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\subset\mathbf{K}
for which

\sigma\cdot\mathbf{m}=h\mathbf{m}
.
Therefore

(4.3.6)

h\cdot O_{\mathbf{K}}=\mathfrak{J}^{(1)}\cdot\mathfrak{J}^{-1}\cdot\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi{M}}.

Let
W_{\mathfrak{J}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{x\in\mathbf{U}}\ord_{\mathfrak{P}_{x}}(\mathfrak{J})\cdot x\in\Div(\mathbf{X})
. The equation (
4.3.6
) gives rise to

\operatorname{div}(h)=W_{\mathfrak{J}}^{(1)}-W_{\mathfrak{J}}+\Xi{M}-\Upsilon,

where
\rm{Supp}(\Upsilon)\subset\mathbf{X}\setminus\mathbf{U}
.
One technical step is to use the purity of
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
to find a divisor
W_{\infty}\in\Div(\mathbf{X})
with
\rm{Supp}(W_{\infty})\subset\mathbf{X}\setminus\mathbf{U}
so that

W_{\infty}^{(1)}-W_{\infty}=I_{\Xi{M}}-\Upsilon
(cf. Lemma
C.2.5
).
Put
W\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=W_{\mathfrak{J}}+W_{\infty}
.
Then we obtain

\operatorname{div}(h)=W^{(1)}-W+\Xi-I_{\Xi{M}}.

By definition,

M_{(W,h)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma\left(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}(-W^{(1)})\right)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{f\in\mathbf{K}\mid\left(\operatorname{div}(f)-W^{(1)}\right)|_{\mathbf{U}}\geq 0\right\}=\mathfrak{J}^{(1)},

and so the map
\Theta\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left(x\mapsto x\cdot\mathbf{m}\right):M_{(W,h)}=\mathfrak{J}^{(1)}\rightarrow\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{J}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}
is an isomorphism of left
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-modules, thus completing the proof.

\Box

Since
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d},\rho^{\Xi})
is pure and uniformizable, so is
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})

(cf. Proposition
C.2.4
). We are now able to show the following.

Theorem 4.3.7
.

Every CM dual
t
-motive is pure and uniformizable.

Proof.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field and
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Suppose
{\rm wt}(\Xi)=n
.
By Proposition
3.3.4
(2), we may write
\Xi=\Xi_{1}+\cdots+\Xi_{n}
, where
\Xi_{1},\ldots,\Xi_{n}
are CM types of
\matheur{K}
. For
1\leq i\leq n
, Theorem
4.3.2
implies that
\matheur{M}_{i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi_{i}})
is a CM dual
t
-motive with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{i})
.
The purity and uniformizability of
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi_{1}}),\ldots,\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi_{n}})
imply that (cf.
[
HaJu20
, Propsition 2.4.10 (e)]
)

\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\matheur{M}_{n}

is pure and uniformizable.

As
\matheur{M}_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\cdots\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{n}
is a quotient of
\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\matheur{M}_{n}
under the canonical homomorphism

\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\matheur{M}_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\cdots\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{n},

it follows from
[
HaJu20
, Proposition 2.4.10 (c)]
that
\matheur{M}_{0}
is also pure and uniformizable.
By Proposition
4.2.7
,
\matheur{M}_{0}
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
Since every CM dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
is isogeneous to
\matheur{M}_{0}
by Proposition
4.2.8
,
we obtain that
\matheur{M}
must be pure and uniformizable by
[
HaJu20
, Proposition 2.4.10 (c)]
again.
∎

5.
Analogue of Shimura’s period symbols

In this section, we fix
\mathbb{K}=\bar{k}
.

5.1.
de Rham module and pairing

Let
\matheur{M}
be a dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
.
For our purposes here the
de Rham module of
\matheur{M}
over
\bar{k}
is the following (cf.
[
HaJu20
, Section 4.5]
):

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M},\bar{k}).

Elements of
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
are called
differentials of
\matheur{M}
over
\bar{k}
.
When it is clear from the context, we also identify
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
with the space of
\bar{k}
-linear functionals on
\matheur{M}
which factor through
\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}
.
Given
\omega\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
,
we naturally extend
\omega
to a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-linear functional on
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
by setting

\omega(f\otimes m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=f(\theta)\cdot\omega(m),\quad\forall f\otimes m\in\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}.

It follows that
\omega((t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger})=0
, i.e.,
\omega
induces a linear functional
\omega:\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Suppose now that
\matheur{M}
is uniformizable, and recall from Remark
2.4.4
that
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\subset\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-submodule. The
de Rham pairing
of
\matheur{M}
is defined as follows:

(5.1.1)

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\times H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty},\quad(\omega,\gamma)\longmapsto\int_{\gamma}\omega\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\omega(\gamma),

and every period integral
\int_{\gamma}\omega
is called a
period of
\matheur{M}
.

Lemma 5.1.2
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a uniformizable dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
.
The de Rham pairing of
\matheur{M}
in (
5.1.1
) is non-degenerate.
Consequently,
put
\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\bar{k}}\matheur{M}
and let the de Rham module of
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
be

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\bar{k}}H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k}).

Then the de Rham pairing of
\matheur{M}
induces the following comparison isomorphism

\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\bigl(\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\bigr)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

Proof.

We first embed
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
into

\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\bar{k}}H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\cong H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\cong\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

Let
\{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}\}
be an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-basis of
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
, and let
v_{i}
be the image of
\gamma_{i}
in the quotient module
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}
for
i=1,\ldots,r
.
Then
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{r}\}
forms a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-basis of
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}
(as
\matheur{M}
is uniformizable).

Given
\omega\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
with
\int_{\gamma}\omega=0
for every
\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
, by extending
\omega
to a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-linear functional on
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}
we have that

\omega(v_{i})=\int_{\gamma_{i}}\omega=0,\quad\text{ for $i=1,\ldots,r$.}

It follows that
\omega
is the zero map on
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}
and so
\omega=0
.

On the other hand, given
\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
, we may choose our basis
\{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}\}
of
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
suitably so that

\gamma=f(t)\cdot\gamma_{1},\quad\textup{for some $f(t)\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]$.}

Now suppose

0=\int_{\gamma}\omega\quad(=f(\theta)\cdot\omega(v_{1})),\quad\forall\,\omega\in H_{\rm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k}).

As
v_{1}
is nonzero in
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}
and elements in
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
span the entire
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-vector space
\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
,
we must have that
f(\theta)=0
, which implies
f(t)=0
. Therefore
\gamma=0
as desired. In conclusion, the de Rham pairing of
\matheur{M}
is non-degenerate.
∎

5.1.3.
Push-forward and pull-back.

Let
f:\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}
be a morphism between dual
t
-motives. Extending
f
to a homomorphism (still denoted by
f
)
\mathbb{M}_{1}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}_{2}^{\dagger}

induces the
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
-linear
push-forward
homomorphism on
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{1})
:

f_{*}:H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{1})\rightarrow H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{2}),\quad f_{*}\gamma\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=f(\gamma),\quad\forall\,\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{1}).

On the other hand
f
also induces the
\bar{k}
-linear
pull-back
homomorphism on
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}_{2})
:

f^{*}:H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}_{2},\bar{k})\longrightarrow H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}_{1},\bar{k}),\quad\omega\longmapsto f^{*}\omega\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\omega\circ f.

The following lemma is straightforward.

Lemma 5.1.4
.

Let
f:\matheur{M}_{1}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{2}
be a morphism of dual
t
-motives over
\bar{k}
. Given
\gamma_{1}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{1})
and
\omega_{2}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}_{2},\bar{k})
, we have

\int_{f_{*}\gamma_{1}}\omega_{2}=\int_{\gamma_{1}}f^{*}\omega_{2}.

5.2.
Definition of period symbols

To define period symbols in our setting, we first need the following proposition.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
For each
\xi\in J{K}
, there exists a nonzero differential
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
, unique up to a
\bar{k}^{\times}
-multiple, satisfying

\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}(\alpha\cdot m)=\nu_{\xi}(\alpha)\cdot\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}(m),\quad\text{$\forall\,m\in\matheur{M}$ and $\alpha\in O{K}$.}

We call
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
the
differential of
\matheur{M}
associated with
\xi
.

Proof.

For each
\xi\in J{K}
, let
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
be the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
associated to
\xi
,
i.e.
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
is generated by
\alpha-\nu_{\xi}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in O{K}
. We consider the following isomorphisms

\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}\cong\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{(t-\theta)O_{\mathbf{K}}}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}\cong\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\prod_{\xi\in J_{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}\cong\bigoplus_{\xi\in J_{K}}\left(\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}\right),

and note in particular that the composite map above sends
\alpha\cdot\bar{m}
to
(\nu_{\xi}(\alpha)\otimes m)_{\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}}
for
\alpha\in O_{\matheur{K}}
and
m\in\matheur{M}
.

Note further that for
\omega\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
the condition

\omega(\alpha\cdot m)=\nu_{\xi}(\alpha)\cdot\omega(m),\quad\textup{$\forall\,m\in\matheur{M}$ and $\alpha\in O{K}$,}

is equivalent to the identity

\omega\bigl((\alpha-\nu_{\xi}(\alpha))\cdot m\bigr)=0,\quad\textup{$\forall\,m\in\matheur{M}$ and $\alpha\in O{K}$,}

which is equivalent to saying that
\omega
factors through
\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}\cong\matheur{M}/\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}
. As
\matheur{M}
is a projective
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module of rank one,
\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}
is a free
O_{\mathbf{K}}/(t-\theta)O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module of rank one. Since

\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}}\cong\bar{k},\quad\forall\,\xi\in J{K},

we see that

\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\matheur{M}/\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M},\bar{k})
is one dimensional over
\bar{k}
, and hence the desired result follows.
∎

Recall in Remark
1.3.2
(1) that for
x
,
y\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
, we let
x\sim y
denote that
x/y\in\bar{k}^{\times}
.
Let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with the generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
and fix any
\xi\in J{K}
.
As
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
is a projective
O{K}
-module of rank one, we have that

\int_{\gamma_{1}}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\sim\int_{\gamma_{2}}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\quad\text{ for nonzero cycles $\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})$}.

Moreover, let
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
be another CM dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
with the same generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
. By Lemma
5.1.4
, the isogeny between
\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\bar{k}
leads to

\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\sim\int_{\gamma^{\prime}}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi},\quad\forall\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\text{ and }\gamma^{\prime}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime}).

It follows that up to algebraic multiples, the period integral
\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
depends only on the generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
and the chosen
\xi\in J{k}
.
This leads to the following definition.

Definition 5.2.2
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
. Given
\xi\in J{K}
, take a CM dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
and the differential
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
of
\matheur{M}
associated with
\xi
(given in Proposition
5.2.1
).
We define the following
period symbol
:

\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left(\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\right)\cdot\bar{k}^{\times}\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}.

As mentioned in Remark
1.3.2
(1), we use the notation
p{K}(\xi,\Xi)
for an arbitrary representative of the coset
\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi)
, and call it a period symbol as well if there is no risk of confusion.

Recall the fundamental period
\tilde{\pi}
of the Carlitz
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module
\mathbf{C}
. When
\matheur{K}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, we note that every generalized CM type must be of the form
\Xi=n\xi_{\theta}
for some positive integer
n
, where
\xi_{\theta}
is the point
t=\theta
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{/\bar{k}}
. In the following proposition, we calculate the period symbol in this case.

Proposition 5.2.3
.

Let
n
be a positive integer and let
\Xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=n\xi_{\theta}
. Then we have that

p_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\xi_{\theta},\Xi)\sim\tilde{\pi}^{n}.

Proof.

To calculate the period symbol
p_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\xi_{\theta},\Xi)
, we note that in this case
\matheur{I}_{\Xi}=n\cdot\infty
, and we can take
h\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(t-\theta)^{n}
and
W=0
in Theorem
4.2.2
. So the associated dual
t
-motive is given by
\matheur{C}^{\otimes n}=\Gamma\left(\mathbf{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}}\right)=\bar{k}[t]
, on which the
\sigma
-action is given by

\sigma\cdot x=(t-\theta)^{n}x^{(-1)},\quad x\in\matheur{C}^{\otimes n}.

We note that
\matheur{C}^{\otimes n}
is the dual
t
-motive associated to the
n
-th tensor power
{\mathbf{C}}^{\otimes n}
of the Carlitz module (see
[
CPY19
, p. 428]
).

Define

\Omega(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(-\theta)^{\frac{-q}{q-1}}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\biggl(1-\frac{t}{\theta^{q^{i}}}\biggr)\in{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t]\!]},

where
(-\theta)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}
is a suitable choice of
(q-1)
-st root of
-\theta
so that
\frac{1}{\Omega(\theta)}=\tilde{\pi}
. We note that
\Omega^{n}
satisfies the functional equation
(\Omega^{n})^{(-1)}=(t-\theta)^{n}\Omega^{n}
, and so
1/\Omega^{n}
constitutes an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-basis of the Betti-module
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{C}^{\otimes n})
. It follows that

p_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\xi_{\theta},\Xi)\sim\int_{\Omega^{-n}}\omega_{\matheur{C}^{\otimes n},\xi_{\theta}}\sim\Omega^{-n}(\theta)=\tilde{\pi}^{n}.\qed

5.3.
Fundamental properties of period symbols

In the following lemma we show that our period symbols satisfy the
additive
property.

Lemma 5.3.1
.

Let
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field and
\Xi_{1},\ldots,\Xi_{n}
be generalized CM types of
\matheur{K}
. Let
\Xi=\Xi_{1}+\cdots+\Xi_{n}
. For each
\xi\in J{K}
, we have that

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi_{i})=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}}(\xi,\Xi)\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}.

Proof.

Let
\matheur{M}_{1},\ldots,\matheur{M}_{n}
be CM dual
t
-motives over
\bar{k}
with the generalized CM types
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{1}),\ldots,(\matheur{K},\Xi_{n})
, respectively.
By Proposition
4.2.7
,

\matheur{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\cdots\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{n}

is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
. Moreover, for
1\leq i\leq n
, let
\omega_{\matheur{M}_{i},\xi}
be the differential of
\matheur{M}_{i}
associated with
\xi
. We then set

\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}=\omega_{\matheur{M}_{1},\xi}\otimes\cdots\otimes\omega_{\matheur{M}_{n},\xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=m_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes m_{n}\mapsto\omega_{\matheur{M}_{1},\xi}(m_{1})\cdot\cdots\cdot\omega_{\matheur{M}_{n},\xi}(m_{n}),

which is a well-defined (from the universal property of tensor products) differential of
\matheur{M}
associated with
\xi
. Notice that

H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})=H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{1})\otimes_{O{K}}\cdots\otimes_{O{K}}H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{n}).

Therefore taking nonzero cycles
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n}
in
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{1}),\ldots,H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}_{n})
, respectively, and choosing

\gamma=\gamma_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\gamma_{n}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
,
we obtain that

\prod_{i=1}^{n}p{K}(\xi,\Xi_{i})\sim\prod_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\gamma_{i}}\omega_{\matheur{M}_{i},\xi}=\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\sim p{K}(\xi,\Xi).\qed

By applying Lemma
5.3.1
, we now extend the period symbols to a unique bi-additive pairing.

Theorem 5.3.2
.

For each CM field
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, there exists a unique bi-additive pairing

\mathcal{P}{K}:I{K}\times I{K}^{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}

satisfying:

(1)

\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi)
is the period symbol in Definition
5.2.2
for each point
\xi\in J{K}
and each generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
.

(2)

Let
\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field containing
\matheur{K}
. For
\Phi^{\prime}\in I_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}
and
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
,

\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}}\bigl(\mathrm{Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{\prime}),\Phi^{0}\bigr)=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\bigl(\Phi^{\prime},\mathrm{Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{0})\bigr).

(3)

Let
\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field containing
\matheur{K}
, and let
\matheur{K}^{\prime+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}^{\prime}
.
For
\Phi\in I{K}
and
\Phi^{\prime,0}\in I_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}^{0}
, we have

\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\bigl(\mathrm{Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi),\Phi^{\prime,0}\bigr)=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}}\bigl(\Phi,\mathrm{Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{\prime,0})\bigr).

(4)

Let
\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field together with an isomorphism
\varrho:\matheur{K}^{\prime}\rightarrow\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, and let
\pi_{\varrho}:X\rightarrow X^{\prime}
be the isomorphism of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-schemes corresponding to
\varrho
.
Then

\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\bigl((\pi_{\varrho})_{*}(\Phi),(\pi_{\varrho})_{*}(\Phi^{0})\bigr)=\mathcal{P}{K}(\Phi,\Phi^{0}),\quad\forall\,(\Phi,\Phi^{0})\in I{K}\times I{K}^{0}.

(5)

Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
Given
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
and
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, one has

\tilde{\pi}^{\text{\rm wt}(\Phi^{0})}\sim\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}p{K}(\xi,\Phi^{0}).

Our strategy matches with Deligne’s table in
[
De80
, p. 29]
for the classical case. We include the detailed proofs of each property individually as follows.

Proof of Theorem
5.3.2
(1).

As
I{K}
is the free abelian group generated by
\xi\in J{K}
and
I{K}^{0}
is the subgroup of
I{K}
generated by all the generalized CM types of
\matheur{K}
(see Proposition
3.3.4
), the pairing
\mathcal{P}{K}
is uniquely determined by property
(1)
. It suffices to prove that it is well-defined.

Given generalized CM types
\Xi_{1},\ldots,\Xi_{n}
of
\matheur{K}
, suppose

m_{1}\Xi_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}\Xi_{n}=0,\quad m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}.

Without loss of generality, assume that
m_{1},\ldots,m_{\ell}>0
and
m_{\ell+1},\ldots,m_{n}<0
.
Then

\Xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=m_{1}\Xi_{1}+\cdots+m_{\ell}\Xi_{\ell}=(-m_{\ell+1})\Xi_{\ell+1}+\cdots+(-m_{n})\Xi_{n}

is also a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
. For
\xi\in I{K}
, by Lemma
5.3.1
we have

\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi_{i})^{m_{i}}=\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi)=\prod_{j=\ell+1}^{n}\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi_{j})^{-m_{j}},

which means that

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi_{i})^{m_{i}}=1\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}.

Thus
\mathcal{P}{K}
is well-defined.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.3.2
(2).

According to the additive properties of
\mathrm{Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}
and
\mathrm{Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}
, we may assume that
\Phi^{\prime}=\xi^{\prime}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}
and
\Phi^{0}=\Xi\in I{K}^{0}
is a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with the generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
Put

\matheur{M}^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=O_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\otimes_{O{K}}\matheur{M}\quad(\cong O_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}),

whose
\bar{k}[t,\sigma]
-module structure is induced from
\matheur{M}
.
As
\matheur{M}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}
, we see that
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}
.
Moreover, we get that

\sigma\matheur{M}^{\prime}=O_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\otimes_{O{K}}\sigma\matheur{M}=O_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{\Xi^{\prime}}\matheur{M}^{\prime},

where
\Xi^{\prime}=\Inf_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Xi)
and
\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{\Xi^{\prime}}
is the ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}
corresponding to
\Xi^{\prime}
.
Finally, the natural surjective morphism

\matheur{M}^{\oplus[\matheur{K}^{\prime}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]}\cong O_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}\matheur{M}\twoheadrightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime},

ensures the purity of
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
by
[
HaJu20
, Proposition 2.4.10 (c)]
.
Therefore Proposition
4.3.4
implies that
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K}^{\prime},\Inf_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Xi))
,
and we have a natural injective morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
of dual
t
-motives which is
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-linear. In particular, the push-forward
f_{*}:H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\rightarrow H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
is also injective.

On the other hand, for a differential
\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\bar{k})
associated with
\xi^{\prime}
, we note that its pull-back
f^{*}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
is a differential associated with
\xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}/\mathbf{X}}(\xi^{\prime})=\text{Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\xi^{\prime})
.
Hence for a nonzero
\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
we have

p_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}(\xi^{\prime},\text{Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Xi))\sim\int_{f_{*}\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}\sim\int_{\gamma}f^{*}\omega_{M^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}\sim p{K}(\text{Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\xi^{\prime}),\Xi).

Therefore property (2) holds.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.3.2
(3).

We may assume that
\Phi=\xi\in J{K}
and
\Phi^{\prime,0}=\Xi^{\prime}\in I_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}^{0}
is a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}^{\prime}
.
Put
n=[\matheur{K}^{\prime}:\matheur{K}]
.
Let
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K}^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})
over
\bar{k}
.
Take

\matheur{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigwedge^{n}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}^{\prime},\quad\text{the $n$-th exterior product of $\matheur{M}^{\prime}$ over $O_{\mathbf{K}}$,}

which is equipped with a
\sigma
-action defined by

\sigma\cdot(m_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge m_{n})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\sigma m_{1})\wedge\cdots\wedge(\sigma m_{n}).

Put
\Xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Res_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Xi^{\prime})
.
Let
\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{\Xi^{\prime}}
(resp.
\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}
) be the ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}
(resp.
O_{\mathbf{K}}
) corresponding to
\Xi^{\prime}
(resp.
\Xi
).
Then
\matheur{M}
is a projective
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module of rank one, and

\sigma\matheur{M}=\bigwedge^{n}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\sigma\matheur{M}^{\prime}=\bigwedge^{n}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi^{\prime}}^{\prime}\matheur{M}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\bigwedge^{n}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\matheur{M}.

Moreover, the surjectivity of the morphisms

\bigotimes_{\bar{k}[t]}^{n}\matheur{M}^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\bigotimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}^{n}\matheur{M}^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\bigwedge^{n}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}^{\prime}=\matheur{M}

ensures the purity of
\matheur{M}

by
[
HaJu20
, Proposition 2.4.10 (c)]
.
Therefore Proposition
4.3.4
implies that
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Res_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Xi^{\prime}))
.

On the other hand,
write
\Inf_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\xi)=\xi_{1}^{\prime}+\cdots+\xi_{n}^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}_{\xi_{i}^{\prime}}
be the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}
corresponding to
\xi_{i}^{\prime}
for
i=1,...,n
, and
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
be the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
corresponding to
\xi
.
One has
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\cdot O_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}_{\xi_{i}^{\prime}}
,
and the quotient map
\matheur{M}^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}/\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}^{\prime}
induces

\frac{\matheur{M}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}}\cong\bigwedge^{n}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\frac{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}^{\prime}}\cong\bigwedge^{n}_{\bar{k}}\frac{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}^{\prime}}.

For
1\leq i\leq n
, let
\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{i}^{\prime}}\in H_{\rm dR}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\bar{k})
be the differential of
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
associated with
\xi_{i}^{\prime}
.
Then
\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{1}^{\prime}},...,\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{n}^{\prime}}
form a
\bar{k}
-basis of
\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime}/\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}^{\prime},\bar{k})
, which implies that

\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{n}^{\prime}}:\bigwedge^{n}_{\bar{k}}\frac{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\bar{k}

can be viewed as a differential of
\matheur{M}
associated with
\xi
.

Finally,
put
\mathbb{O}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\dagger}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}O_{\mathbf{K}}
.
Observe that

\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\bigwedge^{n}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}^{\prime}\cong\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathbb{O}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\dagger}}(\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}^{\prime}),

whence we may regard
\bigwedge\limits^{n}_{O{K}}H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
as an
O{K}
-submodule of
H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})
.
As
H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
is a projective
O_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}
-module of rank one (i.e. a projective
O{K}
-module of rank
n
),
we can find
\gamma_{1}^{\prime},...,\gamma_{n}^{\prime}\in H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
so that

0\neq\gamma\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\gamma_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\cdots\wedge\gamma_{n}^{\prime}\in\bigwedge^{n}_{O{K}}H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})\subseteq H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M}).

Since
\int_{\gamma_{i}^{\prime}}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{j}^{\prime}}\sim p_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}(\xi_{j}^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})
for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
, it follows that

\displaystyle p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi,\Res_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Xi^{\prime}))\sim\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}

\displaystyle=\det\big(\int_{\gamma_{i}^{\prime}}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{j}^{\prime}}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}

\displaystyle\sim\prod_{j=1}^{n}p_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}(\xi_{j}^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})\sim p_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}(\Inf_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\xi),\Xi^{\prime}),

as desired.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.3.2
(4).

Taking (1) above and Proposition
3.3.4
into account, we may assume that
\Phi=\xi\in J{K}
and
\Phi^{0}=\Xi
is a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Note that
\varrho
induces a
\bar{k}(t)
-isomorphism (still denoted by
\varrho
) from
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\matheur{K}^{\prime}
to
\mathbf{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\matheur{K}
.
Let
\matheur{M}=\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with the generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
Let
\pi_{\varrho}:\mathbf{X}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
and
{\pi_{\varrho}}_{*}:\Div(\mathbf{X})\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\Div(\mathbf{X}^{\prime})
be the isomorphisms induced by
\varrho
.
Then

\varrho^{-1}\Bigl(\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}\Bigr)=\matheur{M}_{({\pi_{\varrho}}_{*}W,\varrho^{-1}(h))}=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}\matheur{M}^{\prime}

is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K}^{\prime},{\pi_{\varrho}}_{*}\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
In other words,
f\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\varrho|_{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}:\matheur{M}^{\prime}\rightarrow\matheur{M}
is an isomorphism of dual
t
-motives.
Moreover, let
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
be the differential in
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
associated with
\xi
.
Then
f^{*}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
is the differential in
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\bar{k})
associated with
\xi^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\varrho}(\xi)
.
For a nonzero cycle
\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
, we obtain that

p_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\bigl(\pi_{\varrho}(\xi),{\pi_{\varrho}}_{*}\Xi\bigr)\sim\int_{\gamma}f^{*}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\sim\int_{f_{*}\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\sim p{K}(\xi,\Xi),

whence proving (4).
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.3.2
(5).

Since

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}\xi=\Inf_{\matheur{K}/\matheur{K}^{+}}(\xi^{+})\quad\text{ and }\quad\Res_{\matheur{K}/\matheur{K}^{+}}(\Phi^{0})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{wt}(\Phi^{0})\sum_{\xi_{0}^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\xi_{0}^{+}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{wt}(\Phi^{0})\Inf_{\matheur{K}^{+}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\xi_{\theta}),

we get

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K},~\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}p{K}(\xi,\Phi^{0})

\displaystyle\sim p{K}\big(\Inf_{\matheur{K}/\matheur{K}^{+}}(\xi^{+}),\Phi^{0}\big)

\displaystyle\sim p_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\big(\xi^{+},\Res_{\matheur{K}/\matheur{K}^{+}}(\Phi^{0})\big)\hskip 73.97733pt\text{(by (3))}

\displaystyle\sim p_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\big(\xi^{+},\Inf_{\matheur{K}^{+}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\xi_{\theta})\big)^{\mathrm{wt}(\Phi^{0})}

\displaystyle\sim p_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\big(\Res_{\matheur{K}^{+}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\xi^{+}),\xi_{\theta}\big)^{\mathrm{wt}(\Phi^{0})}\hskip 28.45274pt\text{(by (4))}

\displaystyle\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\mathrm{wt}(\Phi^{0})}\hskip 76.82234pt\text{(by Proposition~\ref{prop: carlitz-ps}).}

∎

5.3.3.
Extension of period symbols.

We extend
\mathcal{P}{K}
to a pairing on
I{K}\times I{K}
as follows:
for
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in J{K}
, we let

\Phi^{0}_{2}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot\xi_{2}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{2})\end{subarray}}\xi\in I{K}^{0},

and define

(5.3.4)

\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\tilde{\pi}^{1/[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]}\cdot\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi_{1},\Phi_{2}^{0})^{1/[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times},

Extending
\mathcal{P}{K}
bi-additively to
I{K}\times I{K}
, we obtain an analogue of Shimura’s period symbols for each CM field
\matheur{K}
satisfying the properties (1)–(5) in Theorem
5.3.2
.
For every
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}\in I{K}
, we also keep using the notation
p{K}(\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2})
for an arbitrary representative of the coset
\mathcal{P}{K}(\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2})
.

6.
Algebraic relations among period symbols

In this section, we determine all the algebraic relations among the period symbols defined in (
5.3.4
), and as such prove our analogue of Shimura’s conjecture in this setting.

6.1.
Analogue of Shimura’s conjecture

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
. We start with the following “Legendre relation.”

Lemma 6.1.1
.

Fix any
\xi_{0}\in J_{\matheur{K}}
. For each
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
, we have

(6.1.2)

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\sim\tilde{\pi}^{1/[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]}.

Proof.

This relation follows directly from the definition in (
5.3.4
) and Theorem
5.3.2
(5).
∎

The main theorem of this paper is the following analogue of Shimura’s conjecture (the proof will be given in Section
6.2
).

Theorem 6.1.3
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\matheur{K}^{+}
its maximal totally real subfield. For each
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
, all algebraic relations among the period symbols
p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})
for
\xi\in J{K}
are generated by (
6.1.2
). Equivalently, we have

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K})=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

As a consequence, Theorem
6.1.3
leads to the following result that matches with the spirit of Conjectures
1.1.1
and
1.1.2
.

Corollary 6.1.4
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.

(a)

Suppose that
\matheur{K}\neq\matheur{K}^{+}
, and
let
\Xi=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{r}
be a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Given
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
, the period symbols

p{K}(\xi_{1},\xi_{0}),\ldots,p{K}(\xi_{r},\xi_{0})

are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
.

(b)

Suppose that
\matheur{K}
is Galois over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
. Then

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}(\tilde{\pi},p{K}(\xi_{0},\xi)\mid\xi\in J{K})=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

Proof.

(a) follows directly from Theorem
6.1.3
.
To prove (b),
the assumption says in particular that every
\xi
must be of the form
\xi_{0}^{\varrho}
for a unique
\varrho\in\Gal(\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t))
.
By Theorem
5.3.2
(4) one has that

p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\sim p{K}(\xi_{0}^{\varrho},\xi_{0})\sim p{K}(\xi_{0},\xi_{0}^{\varrho^{-1}}),

whence

\displaystyle\bar{k}(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K})

\displaystyle=\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi_{0}^{\varrho},\xi_{0})\mid\varrho\in\Gal(\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t))\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi_{0},\xi_{0}^{\varrho})\mid\varrho\in\Gal(\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t))\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bar{k}(p{K}(\xi_{0},\xi)\mid\xi\in J{K}).

Therefore the result follows by Theorem
6.1.3
.
∎

6.2.
Proof of the main theorem

To derive Theorem
6.1.3
, we remark that the Legendre relation in Lemma
6.1.1
assures the one-sided inequality:

\displaystyle\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}(\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K})

\displaystyle\leq 1+\left([\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]-[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]\right)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

For the opposite inequality,
we introduce the notion of “non-degeneracy” of a generalized CM type as follows.

Definition 6.2.1
.

Let
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field, and let
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
. We denote by
I_{\Xi}^{0}
the subgroup of
I{K}^{0}
generated by
\varsigma(\Xi)
for all
\varsigma\in\Gal(k^{\rm sep}/k)
. We say that
\Xi
is
non-degenerate
if

\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi}^{0}=\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}I{K}^{0}.

Remark 6.2.2
.

For a non-degenerate generalized CM type
\Xi
, one has that

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}(I_{\Xi}^{0})=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}(I{K}^{0})=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

Given
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
, we put

(6.2.3)

\Xi_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\xi_{0}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)\neq\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{0})\end{subarray}}\xi\quad\in I{K}^{0},

and derive the following lemma.

Lemma 6.2.4
.

The generalized CM type
\Xi_{0}
of
\matheur{K}
defined in (
6.2.3
) is non-degenerate,
and we have

\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)\subseteq\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr).

Proof.

Let

\Phi_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\xi_{0}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{0})\end{subarray}}\xi=\Xi_{0}-N{K}\quad\in I{K}^{0},

where
N{K}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}\xi
.
Then from the definition of
p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})
one obtains

p{K}(\xi,\Xi_{0})\sim\tilde{\pi}\cdot p{K}(\xi,\Phi_{0})\sim\tilde{\pi}^{1-1/[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]}\cdot p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})^{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}.

Together with the Legendre relation in (
6.1.2
), we have that

\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)\subseteq\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\xi_{0})\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr).

To show the non-degeneracy of
\Xi_{0}
, let
G_{k}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gal(k^{\rm sep}/k)
.
Set

K_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu_{\xi_{0}}(\matheur{K})\subset k^{\mathrm{sep}}\quad\text{ and }\quad H_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gal(k^{\mathrm{sep}}/K_{0})\subset G_{k}.

For each
\xi\in J{K}
,
there exists a unique coset
\varsigma_{\xi}H_{0}\in G_{k}
such that
\nu_{\xi}=\varsigma_{\xi}\circ\nu_{\xi_{0}}
, which says that
\varsigma_{\xi}(\xi_{0})=\xi
.
In particular, given
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
, every
\varsigma\in G_{k}
sends

\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi^{+})
bijectively to

\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\varsigma(\xi^{+}))
.
Observe that

\sum_{\varsigma\in G_{k}/H_{0}}\varsigma(\Xi_{0})=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]\cdot N{K},

i.e.,

N{K}=\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]}\otimes\sum_{\varsigma\in G_{k}/H_{0}}\varsigma(\Xi_{0})\quad\in\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi}^{0}.

Moreover, for each CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
,
write
\Xi=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{d}
, where
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
. We let
\xi_{i}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{i})
for
i=1,\dots,d
, and take

\varsigma_{i}\in G_{k}
so that
\varsigma_{i}(\xi_{0})=\xi_{i}
.
Then

\varsigma_{i}(\Xi_{0})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\xi_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)\neq\xi_{i}^{+}\end{subarray}}\xi,

which implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\varsigma_{i}(\Xi_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\xi_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)\neq\xi_{i}^{+}\end{subarray}}\xi\right)

\displaystyle=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\Xi+\bigl([\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]-1\bigr)N{K}.

Hence
\Xi\in\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi_{0}}^{0}
.
Finally, since
I{K}^{0}
is generated by all CM types of
\matheur{K}
,
we obtain the desired identity

\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi_{0}}^{0}=\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}I{K}^{0}.\qed

Finally, by Lemma
6.2.4
and Remark
6.2.2
, the proof of Theorem
6.1.3
is accomplished by the following key result.

Theorem 6.2.5
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
For each generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
, we have

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}(p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\mid\xi\in J{K})\geq\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi}^{0}.

Remark 6.2.6
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Suppose that
\Xi
is non-degenerate.
Then Lemma
6.1.1
and Theorem
6.2.5
ensure that

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\bigm|\xi\in J{K}\bigr)=1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)],

and that all algebraic relations among period symbols come from the Legendre relation,

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\text{\rm wt}(\Xi)},\quad\forall\,\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

In order to get the lower bound in Theorem
6.2.5
, we need the following explicit connection between period symbols and the periods of CM dual
t
-motives:

Proposition 6.2.7
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
and put
r\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
.
Let
\Upphi\in\Mat_{r}(\bar{k}[t])
represent multiplication by
\sigma
on a
\bar{k}[t]
-basis
\left\{m_{1},\ldots,m_{r}\right\}
of
\matheur{M}
, and
\Uppsi\in\Mat_{r}(\mathbb{T}^{\dagger})\cap\GL_{r}(\mathbb{T})
be a rigid analytic trivialization for
\Upphi
. Then we have

\bar{k}\bigl(p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi,\Xi)|\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}\bigr)=\bar{k}\bigl(\Uppsi^{-1}(\theta)\bigr)=\bar{k}\bigl(\Uppsi(\theta)\bigr).

Proof.

For each
\xi\in J{K}
, let
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
be the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
associated to
\xi
.
We then have the isomorphism

\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}\cong\bigoplus_{\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}}\matheur{M}/\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}.

As
\matheur{M}
is a projective
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module of rank one,
\matheur{M}/\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}
is one dimensional over
\bar{k}
. Since the nontrivial differential
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
given in Proposition
5.2.1
factors through the quotient
\matheur{M}/\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\matheur{M}
,
the differentials

\left\{\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\mid\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}\right\}

form a
\bar{k}
-basis of
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
. On the other hand, denote by
\bar{m}_{i}
the image of
m_{i}
in
\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}
for
i=1,\ldots,r
. For each
1\leq j\leq r
, we define the
\bar{k}
-linear functional
\omega_{j}:\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}\rightarrow\bar{k}
satisfying
\omega_{j}(\bar{m}_{i})=1
if
i=j
, and
0
otherwise.
It follows that

\left\{\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r}\right\}

forms another
\bar{k}
-basis of
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
.

Take an arbitrary nonzero
\gamma_{0}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
. We have

(6.2.8)

\displaystyle\bar{k}\bigl(p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi,\Xi)\mid\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bar{k}\left(\int_{\gamma_{0}}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\ \bigg|\ \xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bar{k}\bigl(\omega_{i}(\gamma)\mid 1\leq i\leq r,\ \gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\bigr).

Note that the last equality holds as
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
is a projective
O{K}
-module of rank one.

Put
{\mathbf{m}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(\matheur{M})
.
Since the entries of
\Psi^{-1}\mathbf{m}
form an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-basis of the Betti module
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
, the value of
\omega_{j}
specialized at the
i
-th component of
\Psi^{-1}\mathbf{m}
is given by

\omega_{\bar{m}_{j}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{r}(\Psi^{-1})_{ij}m_{j}\right)=(\Psi^{-1})_{ij}(\theta),

whence

\bar{k}\bigl(\omega_{i}(\gamma)\mid 1\leq i\leq r,\ \gamma\in H_{\rm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\bigr)=\bar{k}(\Psi^{-1}(\theta))

and the desired identity follows from (
6.2.8
).
∎

Based on Proposition
6.2.7
and Theorem
2.5.3
, showing Theorem
6.2.5
is then translated into finding a lower bound of the dimension of the corresponding
t
-motivic Galois group in question.
We shall use Hodge-Pink theory in the next section and prove Theorem
6.2.5
at the end of Section
7.4
.

7.
Hodge-Pink theory of dual
t
-motives

In order to apply Hodge-Pink theory, we first review the properties of the categories of Hartl and Juschka that we need.
Then we describe explicitly the Hodge-Pink filtration arising from a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
, which enables us to describe the Hodge-Pink cocharacters in question.
This characterization plays a crucial role in giving the precise connection between the lower bound in Theorem
6.2.5
on the one hand and the rank of the Galois module generated by
\Xi
in
I_{\matheur{K}}^{0}
on the other.

7.1.
Hartl-Juschka Categories

We recall the needed properties of the Hartl-Juschka Category
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}
introduced in
[
HaJu20
, Definition 2.4.1]
as follows:
an object of
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}
is a free
\bar{k}[t]
-module
\matheur{M}
of finite rank together with a
\sigma
-action on
M\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
satisfying that

(1)

M
is a pre-
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
;

(2)

there exists a sufficiently large integer
n
so that

(t-\theta)^{n}\matheur{M}\subseteq\sigma\matheur{M}\subseteq(t-\theta)^{-n}\matheur{M}.

A morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}
is a
\bar{k}[t]
-module homomorphism so that the induced
\bar{k}(t)
-linear homomorphism
f:M=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}\rightarrow M^{\prime}=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}^{\prime}
satisfies

f(\sigma m)=\sigma f(m),\quad\forall\,m\in M.

Given two objects
\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}
, the set
\Hom(\matheur{M},\matheur{M}^{\prime})
of morphisms from
\matheur{M}
to
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
has a natural
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module structure, and elements in

\Hom^{0}(\matheur{M},\matheur{M}^{\prime})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}\Hom(\matheur{M},\matheur{M}^{\prime})

are called
quasi-morphisms from
\matheur{M}
to
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
.
The
Hartl-Juschka Category
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}
up to isogeny
has the same objects in
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}
and the morphisms in
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}
are given by quasi-morphisms.
In particular, the functor
\matheur{M}\mapsto\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
from
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}
to the category of pre-
t
-motives is faithful.

Remark 7.1.1
.

An object
\matheur{M}\in\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}
is called
effective
if
\sigma\matheur{M}\subseteq\matheur{M}
.
We note that a dual
t
-motive is an effective object
\matheur{M}
of
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}
, which is also finitely generated over
\bar{k}[\sigma]
.
Thus the category of dual
t
-motives (resp. up to isogeny) is a strictly full subcategory of
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}
).

We let
\mathbb{T}[\sigma]
be the non-commutative algebra generated by
\sigma
over
\mathbb{T}
subject to the relation

\sigma f=f^{(-1)}\sigma,\quad\forall\,f\in\mathbb{T}.

Given an object
\matheur{M}\in\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}
, we set

\mathbb{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
, on which
\sigma
acts diagonally, and so
\mathbb{M}
becomes a left
\mathbb{T}[\sigma]
-module (as
t-\theta
is invertible in
\mathbb{T}
).
Put

H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{m\in\mathbb{M}\mid\sigma m=m\}.

We call
\matheur{M}

uniformizable
(or
rigid analytically trivial
) if the natural homomorphism

\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}

is an isomorphism (as
\mathbb{T}
-modules). It is known that (see
[
HaJu20
, Proposition 2.4.27]
) if
\matheur{M}
is uniformizable, then
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
is contained in
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
, and the natural homomorphism

\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}

is an isomorphism (as
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
-modules).

7.1.2.
t
-motivic Galois groups.

The full subcategory of
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}
consisting of all uniformizable objects is denoted by
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}_{U}
.
By
[
HaJu20
, Remark 2.4.15]
, the functor
\matheur{M}\mapsto\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
from
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}_{U}
to the category
\mathcal{R}
of uniformizable pre-
t
-motives is fully faithful.
Now we let
\mathbf{Vect}_{/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}
be the category of finite dimensional vector spaces over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and note that by
[
HaJu20
, Theorem 2.4.23]

\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}_{U}
is a neutral Tannakian category over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with fiber functor
\boldsymbol{\omega}:\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}_{U}\rightarrow\mathbf{Vect}_{/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}
defined by:

\matheur{M}\longmapsto H{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}).

Given a uniformizable dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
, let
M\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
.
Viewing
