8.5359pt(\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.25145pt\raise-22.04857pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.20831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\rm GV}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.03404pt\raise-24.31944pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 212.79019pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.4156pt\hbox{\xyRotate@@{3072}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 231.08789pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hskip-8.5359pt\mathbf{Rep}(\mathbb{G}_{m/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}))}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 49.9479pt\raise-31.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{Vect}}_{/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 160.56305pt\raise-31.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

By the arguments of
[
DMOS82
, p. 130, Cor. 2.9]
we obtain a cocharacter of
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
(induced from
\mathcal{F}
),

\chi_{\matheur{M}}^{\vee}:\mathbb{G}_{m/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}={\rm{Aut}}^{\otimes}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\rm GV})\rightarrow{\rm{Aut}}^{\otimes}(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})=\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\hookrightarrow\GL(H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}).

Note that the image of
\chi_{\matheur{M}}^{\vee}
is uniquely determined by the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
on

H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Gr}^{i}(H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}),

i.e., for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x\in\mathbb{G}_{m/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
we have

(7.3.2)

\chi_{\matheur{M}}^{\vee}(x)v=x^{i}\cdot v,\quad\forall v\in\text{Gr}^{i}(H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}).

Since
\GL(H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})
(resp.
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
) is the base change
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\GL(H_{\matheur{M}})
(resp.
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
), we have a natural action of
\Aut_{k}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
on
\GL(H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})
, which leaves
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
invariant. Thus any
\Aut_{k}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
-conjugate of
\chi{M}^{\vee}
is still a cocharacter of
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
.

Definition 7.3.3
.

(cf.
[
Pink97
, Definition 7.10]
) Given an object
\matheur{M}
in
\mathcal{H}\mathcal{J}^{I}_{U}
, a
Hodge-Pink cocharacter
of
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
is a cocharacter which lies in the
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\rtimes\Aut_{k}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
-conjugate class of
\chi{M}^{\vee}
.

We shall describe the images of the Hodge-Pink cocharacters of
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
in
\GL(H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})
via (
7.3.2
) when
\matheur{M}
is a CM dual
t
-motive, and provide a lower bound for the dimension of
\Gamma_{\matheur{M}}
in the next subsection.

7.4.
The case of CM dual
t
-motives

Let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
, where
\matheur{K}
is a CM field with
[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]=r
.
For
\xi\in J{K}
, let
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
be the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
associated to
\xi
, i.e.
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
is generated by
\alpha-\nu_{\xi}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in O{K}
.
Write
\Xi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi
and put
\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}}
.
As
\matheur{M}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}
and
\sigma\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\matheur{M}
(see the equality (
B.2.6
)), we have that

\frac{\matheur{M}}{\sigma\matheur{M}}\cong\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}}}.

Since
(t-\theta)O_{\mathbf{K}}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
, i.e., the prime ideal
(t-\theta)\bar{k}[t]
of
\bar{k}[t]
splits completely in
O_{\mathbf{K}}
,
for each
\xi\in J{K}
one has that

\bar{k}[t]\cap\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}}=(t-\theta)^{m_{\xi}}\cdot\bar{k}[t]\quad\text{ and }\quad\dim_{\bar{k}}(\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}}})=m_{\xi}.

Thus the natural inclusion
\bar{k}[t]\hookrightarrow O_{\mathbf{K}}

induces the following isomorphism (as
\bar{k}[t]
-modules)

\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}}}\cong\frac{\bar{k}[t]}{(t-\theta)^{m_{\xi}}\bar{k}[t]}\quad\textup{ for every $\xi\in J{K}$.}

To conclude, we obtain the following description of Hodge-Pink weights of
\matheur{M}
.

Proposition 7.4.1
.

Let
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field and
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
. Write
\Xi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi\in I{K}^{0}
.
Then

\frac{\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}}{\sigma\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\frac{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}{(t-\theta)^{m_{\xi}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}.

Consequently, the Hodge-Pink weights of
\matheur{M}
are

(-m_{\xi})_{\xi\in J{K}}
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
,
and let
M=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
be the pre-
t
-motive associated to
\matheur{M}
. Since
O_{\matheur{K}}\subset\End_{\bar{k}[t,\sigma]}(\matheur{M})
,
we can embed
\matheur{K}
into the endomorphism ring
\End_{\mathcal{T}}(M)
.
From (
7.3.1
), the closed immersion
\Gamma{M}\hookrightarrow\GL_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(H{M})
actually factors through
T{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}={\rm Res}_{\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the Weil restriction of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
from
\matheur{K}
to
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
Thus the base change
\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
can be viewed as a subgroup of

T_{\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}{\times}{\rm Res}_{\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\mathbb{G}_{m}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{\nu_{\xi},\matheur{K}}{\times}\mathbb{G}_{m/\matheur{K}}.

Moreover, for each
\xi\in J{K}
, let
H_{\xi}
be the unique one-dimensional subspace of
H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
satisfying that

\alpha\cdot v=\nu_{\xi}(\alpha)\cdot v,\quad\forall\,\alpha\in O{K},\ v\in H_{\xi}.

The decomposition

H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}=\bigoplus_{\xi\in J{K}}H_{\xi}

gives the embedding

\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{\nu_{\xi},\matheur{K}}{\times}\mathbb{G}_{m/\matheur{K}}\hookrightarrow\GL_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}),\quad(x_{\xi})_{\xi\in J{K}}\longmapsto\begin{pmatrix}\ddots&&\\
&x_{\xi}&\\
&&\ddots\end{pmatrix}.

Write
\Xi=\sum_{\xi}m_{\xi}\xi\in I{K}^{0}
. Then for
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

{\Gr}^{i}(H_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
-m_{\xi}=i\end{subarray}}H_{\xi}\quad\textup{(see~\eqref{eqn: Grading}).}

Therefore by (
7.3.2
) the cocharacter

\chi{M}^{\vee}:\mathbb{G}_{m/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\subset T_{\matheur{K}}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{\nu_{\xi},\matheur{K}}{\times}\mathbb{G}_{m/\matheur{K}}

is realized as

\chi{M}^{\vee}(x)=(x^{-m_{\xi}})_{\xi\in J{K}}\quad\in\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{\nu_{\xi},\matheur{K}}{\times}\mathbb{G}_{m/\matheur{K}}.

On the other hand, for each cocharacter
\chi^{\vee}:\mathbb{G}_{m/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\rightarrow T_{\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
,
there exists a unique set of integers
\{w_{\xi}:\xi\in J{K}\}
(i.e., the coweights of
\chi^{\vee}
) so that

\chi^{\vee}(x)=(x^{w_{\xi}})_{\xi\in J{K}}\quad\in\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{\nu_{\xi},\matheur{K}}{\times}\mathbb{G}_{m/\matheur{K}}.

We remark that the map

\chi^{\vee}\longmapsto\Phi_{\chi^{\vee}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}(-w_{\xi})\xi\in I{K}

gives an isomorphism between the group of cocharacters of
T_{\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
and
I{K}
, which is equivariant under the action of
\Aut_{k}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
, i.e.

\Phi_{({}^{\varsigma}\chi^{\vee})}=\varsigma(\Phi_{\chi^{\vee}}),\quad\forall\,\chi^{\vee}:\mathbb{G}_{m/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\rightarrow T_{\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\ \text{ and }\ \varsigma\in\Aut_{k}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

In particular, we have

(7.4.2)

\Phi_{\chi^{\vee}{M}}=\Xi\in I{K}^{0}.

As
\Gamma{M}
is commutative, we obtain the following correspondence.

Proposition 7.4.3
.

Under the above identification between the group of cocharacters of
T_{\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
and
I{K}
, the set of the Hodge-Pink cocharacters of
\Gamma{M}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
corresponds bijectively to

\{\varsigma(\Xi):\varsigma\in\Aut_{k}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\}.

Remark 7.4.4
.

(1) Since every point
\xi\in J{K}
given in (
3.3.1
) is defined over
k^{\text{sep}}
(as
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
is separable),
one has

\{\varsigma(\Xi):\varsigma\in\Aut_{k}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\}=\{\varsigma(\Xi):\varsigma\in\Gal(k^{\text{sep}}/k)\}.

(2) Let

I^{0}_{\Xi}
be the subgroup of
I{K}^{0}
generated by
\varsigma(\Xi)
for all
\varsigma\in\Gal(k^{\text{sep}}/k)
,
and let

T{M}
be the subgroup of
\Gamma{M}
generated by
the image of all the Hodge-Pink cocharacters of
\Gamma{M}
.
Then

\dim\Gamma{M}\geq\dim T{M}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I^{0}_{\Xi}.

Proof of Theorem
6.2.5
.

Given a non-degenerate CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
, let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
From Proposition
6.2.7
and Theorem
2.5.3
, we obtain that

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\mid\xi\in J{K}\bigr)=\dim\Gamma{M}\geq\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi}^{0}

as desired.
∎

8.
Application to periods of abelian
t
-modules

Let
E_{\rho}
be an abelian
t
-module over
\bar{k}
and
\matheur{M}(\rho)
be the Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motive associated with
E_{\rho}
(see Remark
2.3.3
).
Based on Remark
4.3.3
, we say that
E_{\rho}
is
CM with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
if
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
The aim of this section is to apply Theorem
6.2.5
to prove the algebraic independence of the coordinates of a nonzero period vector of
E_{\rho}
when
E_{\rho}
has a non-degenerate CM type.
To do so, we first establish the needed comparisons for the Lie spaces, Betti modules and de Rham modules in terms of
t
-motives in the following subsections.

8.1.
Comparisons between Lie spaces

Let
E_{\rho}
be an abelian
t
-module over
\bar{k}
, and let
\mathscr{M}(\rho)
be the
\bar{k}[t,\tau]
-module defined in Definition
2.1.1
(2).
There is a canonical
\bar{k}
-vector space isomorphism (see
[
And86
, Lemma 1.3.4]
)

(8.1.1)

\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\mathscr{M}(\rho)/\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho),\bar{k}).

On the other hand, as
(t-\theta)^{N}\matheur{M}(\rho)\subset\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)
for a sufficiently large integer
N
, we may identify
\matheur{M}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho),\bar{k}[t]dt)
with

\biggl\{\matheur{m}\in\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}\Bigl(\mathscr{M}(\rho),\bar{k}[t][\frac{1}{t-\theta}]dt\Bigr)\biggm|\matheur{m}(\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho))\subset\bar{k}[t]dt\biggr\},

and
\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)
corresponds to
\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\mathscr{M}(\rho),\bar{k}[t]dt)
.
Thus we obtain a
\bar{k}
-bilinear map

\mathscr{M}(\rho)\times\matheur{M}(\rho)\longrightarrow\bar{k}[t][\frac{1}{t-\theta}]dt\xrightarrow{\Res_{t=\theta}}\bar{k},

where
(m,\matheur{m})\mapsto\Res_{t=\theta}\matheur{m}(m)
for every
m\in\mathscr{M}(\rho)
and
\matheur{m}\in\matheur{M}(\rho)
.
This induces a perfect pairing

(8.1.2)

\frac{\mathscr{M}(\rho)}{\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)}\times\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)}\longrightarrow\bar{k}.

Lemma 8.1.3
.

The above perfect pairing and (
8.1.1
) lead to an isomorphism of
\bar{k}
-vector spaces,

\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\matheur{M}(\rho)/\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho).

Remark 8.1.4
.

Viewing
\Lie(E_{\rho})
as a
\bar{k}[t]\ (=\!\!\bar{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\mathbb{F}_{q}[t])
-module via
\text{id}_{\bar{k}}\otimes\partial\rho
, the above isomorphism is actually
\bar{k}[t]
-linear.

8.2.
Comparisons between period lattices and Betti modules

Suppose
E_{\rho}
is a uniformizable abelian
t
-module over
\bar{k}
, i.e. the corresponding exponential map
\exp_{\rho}:\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\rightarrow E_{\rho}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
is surjective.
Let
\Lambda_{\rho}
be the period lattice of
E_{\rho}
, i.e.

\Lambda_{\rho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{\lambda\in\Lie(E)(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\mid\exp_{\rho}(\lambda)=0\},

which is a discrete
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-submodule with

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}(\Lambda_{\rho})=\operatorname{rank}_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho).

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
, let
\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}:\mathscr{M}(\rho)\rightarrow\mathbb{T}
be the following
Anderson generating function

(8.2.1)

\langle m\mid\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\rangle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}m\Bigl(\exp_{\rho}(\partial\rho_{t}^{-n-1}\lambda)\Bigr)t^{n},\quad\forall\,m\in\mathscr{M}(\rho).

Following
[
NP21
]
, we take

\gamma_{\lambda}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\big|_{\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)}dt\in\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho),\mathbb{T}\,dt)\cong\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho).

It is straightforward to check that
\sigma\cdot\gamma_{\lambda}=\gamma_{\lambda}
, which means that

\gamma_{\lambda}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))
.

Lemma 8.2.2
.

The map
(\lambda\longmapsto\gamma_{\lambda})
gives an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module isomorphism from
\Lambda_{\rho}
onto
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))
.

Proof.

Note that
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}\Lambda_{\rho}=\operatorname{rank}_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho)=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))
. Let
\{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{r}\}
be an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-base of
\Lambda_{\rho}
and
\{\tau m_{1},\ldots,\tau m_{r}\}
be a
\bar{k}[t]
-base of
\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)
.
The result follows immediately from the fact (cf.
[
NP21
, Proposition 4.3.10]
, see also
[
GP19
, Proposition 6.2.4]
and
[
P08
, Proposition 3.3.9]
) that

(\langle\tau m_{i}\mid\mathcal{G}_{\lambda_{j}}\rangle)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\in\GL_{r}(\mathbb{T}).\qed

Recall that
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
is the ring of rigid analytic functions on
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus\{\theta^{q^{i}}\mid i\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
As
E_{\rho}
is uniformizable, so is
\matheur{M}(\rho)
.
Thus by
[
HaJu20
, Prop. 2.3.30]
we have that

H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho)\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho).

Let
\bar{\gamma}_{\lambda}
be the image of
\gamma_{\lambda}
in
\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\mathscr{M}(\rho)/\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho),\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
under the following maps

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\bar{k}}\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\frac{\mathscr{M}(\rho)}{\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}),

where the last isomorphism comes from (
8.1.2
). We obtain

\bar{\gamma}_{\lambda}(m)=-\Res_{t=\theta}\Bigl(\langle m|\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\rangle dt\Bigr),

which coincides with the image of
\lambda
in
\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\mathscr{M}(\rho)/\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho),\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
under the isomorphism (
8.1.1
) by
[
And86
, Lemma 1.3.4 and Proposition 3.3.2]
.
In conclusion we obtain the following.

Proposition 8.2.3
.

The following diagram commutes:

\Lie(E)(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})

\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}

\displaystyle\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\frac{\mathscr{M}(\rho)}{\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})

\mathrel{{\mathop{\longleftarrow}\limits}}

\hskip-8.5359pt\displaystyle\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\bar{k}}\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)}

\cup

\uparrow

\cong

\Lambda_{\rho}

\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}

H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))

\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)}
.

\in

\in

\lambda

\longmapsto

\gamma_{\lambda}

8.3.
Comparisons between de Rham modules and pairings

Let
E_{\rho}
be an abelian
t
-module over
\bar{k}
.
The de Rham module of
E_{\rho}
was first introduced via “bi-derivations” in
[
Ge89
,
Yu90
,
BP02
]
. When
E_{\rho}
is uniformizable, the de Rham pairing of
E_{\rho}
was constructed using the Anderson generating function (cf.
[
BP02
]
, see also
[
NP21
, (4.3.3) and (4.3.4)]
). Here we shall identify canonically the de Rham modules of
E_{\rho}
with the one of the Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}(\rho)
associated with
E_{\rho}
, and make a natural comparison between their de Rham pairings.

Recall that the de Rham module of
E_{\rho}
is isomorphic to (see
[
BP02
, §3.1]
and
[
NP21
, Proposition 4.1.3]
)

(8.3.1)

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})\cong\frac{\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)}{(t-\theta)\cdot\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)}.

From the canonical isomorphism
\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)\cong\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\matheur{M}(\rho),\Omega_{\bar{k}[t]/\bar{k}})
,
we may identify
(t-\theta)\cdot\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)
with
\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\matheur{M}(\rho),(t-\theta)\Omega_{\bar{k}[t]/\bar{k}})
.
This induces the following isomorphism.

Lemma 8.3.2
.

In the setting above, we have the following identifications:

\hskip-11.38092ptH_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})\cong\frac{\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\matheur{M}(\rho),\Omega_{\bar{k}[t]/\bar{k}})}{\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\matheur{M}(\rho),(t-\theta)\Omega_{\bar{k}[t]/\bar{k}})}\cong\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}\left(\matheur{M}(\rho),\frac{\Omega_{\bar{k}[t]/\bar{k}}}{(t-\theta)\Omega_{\bar{k}[t]/\bar{k}}}\right)\\
\cong\Hom_{\bar{k}}\left(\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{(t-\theta)\matheur{M}(\rho)},\bar{k}\right)=H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}(\rho),\bar{k}).

Assume that
E_{\rho}
is uniformizable.
Let
\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
be the period lattice of
E_{\rho}
.
For each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
,
let
\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}:\mathscr{M}(\rho)\rightarrow\mathbb{T}
be the Anderson generating function in (
8.2.1
). Recall that the de Rham pairing of
E_{\rho}
is defined by (cf.
[
NP21
, (4.3.3) and (4.3.4)]
)

(8.3.3)

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})\times\Lambda_{\rho}

\longrightarrow

\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
,

(\delta,\lambda)

\longmapsto

[\delta,\lambda]\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle\tau m_{\delta}|\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\rangle\big|_{t=\theta}
,

where
\tau m_{\delta}\in\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho)
, whose image in
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})
is
\delta
via (
8.3.1
).
On the other hand, for each
\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})
and
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
,
let
\omega_{\delta}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}(\rho),\bar{k})
be the differential corresponding to
\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})
under the isomorphism in Lemma
8.3.2
, and
let
\gamma_{\lambda}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))
be the cycle
corresponding to
\lambda
under the isomorphism in Lemma
8.2.2
.
Identifying
\tau m_{\delta}
as an element of
\Hom_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}}(\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho),\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}dt)
,
we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\tau m_{\delta}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\,dt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\cdot)|_{t=\theta}}

This gives us the comparison,

\int_{\gamma_{\lambda}}\omega_{\delta}=\omega_{\delta}(\gamma_{\lambda})=\langle\tau m_{\delta}|-\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\rangle|_{t=\theta}=-\langle\tau m_{\delta}|\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\rangle\big|_{t=\theta}=-[\delta,\lambda],

from which we conclude the following.

Proposition 8.3.4
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a uniformizable abelian
t
-module over
\bar{k}
.
We have the following comparison between the de Rham pairings of
E_{\rho}
and
\matheur{M}(\rho)
:

\textstyle{[\ ,\ ]:}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

Lem.
8.3.2
\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{\Lambda_{\rho}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

Lem.
8.2.2
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\int:}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}(\rho),\bar{k})}
\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
\textstyle{-z}

8.4.
Quasi-periods of CM abelian
t
-modules

Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
By Theorem
4.3.7
, we know that
E_{\rho}
is always uniformizable.
Let
\Lambda_{\rho}
be the period lattice of
E_{\rho}
.
For each
\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})
and
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
, we call
[\delta,\lambda]
the
quasi-period with respect to
\delta
associated to
\lambda
(see
[
BP02
, p. 113]
).
From the natural comparison between the de Rham pairings of
E_{\rho}
and
\matheur{M}(\rho)
in Proposition
8.3.4
, we obtain the following:

Theorem 8.4.1
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
Then

(8.4.2)

\displaystyle 1+\frac{([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]

\displaystyle\geq\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\,\bar{k}\bigl([\delta,\lambda]\bigm|\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k}),\ \lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi}^{0}.

In particular, if the generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
is non-degenerate, then

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\bigl([\delta,\lambda]\bigm|\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k}),\ \lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\bigr)=1+\frac{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

Proof.

From Proposition
8.3.4
,
we have that

\bar{k}\bigl([\delta,\lambda]\bigm|\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k}),\ \lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\bigr)=\bar{k}\bigl(p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\bigm|\xi\in J{K}\bigr).

Hence (
8.4.2
) follows immediately from Lemma
6.1.1
and Theorem
6.2.5
.
When
\Xi
is non-degenerate, one obtains

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I_{\Xi}^{0}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I{K}^{0}=1+\frac{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]-1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 8.4.3
.

(1) Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with a generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
Suppose that
\Xi
is non-degenerate.
Then all of the algebraic relations among the quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
come from the following Legendre relation of the period symbols (cf. Remark
6.2.6
):

(8.4.4)

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}(\xi)=\xi^{+}}\end{subarray}}p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\text{\rm wt}(\Xi)},\quad\forall\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

(2) Let
E_{\rho}=(\mathbb{G}_{a/\bar{k}},\rho)
be a Drinfeld
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module of rank
r
over
\bar{k}
.
Suppose
E_{\rho}
has CM by
O{K}
, where
O{K}
is the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in a separable imaginary field
\matheur{K}
(see Remark
3.1.2
(2)) and
[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]=r
.
Extending
\rho
to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra homomorphism
\rho:O{K}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}_{a/\bar{k}})
, its derivation
\partial\rho:O{K}\rightarrow\Lie(\mathbb{G}_{a/\bar{k}})
induces an embedding
\nu_{\rho}:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Let
\matheur{M}(\rho)
be the Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motive associated to
E_{\rho}
. Then
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is a CM dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
with CM type
(\matheur{K},\xi_{\rho})
,
where
\xi_{\rho}\in J{K}
is the point corresponding to
\nu_{\rho}
.
As
\xi_{\rho}
is non-degenerate, Theorem
1.5.1
implies that

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\bigl([\delta,\lambda]\bigm|\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k}),\ \lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\bigr)=r.

This coincides with
[
CP12
, Theorem 1.2.2]
.

8.5.
Periods of CM Hilbert-Blumenthal
t
-modules

Now, let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field and
\Xi
be a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Suppose
E_{\rho}
is a CM abelian
t
-module with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
, and write
\Xi=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}
, where
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
.
For
1\leq i\leq d
, recall that
\mathfrak{P}_{i}
is the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
corresponding to
\xi_{i}
.
By Lemma
8.1.3
and the equality (
B.2.6
) in the appendix, we have the following identification:

(8.5.1)

\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)}=\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\matheur{M}(\rho)}\cong\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho)}{\mathfrak{P}_{i}\matheur{M}(\rho)}\cong\bar{k}^{d}.

Let
\xi_{i}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{i})
and
\nu_{i}^{+}=\nu_{\xi_{i}^{+}}\in\Emb(\matheur{K}^{+},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
.
With respect to (
8.5.1
), the multiplication of
O{K}^{+}
on
\matheur{M}(\rho)
induces a structure homomorphism (still denoted by)
\rho:O{K}^{+}\rightarrow\End_{\rm ab.\ t-mod.}(E_{\rho})
such that the induced
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-linear homomorphism
\partial\rho:O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty}))
is isomorphic to
\nu_{1}^{+}\oplus\cdots\oplus\nu_{d}^{+}
.
In other words,
E_{\rho}
is actually a Hilbert-Blumenthal
O{K}^{+}
-module (introduced in the beginning of Section
4.3
).

Conversely, let
E_{\rho}
be a Hilbert-Blumenthal
O{K}^{+}
-module satisfying the property that
\operatorname{rank}_{A}(\Lambda_{\rho})=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
, and suppose that the structure homomorphism
\rho
can be extended to a homomorphism (still denoted by
\rho
)
O{K}\rightarrow\End_{\rm ab.\ t-mod.}(E_{\rho})
.
Then
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is pure,
\operatorname{rank}_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho)=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
, and
\rho
induces an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-algebra embedding
O{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\bar{k}[t,\sigma]}(\matheur{M}(\rho))
.
Moreover, one checks that

\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho)=\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\matheur{M}(\rho),

where
\Xi
is the CM type of
\matheur{K}
fixed above. Therefore by Proposition
C.1.3
we have that
E_{\rho}
is a CM abelian
t
-module with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

In
[
Yu89
]
, Yu showed that for any Hilbert-Blumenthal
t
-module
E_{\rho}
defined over
\bar{k}
, every coordinate of a nonzero period vector of
E_{\rho}
is transcendental over
k
.
When
E_{\rho}
is in particular a CM abelian
t
-module with non-degenerate CM type,
we extend Yu’s result as follows.

Theorem 8.5.2
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module of dimension
d
with a non-degenerate CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
and suppose
\matheur{K}\neq\matheur{K}^{+}
.
Fix an identification
\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\bar{k}^{d}
, where
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
, and extend it to
\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
.
Given a nonzero period vector
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subseteq\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
, let
(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})
be the corresponding vector in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
. Then the coordinates
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d}
are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
.

Proof.

Let
\Xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{d}
be the given CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that the fixed identification
\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\bar{k}^{d}
coincides with (
8.5.1
).
Then for a nonzero period vector
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
with corresponding coordinate vector
(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{d})\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
,
we claim that

\lambda_{i}\sim p{K}(\xi_{i},\Xi),\quad i=1,\dots,d.

For
1\leq i\leq d
, let
\xi_{i}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{i})
.
Then
J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}=\{\xi_{1}^{+},\dots,\xi_{d}^{+}\}
, and the result follows from Remark
6.2.6
.

To prove the claim above, let
\matheur{M}=\matheur{M}(\rho)
.
Take the differential
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{i}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
associated with
\xi_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq d
, each of which extends to a nonzero
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-linear functional on
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\bar{k}}(\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M})
factoring through

\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\bar{k}}(\matheur{M}/\mathfrak{P}_{i}\matheur{M})
.
Let
\gamma_{\lambda}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
be the cycle corresponding to
\lambda
in Lemma
8.2.2
.
We use the commutative diagram in Proposition
8.2.3
together with the identification (
8.5.1
) extended to
\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
to conclude that

\lambda_{i}\sim\int_{\gamma_{\lambda}}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{i}}\sim p{K}(\xi_{i},\Xi),\quad 1\leq i\leq d,

as desired.
∎

Appendix A
Non-geometric CM fields and CM types

A.1.
Notation on curves

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
Suppose the constant field of
\matheur{K}
is
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
with
\ell>1
.
Then
\matheur{K}
is geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}(t)
.
Let
X
be the smooth, projective, geometrically connected algebraic curve defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
with function field
\matheur{K}
.
We call
X
the curve associated with
\matheur{K}
.
Fix an algebraically closed field
\mathbb{K}
with
k\subset\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Viewing
X
as a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, let
\mathbf{X}
be the base change of
X
from
\mathbb{F}_{q}
to
\mathbb{K}
, i.e.,

\mathbf{X}=\mathbb{K}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}X=\coprod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\mathbf{X}_{(i)},\quad\text{ where }\quad\mathbf{X}_{(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\underset{\operatorname{Frob}_{q}^{-i},\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}{\times}X,\quad i\in\mathbb{Z},

i.e. the base change of
X
from
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
to
\mathbb{K}
with respect to the Frobenius twist

\operatorname{Frob}_{q}^{-i}:\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{K},\quad\varepsilon\longmapsto\varepsilon^{1/q^{i}},\quad\forall\varepsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}.

In particular, we have
\mathbf{X}_{(i+\ell)}=\mathbf{X}_{(i)}
.
Moreover, let
\mathbf{K}_{(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X}_{(i)})
be the function field of
\mathbf{X}_{(i)}
.
The Frobenius twist
(\alpha\mapsto\alpha^{q^{-1}})
on the scalar field
\mathbb{K}
induces a field isomorphism
\mathbf{K}_{(i)}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\mathbf{K}_{(i+1)}
corresponding to the (geometric) Frobenius twist

\mathbf{X}_{(i+1)}\longrightarrow\mathbf{X}_{(i)},\quad x\longmapsto x^{(1)},\quad\forall x\in\mathbf{X}_{(i+1)},\ i\in\mathbb{Z}.

Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
(which is always geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
), and let
X^{+}
be the curve over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
with function field
\matheur{K}^{+}
as before.
The embeddings

\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}^{+}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}

induce the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphisms
\pi_{X/X^{+}}:X\rightarrow X^{+}
,
\pi_{X^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}:X^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and
\pi_{X/\mathbb{P}^{1}}:X\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}

so that

\pi_{X/\mathbb{P}^{1}}=\pi_{X^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}\circ\pi_{X/X^{+}}.

Let
\mathbf{X}^{+}
be the base change of
X^{+}
from
\mathbb{F}_{q}
to
\mathbb{K}
, and denote by
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
,
\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
, and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}
the base change of respective morphisms to
\mathbb{K}
.

A.2.
CM types

We continue in the setting above with a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
Denote by

J{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\theta)=\{\xi\in\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\mid\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi)=\theta\},

the set of points of
\mathbf{X}
lying above
\theta
.
Let
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
be the set of all
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra embeddings from
\matheur{K}
into
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
which send
t
to
\theta
.
As in the geometric case, we have a bijection between
J{K}
and

\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})

given as follows:
Take
\xi\in J{K}
, corresponding to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism

\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\rightarrow X.

As
\xi
is not
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-valued (where
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
is the algebraic closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
),
this morphism must factor through the generic point

\Spec(\matheur{K})\hookrightarrow X
.
Thus we obtain an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism
\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\rightarrow\Spec(\matheur{K})
, which corresponds to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra embedding
\nu_{\xi}:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
sending
t
to
\theta
(as
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi)=\theta
).
Conversely, for
\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
, the induced
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism

\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\Spec(\matheur{K})\hookrightarrow X

gives a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-valued point
\xi_{\nu}
of
\mathbf{X}
so that
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi_{\nu})=\theta
(as
\nu(t)=\theta
).
It is clear that

\xi_{\nu_{\xi}}=\xi,\quad\forall\xi\in J{K},\quad\text{ and }\quad\nu_{\xi_{\nu}}=\nu,\quad\forall\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

In particular, for
\xi\in J{K}
, one has that

\xi\in\mathbf{X}_{(i)}\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad\nu_{\xi}\big|_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}=\text{Frob}_{q}^{-i},\quad 0\leq i<\ell.

The generalized CM types of a given CM field are defined as follows.

Definition A.2.1
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with the maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
.
Let
I{K}
be the free abelian group generated by elements in
J{K}
.
Let
I{K}^{0}
be the subgroup of
I{K}
consisting of elements

\Phi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi,\quad m_{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z},

satisfying

\sum_{\xi\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi_{1}^{+})}m_{\xi}=\sum_{\xi^{\prime}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi_{2}^{+})}m_{\xi^{\prime}},\quad\forall\xi_{1}^{+},\xi_{2}^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

We put

\wt(\Phi)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi^{+})}m_{\xi}\quad\text{ for a }\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

A
generalized CM type
of
\matheur{K}
is a nonzero effective divisor in
I{K}^{0}
. A
CM type
of
\matheur{K}
is a generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
for which
\text{wt}(\Xi)=1
.

The following properties and definitions extend naturally to the case of non-geometric CM fields without difficulties.

•

I{K}^{0}
is generated by all the CM types of
\matheur{K}
(Proposition
3.3.4
).

•

I{K}^{0}
is invariant under the right action of
\Aut_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}(\matheur{K})
and the left action of
\Gal(k^{\rm sep}/k)
(Lemma
3.3.5
and Remark
3.3.6
).

•

The restriction and inflation maps on CM types (Section
3.4
).

•

The reduction points at infinity (Proposition
3.5.1
and Definition
3.5.4
).

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with the constant field
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
, and let
X
be the curve over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
associated with
\matheur{K}
.
Given a CM type
\Xi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi
of
\matheur{K}
, we may also define the
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-rational divisor
\matheur{I}_{\Xi}
as in Definition
3.5.4
.
Recall that the Frobenius twist
(\alpha\mapsto\alpha^{q^{-1}})
on the scalar field
\mathbb{K}
corresponds to the geometric Frobenius morphism on
\mathbf{X}=\coprod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\mathbf{X}_{(i)}
,
which maps
\mathbf{X}_{(i+1)}
to
\mathbf{X}_{(i)}
by sending
x
to
x^{(1)}
for every
x\in\mathbf{X}_{(i+1)}
.
Write

\Xi=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\Xi_{(i)},\quad\text{ where }\quad\Xi_{(i)}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}\cap\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}m_{\xi}\xi.

We may also decompose
\matheur{I}_{\Xi}
into

\matheur{I}_{\Xi}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\matheur{I}_{\Xi_{(i)}},\quad\text{ where }\quad\matheur{I}_{\Xi_{(i)}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}\cap\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}m_{\xi}\overline{\infty}_{\xi}.

Set

(A.2.2)

\displaystyle\Xi^{\#}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\Xi_{(1)}^{(1-\ell)}+\cdots+\Xi_{(\ell-1)}^{(-1)})+\Xi_{(0)}

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\matheur{I}^{\#}_{\Xi}\

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\matheur{I}_{\Xi_{(1)}}^{(1-\ell)}+\cdots+\matheur{I}_{\Xi_{(\ell-1)}}^{(-1)})+\matheur{I}_{\Xi_{(0)}}.

Then
\Xi^{\#}
and
\matheur{I}^{\#}_{\Xi}
are divisors on
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
and
\Xi^{\#}-\matheur{I}^{\#}_{\Xi}
has degree zero.
Next, we shall apply Lang’s isogeny theorem on the Jacobian of
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
to introduce shtuka functions for a given CM type
\Xi
and define the CM dual
t
-motives in the case of non-geometric CM fields.

Appendix B
CM dual
t
-motives for non-geometric case

B.1.
Shtuka functions of CM types

We retain the notations of Appendix
A
with a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
whose constant field is
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
.

Lemma B.1.1
.

Let
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
There exists a divisor
W\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})
and a function
h\in\mathbf{K}_{(0)}=\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})
so that

(B.1.2)

\operatorname{div}(h)=W^{(\ell)}-W+\Xi^{\#}-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)}).

We call
h
a shtuka function associated to the divisor
W
for the generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

Proof.

As the divisor
\Xi^{\#}-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}
is of degree zero on the curve
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
,
the proof is the same as the geometric case in Lemma
4.1.1
.
∎

B.2.
Anderson dual
t
-motives with CM types

Let
O{K}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
, and put

O_{\mathbf{K}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O{K}=\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}O_{\mathbf{K},{(i)}},\text{ where }O_{\mathbf{K},{(i)}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\underset{\operatorname{Frob}_{q}^{-i},\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}{\otimes}O{K}\subset\mathbf{K}_{(i)},\ i\in\mathbb{Z}.

For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
n
-th Frobenius twist on
O_{\mathbf{K}}
is given by:

(c\otimes a)^{(n)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=c^{q^{n}}\otimes a,\quad\forall c\in\mathbb{K}\text{ and }a\in O{K}.

Hence for
\alpha_{(i)}\in O_{\mathbf{K},(i)}
one has

\alpha_{(i)}^{(-n)}\in O_{\mathbf{K},(i+n)},\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}.

In particular, for each
\alpha\in O_{\mathbf{K}}
, writing
\alpha=(\alpha_{(0)},\ldots,\alpha_{(\ell-1)})\in O_{\mathbf{K},{(0)}}\times\ldots\times O_{\mathbf{K},{(\ell-1)}}
,
we have the following expression:

\alpha^{(-1)}=(\alpha_{(\ell-1)}^{(-1)},\alpha_{(0)}^{(-1)},\alpha_{(1)}^{(-1)},\ldots,\alpha_{(\ell-2)}^{(-1)}).

Set

\mathbf{U}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{X}-\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)=\Spec(O_{\mathbf{K}})=\coprod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\mathbf{U}_{(i)},

where

\mathbf{U}_{(i)}=\Spec(O_{\mathbf{K},(i)})=\mathbf{X}_{(i)}\cap\mathbf{U}
.
We may identify
\mathbf{K}_{(i)}=\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X}_{(i)})
with the field of fractions of
O_{\mathbf{K},(i)}
for
0\leq i<\ell
.
Let
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
, and take
h\in\mathbf{K}_{(0)}
and
W\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})
satisfying (
B.1.2
).
Define

(B.2.1)

\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\matheur{M}_{(W,h),(i)},

where

\matheur{M}_{(W,h),(0)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W^{(\ell)})\bigr)\quad\subset\mathbf{K}_{(0)},

and

\matheur{M}_{(W,h),(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(i)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}(-W^{(\ell-i)}+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-i)})\bigr)\quad\subset\mathbf{K}_{(i)},\quad 1\leq i<\ell.

The
\sigma
-action on
\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
is given by: for
(m_{(0)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)})\in\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
,

\sigma\cdot(m_{(0)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Bigl(h\cdot m_{(\ell-1)}^{(-1)},m_{(0)}^{(-1)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-2)}^{(-1)}\Bigr).

Then we have

\sigma\cdot(\alpha\cdot m)=\alpha^{(-1)}\cdot(\sigma\cdot m),\quad\forall\alpha\in O_{\mathbf{K}}\quad\text{ and }m\in\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}.

Moreover,

(B.2.2)

\sigma\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W^{(\ell)}-\Xi_{(0)})\bigr)\\
{}\times\prod_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\Gamma\Bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(i)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}{(i)}}\bigl(-W^{(\ell-i)}+(\sum_{j=1}^{i}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-i)})-\Xi_{(i)}\bigr)\Bigr).

In particular, we point out that for
(m_{(0)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)})\in\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
,

\sigma^{\ell}\cdot(m_{(0)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)})=\Bigl(h\cdot m_{(0)}^{(-\ell)},h^{(-1)}\cdot m_{(1)}^{(-\ell)},\ldots,h^{(1-\ell)}\cdot m_{(\ell-1)}^{(-\ell)}\Bigr).

Adapting the proof of Theorem
4.2.2
, we have the following:

Theorem B.2.3
.

The
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module
\matheur{M}=\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
constructed above is a dual
t
-motive over
\mathbb{K}
equipped with an
O{K}
-module structure commuting with the
\sigma
-action.
In particular,

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]\quad\text{ and }\quad\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\sigma]}\matheur{M}=\deg\Xi.

Proof.

As
\matheur{M}
is a projective
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module of rank one, we have that
\matheur{M}
is free over
\mathbb{K}[t]
with

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}O_{\mathbf{K}}=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)].

From the above description of
\sigma\matheur{M}
, we get that

(t-\theta)^{\text{wt}(\Xi)}\cdot\matheur{M}\subset\sigma\matheur{M}.

It suffices to show that
\matheur{M}
is finitely generated as a
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
-module, and so
\matheur{M}
is free over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
with

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\sigma]}\matheur{M}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}(\matheur{M}/\sigma\matheur{M})=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\deg\Xi_{(i)}=\deg\Xi\quad\quad\text{(by Lemma~\ref{L:degE})}.

To prove this, take
\mu\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large so that
(\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#})^{(\ell\mu)}=\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}
.
Put

\mathbf{I}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}+(\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#})^{(-\ell)}+\cdots+(\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#})^{(\ell-\ell\mu)}.

One has that
\mathbf{I}^{(-\ell)}=\mathbf{I}
, and the support of
\mathbf{I}
covers
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}-\mathbf{U}_{(0)}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)\cap\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
.
For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

\displaystyle\matheur{M}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathbf{X}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W^{(\ell)}+n\mathbf{I})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\Gamma\Bigl(\mathbf{X}_{(i)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}\bigl(-W^{(\ell-i)}+(\sum_{j=1}^{i}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-i)})+n\mathbf{I}^{(-i)}\bigr)\Bigr).

Thus there exists
n_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
so that

\matheur{M}=\cup_{n\geq n_{0}}\matheur{M}_{n}
.

Put
\mathbf{\Xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Xi^{\#}+(\Xi^{\#})^{(-\ell)}+\cdots+(\Xi^{\#})^{(\ell-\ell\mu)}
.
Multiplication by
\sigma^{\ell\mu}
on
\matheur{M}_{n}
for each
n>n_{0}
induces a
\mathbb{K}
-vector space isomorphism

\sigma^{\ell\mu}:\frac{\matheur{M}_{n}}{\matheur{M}_{n-1}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{}\frac{\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W^{(\ell)}-\mathbf{\Xi}+(n+1)\mathbf{I})\bigr)}{\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W^{(\ell)}-\mathbf{\Xi}+n\mathbf{I})\bigr)}\\
\times\prod_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\frac{\Gamma\Bigl(\mathbf{X}_{(i)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}\bigl(-W^{(\ell-i)}+(\sum_{j=1}^{i}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-i)})-\mathbf{\Xi}^{(-i)}+(n+1)\mathbf{I}^{(-i)}\bigr)\Bigr)}{\Gamma\Bigl(\mathbf{X}_{(i)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}\bigl(-W^{(\ell-i)}+(\sum_{j=1}^{i}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-i)})-\mathbf{\Xi}^{(-i)}+n\mathbf{I}^{(-i)}\bigr)\Bigr)}.

By the Riemann-Roch theorem (cf.
[
Mum66
, p. 80, Cor. 2]
), the right hand side in the above is isomorphic to
\matheur{M}_{n+1}/\matheur{M}_{n}
(as
\mathbb{K}
-vector spaces) under the natural inclusion map when
n
is sufficiently large.
In other words, there exists a sufficiently large
n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
so that the multiplication by
\sigma^{\ell\mu}
induces a
\mathbb{K}
-vector space isomorphism

\sigma^{\ell\mu}:\frac{\matheur{M}_{n}}{\matheur{M}_{n-1}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\frac{\matheur{M}_{n+1}}{\matheur{M}_{n}}\quad\text{ for all $n\geq n_{1}$}.

This implies that any
\mathbb{K}
-basis of
\matheur{M}_{n_{1}}
generates
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma^{\ell\mu}]
, and so over
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
.
As
\matheur{M}_{n_{1}}
is finite dimensional over
\mathbb{K}
, the result follows.
∎

Definition B.2.4
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
and
\Xi
be a (resp. generalized) CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
A dual
t
-motive (over
\mathbb{K}
) which is isomorphic to
\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
for some shtuka function
h
associated to
W
for
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
is called a

CM dual
t
-motive with the
(
resp. generalized
)
CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

Remark B.2.5
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
.
For each
\xi\in J{K}
, let
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
be the ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
associated with
\xi
.
Given a generalized CM type
\Xi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi
of
\matheur{K}
,
let
\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}}
.
Let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
From the description of
\sigma\matheur{M}
in (
B.2.2
) (when identifying
\matheur{M}\cong\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
for some pair
(W,h)
), we have that

(B.2.6)

\sigma\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\cdot\matheur{M}\subset\matheur{M}.

Similarly we obtain the extension of Proposition
4.2.7
.

Proposition B.2.7
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field.
Given CM dual
t
-motives
\matheur{M}_{i}
with generalized CM types
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{i})
for
i=1,...,n
,
the tensor product

\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\cdots\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{n}

with
\sigma
-action defined by

\sigma(m_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes m_{n})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sigma m_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\sigma m_{n},\quad\forall m_{i}\in\matheur{M}_{i},\ i=1,\ldots,n,

is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{1}+\cdots+\Xi_{n})
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
n=2
and

\matheur{M}_{i}=\matheur{M}_{(W_{i},h_{i})}
, where

h_{i}\in\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})
is a shtuka function associated to
W_{i}\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})
for
(\matheur{K},\Xi_{i})
for
i=1,2
.
Put

W\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=W_{1}+W_{2},\quad h\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=h_{1}h_{2},\quad\text{ and }\quad\Xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Xi_{1}+\Xi_{2}.

One has

\operatorname{div}(h)=W^{(\ell)}-W+\Xi^{\#}-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)}).

Let
\matheur{M}=\matheur{M}_{(W,h)}
.
For
0\leq i\leq\ell-1
,
the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}
-module isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}\bigl(-W_{1}^{(\ell-i)}+(\sum_{j=1}^{i}(\Xi_{1})_{(j)}^{(j-i)})\bigr)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}\bigl(-W_{2}^{(\ell-i)}+(\sum_{j=1}^{i}(\Xi_{2})_{(j)}^{(j-i)})\bigr)\\
\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}\bigl(-W^{(\ell-i)}+(\sum_{j=1}^{i}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-i)})\bigr)

induces the following
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module isomorphism

\textstyle{\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}

\sim

\textstyle{((m_{1})_{(1)},\ldots,(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)})\otimes((m_{2})_{(1)},\ldots,(m_{2})_{(\ell-1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\matheur{M}}
\textstyle{((m_{1})_{(1)}(m_{2})_{(1)},\ldots,(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)}(m_{2})_{(\ell-1)}).}

Moreover, it is straightforward to see that
\Theta
is invariant under the action of
\sigma
.
Hence
\matheur{M}_{1}\otimes_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}_{2}
is isomorphic to the CM dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
, and the proof is complete.
∎

To end this section, we generalize Proposition
4.2.8
to arbitrary CM fields.

Proposition B.2.8
.

Any CM dual
t
-motives with the same generalized CM type must be isogeneous.

Proof.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
with constant field
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
.
Let
\matheur{M}_{(W_{1},h_{1})}
and
\matheur{M}_{(W_{2},h_{2})}
be two CM dual
t
-motives with the same generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
Take an
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
-rational divisor
D_{0}
of the curve
X
(associated with
\matheur{K}
) over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
with degree one (see
[
Weil74
, Cor. VII. 5.5]
).
Viewing
D_{0}
as a divisor of
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
, let

W=W_{2}-W_{1}-(\deg W_{2}-\deg W_{1})\cdot D_{0}.

Then

W^{(\ell)}-W=\operatorname{div}(h_{2}/h_{1})\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)}).

Thus as in the geometric case, there exist an
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
-rational divisor
D_{1}
of
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
with degree
0
and
f\in\mathbb{K}(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})^{\times}=\mathbf{K}_{(0)}^{\times}
such that

W=D_{1}+\operatorname{div}(f)
, i.e.,

(B.2.9)

\hskip 42.67912ptW_{2}-W_{1}+\bigl(-D_{1}-(\deg W_{2}-\deg W_{1})\cdot D_{0}\bigr)=\operatorname{div}(f)\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)}).

Applying the Riemann-Roch Theorem on the curve
X
over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
as in the geometric case, we may assume that
D_{1}
has been chosen so that

D_{3}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-D_{1}-(\deg W_{2}-\deg W_{1})\cdot D_{0}\quad\text{is effective on $\mathbf{U}_{(0)}$.}

Then
W+D_{3}=\operatorname{div}(f)
and

\operatorname{div}(h_{2}/h_{1})=W^{(\ell)}-W=\operatorname{div}(f^{(\ell)}/f)\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)}).

Up to a
\mathbb{K}
-scalar multiple, we may choose
f
so that

h_{2}\cdot f=f^{(\ell)}\cdot h_{1}.

Define
\Theta:\matheur{M}_{(W_{1},h_{1})}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{(W_{2},h_{2})}
as follows:
for
((m_{1})_{(0)},\ldots,(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)})\in\matheur{M}_{(W_{1},h_{1})}
, set

\displaystyle\Theta

\displaystyle\bigl((m_{1})_{(0)},(m_{1})_{(1)},\ldots,(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigl(f^{(\ell)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(0)},f^{(\ell-1)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(1)},\ldots,f^{(1)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)}\bigr).

From the equality (
B.2.9
), the map
\Theta
is an injective
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module homomorphism and the cokernel is of finite dimension over
\mathbb{K}
(by Lemma
4.2.4
).
Moreover,

\displaystyle\Theta{}

\displaystyle\bigl(\sigma\cdot((m_{1})_{(0)},(m_{1})_{(1)},\ldots,(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Theta\bigl(h_{1}\cdot(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)}^{(-1)},(m_{1})_{(0)}^{(-1)},\ldots,(m_{1})_{(\ell-2)}^{(-1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(f^{(\ell)}\cdot h_{1}\cdot(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)}^{(-1)},f^{(\ell-1)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(0)}^{(-1)},\ldots,f^{(1)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(\ell-2)}^{(-1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(h_{2}\cdot(f^{(1)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)})^{(-1)},(f^{(\ell)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(0)})^{(-1)},\ldots,(f^{(2)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(\ell-2)})^{(-1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sigma\cdot\bigl(f^{(\ell)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(0)},\ldots,f^{(1)}\cdot(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sigma\cdot\Theta\bigl((m_{1})_{(0)},(m_{1})_{(1)},\ldots,(m_{1})_{(\ell-1)})\bigr).

Hence
\Theta:\matheur{M}_{(W_{1},h_{1})}\rightarrow\matheur{M}_{(W_{2},h_{2})}
is an isogeny, and the statement holds.
∎

Appendix C
Uniformizability of CM dual
t
-motives

C.1.
Characterization of CM dual
t
-motives

Fix an algebraically closed field
\mathbb{K}
with
k\subset\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
We first characterize when a given dual
t
-motive is “CM”.

Lemma C.1.1
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, and
O{K}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
.
Given a dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
, suppose that
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}(\matheur{M})=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
and that there is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-algebra embedding
O{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]}(\matheur{M})
.
Then
\matheur{M}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O{K}
.

Proof.

Suppose that the constant field of
\matheur{K}
is
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
. Let
e_{0},\ldots,e_{\ell-1}
be the idempotents in

O_{\mathbf{K}}=\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}O_{\mathbf{K},{(i)}},\quad\text{ where }\quad O_{\mathbf{K},{(i)}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{\operatorname{Frob}_{q}^{-i},\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}{\otimes}O{K},

so that
O_{\mathbf{K}}\cdot e_{i}=O_{\mathbf{K},(i)}
for
0\leq i<\ell
.
Put
\matheur{M}_{(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=e_{i}\cdot\matheur{M}
and then we may write

\matheur{M}=\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}\matheur{M}_{(i)}.

The Frobenius twisting permutes
e_{0},\ldots,e_{\ell-1}
by

e_{i}^{(-1)}=e_{i+1},\quad 0\leq i\leq\ell-2,\quad\text{ and }\quad e_{\ell-1}^{(-1)}=e_{0}.

It follows that

\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}_{(i)}\subset\matheur{M}_{(i+1)}\quad\text{ for }0\leq i<\ell-1,\quad\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}_{(\ell-1)}\subset\matheur{M}_{(0)}.

As
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}(\matheur{M}/\sigma\matheur{M})
is finite, we obtain the equalities

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}_{(0)}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}_{(1)}=\cdots=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}_{(\ell-1)},

whence

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}M_{(i)}=\frac{1}{\ell}\cdot\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}(\matheur{M})=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}(t)],\quad 0\leq i<\ell.

Therefore

\matheur{M}_{(i)}
is a projective
O_{\mathbf{K},(i)}
-module of rank one for
0\leq i\leq\ell-1
,
showing that
\matheur{M}
is a projective
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module of rank one.
∎

Remark C.1.2
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a dual
t
-motive satisfing the conditions in the above lemma.
As
\matheur{M}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}
and
(t-\theta)^{N}\cdot\matheur{M}\subseteq\sigma\matheur{M}
when
N
is sufficiently large,
there exists a unique ideal
\mathfrak{I}{M}
of
O{K}
which is of the form

\mathfrak{I}{M}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}},\quad m_{\xi}\geq 0,

where
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
is the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
corresponding to
\xi
for each
\xi\in J{K}
, so that
\sigma\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{I}{M}\cdot\matheur{M}
.
In this case, we set

\Xi{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi\quad\in I{K}.

Proposition C.1.3
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
,
and
O{K}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
.
Given a dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
, suppose the following conditions hold:

(1)

\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
;

(2)

there is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-algebra embedding
O{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]}\matheur{M}
;

(3)

\Xi{M}
is a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
;

(4)

\matheur{M}
is pure (see
[
HaJu20
, Definition 2.4.6 (a)]
).

Then
\matheur{M}
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi{M})
.

Proof.

As in Lemma
C.1.1
, we identify

\matheur{M}=\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\matheur{M}_{(i)},

where
\matheur{M}_{(i)}=e_{i}\cdot\matheur{M}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K},(i)}
for
0\leq i<\ell
.
Write

\Xi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Xi{M}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\Xi_{(i)},\quad\text{ where }\Xi_{(i)}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}\cap\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}m_{\xi}\xi,\quad 0\leq i<\ell.

Let
\mathfrak{I}_{\matheur{M},(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\prod_{\xi\in J{K}\cap\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}})\cdot O_{\mathbf{K},(i)}
for
0\leq i<\ell
.
As
\sigma\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{I}{M}\cdot\matheur{M}
, we have that

(C.1.4)

\sigma\matheur{M}_{(i)}=\mathfrak{I}_{\matheur{M},(i+1)}\cdot\matheur{M}_{(i+1)},\quad\textup{for $0\leq i\leq\ell-2$,}\quad\textup{and $\sigma\matheur{M}_{(\ell-1)}=\mathfrak{I}_{\matheur{M},(0)}\cdot\matheur{M}_{(0)}$.}

Take a suitable divisor
W_{0}
of
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
and
\mathbf{m}\in\matheur{M}_{(0)}
so that

(C.1.5)

\matheur{M}_{(0)}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W_{0}^{(\ell)})\bigr)\cdot\mathbf{m}.

It follows from (
C.1.4
) that for
1\leq i\leq\ell-1
,

\matheur{M}_{(i)}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(i)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(i)}}(-W_{0}^{(\ell-i)}+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-i)})\bigr)\cdot(\sigma^{i}\cdot\mathbf{m}).

In particular,

\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}_{(\ell-1)}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{\ell-1}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-\ell)})\bigr)\cdot(\sigma^{\ell}\cdot\mathbf{m}),

and so

(C.1.6)

\displaystyle\matheur{M}_{(0)}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{I}_{\matheur{M},(0)}^{-1}\cdot\bigl(\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}_{(\ell-1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W_{0}+(\sum_{j=1}^{\ell-1}\Xi_{(j)}^{(j-\ell)})+\Xi_{(0)})\bigr)\cdot(\sigma^{\ell}\cdot\mathbf{m})

\displaystyle=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W_{0}+\Xi^{\#})\bigr)\cdot(\sigma^{\ell}\cdot\mathbf{m}),

where
\Xi^{\#}
is defined in (
A.2.2
).

From the two expressions for
\matheur{M}_{(0)}
in (
C.1.5
) and (
C.1.6
), we have an
h{M}\in\mathbf{K}_{(0)}^{\times}
such that

\sigma^{\ell}\cdot\mathbf{m}=h{M}\cdot\mathbf{m},

and

\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W_{0}+\Xi^{\#})\bigr)\cdot h{M}=\Gamma\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(-W_{0}^{(\ell)})\bigr).

Therefore

\operatorname{div}(h{M})=W_{0}^{(\ell)}-W_{0}+\Xi^{\#}-\matheur{I}^{\#}_{h{M}}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)}),

where
\matheur{I}_{h{M}}^{\#}\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})
has support at the points of
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
lying above
\infty
.
Finally, to apply the conditions (3) and (4), we invoke the following technical lemma (whose proof will then be given):

Lemma C.1.7
.

There exists
W_{\infty}\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})
so that

W_{\infty}^{(\ell)}-W_{\infty}=\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}-\matheur{I}_{h{M}}^{\#}
.

Put
W\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=W_{0}+W_{\infty}
.
We then have

\operatorname{div}(h{M})=W^{(\ell)}-W+\Xi^{\#}-\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)}).

In conclusion, we obtain a dual
t
-motive isomorphism

\matheur{M}_{(W,h{M})}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\matheur{M}
defined by sending

(m_{(0)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)})

to
(m_{(0)}\cdot\mathbf{m},m_{(1)}\cdot\sigma\mathbf{m},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)}\cdot\sigma^{\ell-1}\mathbf{m})

for every
(m_{(0)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)})
in
\matheur{M}_{(W,h{M})}
.
Therefore
\matheur{M}
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.1.7
.

Regarding
X
as a curve over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
, we may view
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
as the base change of
X
from
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
to
\mathbb{K}
, and
\matheur{K}
is a geometric extension over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}(t)
(corresponding to an
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
-morphism from
X
to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
).
Let
\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}:\mathbf{X}_{(0)}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the base change from
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
to
\mathbb{K}
, which coincides with the composition of the inclusion
\mathbf{X}_{(0)}\subset\mathbf{X}
and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}:\mathbf{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{\matheur{K},\infty}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}[1/t]
in
\matheur{K}
.
Since the smoothness of
X
(over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
) is preserved under the base change from
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
to
\mathbb{K}
by
[
Hart77
, Chapter III Proposition 10.1(b)]
,
the integral closure of
\mathbb{K}[1/t]
in
\mathbf{K}_{(0)}

(=\mathbb{K}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}(t)}\matheur{K}=\mathbb{K}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}[1/t]}\mathcal{O}_{\matheur{K},\infty})
can be identified with
\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}\mathcal{O}_{\matheur{K},\infty}=\mathbb{K}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}[1/t]}\mathcal{O}_{\matheur{K},\infty}
.
For each
\overline{\infty}\in\pi^{-1}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\infty)
,
let

\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\overline{\infty}}
be the completion of
\mathbf{K}_{(0)}
(
=\mathbb{K}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}(t)}\matheur{K}
) with respect to
\overline{\infty}
.
We have the following identifications,

\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{(0),\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)}\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\overline{\infty}}=\mathbb{K}(\!(1/t)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}(t)}\mathbf{K}_{(0)}=\mathbb{K}(\!(1/t)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}[1/t]}\mathcal{O}_{\matheur{K},\infty}.

Moreover, put

\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{X},\overline{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{\alpha\in\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\overline{\infty}}\mid\ord_{\overline{\infty}}(\alpha)\geq 0\}\ \text{ and }\ \mathfrak{M}_{\overline{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{\alpha\in\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\overline{\infty}}\mid\ord_{\overline{\infty}}(\alpha)>0\}.

By
[
Liu06
, Exercise 3.17 in §4.3]
) we obtain

(C.1.8)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)},\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{X},\overline{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{K}[\![1/t]\!]\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[1/t]}(\mathbb{K}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}[1/t]}\mathcal{O}_{\matheur{K},\infty})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{K}[\![1/t]\!]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}[1/t]}\mathcal{O}_{\matheur{K},\infty}.

Now, the purity of
\matheur{M}
(from the condition (4) in Proposition
C.1.3
) says that there exists a
\mathbb{K}[\![1/t]\!]
-lattice
L
of full rank in

\mathbb{K}(\!(1/t)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}

and positive integers
u,w

satisfying that

t^{u}L=\sigma^{w}L.

After iterating the action of
t^{u}
on the equation above
\ell
times, we may assume that
\ell
divides
w
.
Since the idempotents
e_{0},\ldots,e_{\ell-1}\in O_{\mathbf{K}}=\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}O_{\mathbf{K},(i)}

acting on the module
\mathbb{K}(\!(1/t)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}
actually commute with
\sigma^{\ell}
,
we get that
L_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=e_{0}\cdot L
is a
\mathbb{K}[\![1/t]\!]
-lattice of full rank in
\matheur{M}_{(0)}(\!(1/t)\!)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}(\!(1/t)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}_{(0)}
satisfying

t^{u}L_{0}=\sigma^{w}L_{0}.

Notice that the action of
\sigma^{\ell}
on
\matheur{M}_{(0)}(\!(1/t)\!)
commutes with multiplication by elements in
\hat{\matheur{K}}_{(0),\infty}
.
After replacing
L_{0}
by
\mathcal{O}_{\matheur{K},\infty}\cdot L_{0}
, we may assume that
L_{0}
is an
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)},\infty}
-module via (
C.1.8
).
Identifying
\matheur{M}_{(0)}(\!(1/t)\!)
as a free
\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{(0),\infty}
-module of rank one generated by
\mathbf{m}
, we
can write
L_{0}=\mathcal{L}_{0}\cdot\mathbf{m}
, where

\mathcal{L}_{0}=\prod_{\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)}\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{\infty}}^{\ell_{\overline{\infty}}}\subset\prod_{\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)}\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\overline{\infty}}\quad\text{ for $\ell_{\overline{\infty}}\in\mathbb{Z}$.}

Then

(t^{u}\mathcal{L}_{0})\cdot\mathbf{m}=t^{u}L_{0}=\sigma^{w}L_{0}=\Bigl(\prod_{i=0}^{(w/\ell)-1}h{M}^{(-\ell i)}\Bigr)\cdot\mathcal{L}_{0}^{(-w)}\cdot\mathbf{m}.

Since
\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
is invariant under the
\ell
-th Frobenius twisting (as the maximal ideals
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{\infty}}
with
\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)
are permuted by the
\ell
-th Frobenius twisting), there exists a positive integer
\ell_{\infty}
so that

\overline{\infty}^{(\ell_{\infty})}=\overline{\infty},\quad\forall\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty).

we may increase
u
and
w
to be multiples of
\ell_{\infty}
, and get
\mathcal{L}_{0}^{(-w)}=\mathcal{L}_{0}
.
Hence

t^{u}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{0}=\Bigl(\prod_{i=0}^{(w/\ell)-1}h{M}^{(-\ell i)}\Bigr)\cdot\mathcal{L}_{0}\quad\quad\subset\prod_{\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)}\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\overline{\infty}}.

The above equality implies that

(C.1.9)

u\cdot\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\infty)=\sum_{i=0}^{(w/\ell)-1}(\matheur{I}_{h{M}}^{\#})^{(-\ell i)},

where
\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{(0)}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\infty)\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{(0)})
is the pull-back of the divisor
\infty
.
In particular, let
X^{+}
be the curve over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
associated with the maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
of
\matheur{K}
, and let
\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)=\{\infty_{1}^{+},\ldots,\infty_{d}^{+}\}
where
d\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
.
Write

\matheur{I}_{h{M}}^{\#}=\sum_{\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)\cap\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}n_{\overline{\infty}}\overline{\infty}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{\infty}\in\mathbf{X}_{(0)}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\overline{\infty})=\infty_{i}^{+}\end{subarray}}n_{\overline{\infty}}\overline{\infty}\right).

As
\deg\matheur{I}_{h{M}}^{\#}=\deg\Xi^{\#}=d\cdot{\rm wt}(\Xi)
, equality (
C.1.9
) assures us that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{\infty}\in\mathbf{X}_{(0)}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\overline{\infty})=\infty_{i}^{+}\end{subarray}}n_{\overline{\infty}}={\rm wt}(\Xi),\quad\forall i=1,\ldots,d.

On the other hand,
we may write

\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}=\sum_{\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)\cap\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}m_{\overline{\infty}}\overline{\infty}\ \text{ with}\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{\infty}\in\mathbf{X}_{(0)}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\overline{\infty})=\infty_{i}^{+}\end{subarray}}m_{\overline{\infty}}={\rm wt}(\Xi),\ \forall i=1,\ldots,d.

For
1\leq i\leq d
, we take a point
\overline{\infty}_{i}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\infty_{i}^{+})\cap\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
.
Since
\infty_{i}^{+}
, as a place of
\matheur{K}^{+}
, is non-split in
\matheur{K}
, every point
\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\infty_{i}^{+})\cap\mathbf{X}_{(0)}
must be equal to the Frobenius twist
(\overline{\infty}_{i})^{(\ell\mu_{\overline{\infty}})}
for some non-negative integer
\mu_{\overline{\infty}}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}-\matheur{I}_{h{M}}^{\#}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\overline{\infty}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\infty)\cap\mathbf{X}_{(0)}}(m_{\overline{\infty}}-n_{\overline{\infty}})\overline{\infty}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{\infty}\in\mathbf{X}_{(0)}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\overline{\infty})=\infty_{i}^{+}\end{subarray}}(m_{\overline{\infty}}-n_{\overline{\infty}})(\overline{\infty}_{i}^{(\ell\mu_{\overline{\infty}})}-\overline{\infty}_{i})\right).

Consequently we can take

W_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{\infty}\in\mathbf{X}_{(0)}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\overline{\infty})=\infty_{i}^{+}\end{subarray}}(m_{\overline{\infty}}-n_{\overline{\infty}})\sum_{j=0}^{\mu_{\overline{\infty}}-1}\overline{\infty}_{i}^{(\ell j)}\right)

and obtain

W_{\infty}^{(\ell)}-W_{\infty}=\matheur{I}_{\Xi}^{\#}-\matheur{I}_{h{M}}^{\#}.\qed

C.2.
Dual
t
-motives arising from Hilbert-Blumenthal
t
-modules

Throughout this section,
we take
\mathbb{K}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Let
\matheur{K}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
be a CM field with maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
.
Put
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]
.
Let
\Xi=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{d}
be a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Notice that

\nu_{\xi_{1}}\big|_{\matheur{K}^{+}},\ldots,\nu_{\xi_{d}}\big|_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
give all the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra embeddings from
\matheur{K}^{+}
into
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
sending
t
to
\theta
.
Set

\nu_{\Xi}:\matheur{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d},\quad\nu_{\Xi}(\lambda)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\nu_{\xi_{1}}(\lambda),\ldots,\nu_{\xi_{d}}(\lambda)),\quad\forall\lambda\in\matheur{K}.

Let
O{K}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
. Then
\nu_{\Xi}(O{K})
becomes a discrete
A
-lattice in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
.

Proposition C.2.1
.

There exists a Hilbert-Blumenthal
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
-module
E_{(\matheur{K},\Xi)}=(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d},\rho^{\Xi})
whose period lattice is
\nu_{\Xi}(O{K})
.

Proof.

This is directly from
[
And86
, Theorem 7 (II)]
.
∎

From
[
And86
, Theorem 7 I]
we can actually extend
\rho^{\Xi}
to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra homomorphism from
O{K}
into
\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}^{d}_{a/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})
so that

\partial\rho^{\Xi}=\nu_{\xi_{1}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\nu_{\xi_{d}}\quad\text{ on $\Lie(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d})$}.

Recall in Definition
2.1.1
that we set

\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}({\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}},{\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}),

together with the
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module structure given by

(c\otimes a)\cdot m\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=c\cdot m\circ\rho{K}(a),\quad\forall c\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty},\ a\in O{K},\ m\in\mathscr{M}(\rho{K}),

where
O_{\mathbf{K}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O_{\matheur{K}}
.
As
E_{\rho^{\Xi}}
is pure and uniformizable (from the definition of a Hilbert-Blumenthal
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
-module), the
t
-motive
\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is pure and uniformizable
with

\displaystyle\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})=[\matheur{K}:\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)]=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}r

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\tau]}\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})=\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\left(\frac{\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})}{\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})}\right)=d

(see
[
And86
, Theorem 4 (III)]
).
In particular,
let

\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathfrak{P}_{i}\subset O_{\mathbf{K}},

where
\mathfrak{P}_{i}
is the maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
generated by
a-\nu_{\xi_{i}}(a)
for
a\in O{K}
(i.e. the maximal ideal corresponding to
\xi_{i}
).
We have the following:

Lemma C.2.2
.

(C.2.3)

\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\cdot\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})\subset\tau\cdot\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi}).

Proof.

It suffices to show that for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{d}\in O_{\matheur{K}}
,

(a_{1}-\nu_{\xi_{1}}(a_{1})\cdots(a_{d}-\nu_{\xi_{d}}(a_{d}))\cdot m\in\tau\cdot\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi}),\quad\forall m\in\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi}).

Now, given any
m\in\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})\cong\Mat_{1\times d}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\tau])
we write

m=\sum_{j=0}^{s}v_{j}\tau^{j}\quad{\rm{for}}\quad v_{o},\ldots,v_{s}\in\Mat_{1\times d}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

Note that for any
a\in O_{\matheur{K}}
and
\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}
, we have

(a-\nu_{\xi}(a))\cdot m\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=m\rho_{a}^{\Xi}-\nu_{\xi}(a)\cdot m=\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}v_{j}\tau^{j}\right)\rho_{a}^{\Xi}-\nu_{\xi}(a)\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}v_{j}\tau^{j}\right)

and hence the constant vector of
(a-\nu_{\xi}(a))\cdot m
is given by

v_{0}\cdot\left(\partial(\rho_{a}^{\Xi})-\nu_{\xi}(a)I_{d}\right).

It follows that for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{d}\in O_{\matheur{K}}
, the constant vector of

(a_{1}-\nu_{\xi_{1}}(a_{1}))\cdots(a_{d}-\nu_{\xi_{d}}(a_{d}))\cdot m

is given by

v_{0}\cdot\left(\partial(\rho_{a_{d}}^{\Xi})-\nu_{\xi_{d}}(a_{d})I_{d}\right)\cdots\left(\partial(\rho_{a_{1}}^{\Xi})-\nu_{\xi_{1}}(a_{1})I_{d}\right),

which is zero because

\partial(\rho_{a}^{\Xi})=\begin{pmatrix}\nu_{\xi_{1}}(a)&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\nu_{\xi_{d}}(a)\end{pmatrix}\quad\forall a\in O_{\matheur{K}}.

That is,
(a_{1}-\nu_{\xi_{1}}(a_{1})\cdots(a_{d}-\nu_{\xi_{d}}(a_{d}))\cdot m\in\tau\cdot\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
as claimed.
∎

In (
2.3.1
) we define the Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motive associated with
E_{(\matheur{K},\Xi)}
by

\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}(\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi}),\Omega_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}),

where
\Omega_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]/\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]dt
is the module of Kähler differentials of
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, together with the following
\sigma
-action

(\sigma\cdot\phi)(\tau m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\phi(\tau^{2}m)^{(-1)},\quad\forall\tau m\in\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi}).

The following result is known from
[
HaJu20
, Remark 2.4.4 (c), Proposition 2.4.9, and Proposition 2.4.17]
:

Proposition C.2.4
.

\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is pure and uniformizable with

\displaystyle\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})=r

and

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})})=\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}(\frac{\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})}{\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})})=d.

We note that the
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module structure on
\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
induces an
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module structure on
\matheur{M}(\rho{K})
as follows:
for
\phi\in\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
and
\alpha\in O_{\mathbf{K}}
,

(\alpha\cdot\phi)(\tau m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\phi(\alpha\tau m),\quad\forall\tau m\in\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi}).

In particular, this induces an
\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
-algebra embedding
O{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t,\sigma]}(\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi}))
, and the following lemma holds:

Lemma C.2.5
.

\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\cdot\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})=\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
C.1.1
, we know that
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}
.
As

\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\left(\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})}{\sigma\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})}\right)=d=\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\left(\frac{O_{\mathbf{K}}}{\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}}\right)=\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\left(\frac{\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})}{\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})}\right),

it suffices to show that

\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\cdot\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})\subseteq\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
.
Given
\alpha\in\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}
and
\phi\in\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
, define
\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}\in\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
by

\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\tau m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\phi(\alpha m)^{(1)},\quad\forall\tau m\in\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi}).

The map
\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}
is well-defined since
\alpha m\in\alpha\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})\subset\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
, and for
a\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]
one has

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}(a\tau m)=\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\tau a^{(-1)}m)

\displaystyle=\phi(\alpha a^{(-1)}m)^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\Bigl(a^{(-1)}\phi(\alpha m)\Bigr)^{(1)}=a\phi(\alpha m)^{(1)}=a\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\tau m).

We shall check that
\alpha\cdot\phi=\sigma\cdot\phi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\in\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
and the result follows.
Indeed, given any
\tau m\in\tau\mathcal{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
, we have

(\sigma\cdot\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha})(\tau m)=\phi^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\tau^{2}m)^{(-1)}=\Bigl(\phi(\alpha\tau m)^{(1)}\Bigr)^{(-1)}=\phi(\alpha\tau m)=(\alpha\cdot\phi)(\tau m),

whence

\alpha\cdot\phi=\sigma\cdot\phi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\subset\sigma\cdot\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi}).

∎

From the characterization in Proposition
C.1.3
, we obtain

Corollary C.2.6
.

\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
is a CM dual
t
-motive with the CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
C.2.4
and Lemma
C.2.5
, the dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}(\rho^{\Xi})
satisfies the conditions (1)–(4) in Proposition
C.1.3
.
Hence the result follows.
∎
